`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/FullCore/DeadlockChargedReturn.lean`:

```text
M = [ 0  3 -1  3
      2  0  1 -3
      2 -2  0 -1
     -1 -2  1  0 ].
```

Its only ordered strict directed triangle in the orientation of Section 20,
up to cyclic rotation, is `(A,B,C)=(0,2,3)`. The six magnitudes and gap are

`(a_A,b_A,a_B,b_B,a_C,b_C)=(1,3,1,2,1,1)` and `Delta=5`.

Proposition 17 therefore forces the unique rates

`(p_A,p_B,p_C)=(5/14,5/9,5/12)`.

The omitted player is `1`, whose singleton-margin triple against these owners
is `(2,1,-3)`. Substitution into `(F)` gives the exact three numerators

`(5/7,5/27,-5/8)`.

Since `q_Aq_Bq_C=1/6`, the corresponding value margins above the solo baseline
are `(6/7,2/9,-3/4)`. Thus the last displayed value gives player `1` only
`s_1-3/4`; the balanced-cycle solo floor fails by exactly `3/4`.

**Proposition 19 (exact diagnostic, ordinary mathematics).** No choice of
three distinct owners in `deadlockMatrix`, in either cyclic starting phase,
satisfies the ambient directed-triangle criterion of Proposition 18.

**Proof.** Direct inspection of the twelve unordered row-pairs shows that the
only strict orientation with three required negative and three required
positive entries uses the set `{0,2,3}` in the order above, up to rotation.
It has positive gap and hence the unique rates just computed, but its sole
outsider violates one floor by `3/4`. Rotation only rotates which displayed
phase contains that same failed floor. Every other owner triple fails the
strict sign premise before rates are considered. ∎

This exact screen is consistent with, but much weaker than, the checked global
no-go `ReducedIdealSingletonLasso.debt_pos`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/FullCore/DeadlockReducedSingletonLassoBarrier.lean`):
every finite reduced ideal-singleton lasso for this matrix has strictly positive
debt at every phase. Proposition 19 identifies the first failure in the
smallest candidate triangle; the checked theorem rules out repairing it by a
longer finite cycle of the same singleton-arc type.

Consequently Proposition 18 does not consume the named hard full-core witness.
The next route cannot merely add more singleton owners or repeat phases. It
must introduce a genuinely non-singleton chronological phase, a punishment
state not represented by singleton clearance, or an infinite nonperiodic orbit.
The exact deficit `3/4` at the omitted player is a concrete amount any such
repair must finance in the first strict triangle.

### 22. One joint phase exactly repairs the concrete deadlock completion

The hard boundary in Section 21 is specific to singleton arcs. The concrete
completion `FullCoreDeadlock.reward` has especially simple nonsingleton rows:
every singleton payoff is `1+M`, and every coalition of cardinality at least
two pays zero. Its four singleton columns are

```text
R_0=(1,3,3,0),   R_1=(4,1,-1,-1),
R_2=(0,2,1,2),   R_3=(4,-2,0,1).
```

Thus the complete raw table used below is

```text
r({j})=R_j  for j=0,1,2,3,
r(S)=(0,0,0,0) for every |S|>=2.
```

Consider a three-phase cycle. At phase `0`, only player `0` mixes; at phase
`2`, only player `2` mixes; at the last phase `J`, players `1` and `3` mix
independently. All unspecified hazards are zero. The exact construction below
uses one algebraic root. It is deliberately given by an interval certificate,
not by floating-point selection.

Define

```text
P(t) = -125 + 335t + 3056t^2 - 908t^3 - 24864t^4
       - 41392t^5 - 19328t^6 + 5056t^7 + 4352t^8,
D(t) = 50 + 10t - 836t^2 - 344t^3 + 144t^4,
N(t) = 30t - 148t^2 + 88t^3 + 112t^4.
```

Exact evaluation gives

```text
P(191/1000) =
  -1374675715873650310293 / 3906250000000000000000 < 0,
P(24/125) = 2923619984558827 / 59604644775390625 > 0.
```

Choose any root `t` of `P` in `I=(191/1000,24/125)` and put `y=N(t)/D(t)`.
The denominator is positive throughout `I`; a coarse exact bound is

`D(t)>50-836(24/125)^2-344(24/125)^3>16`.

Moreover

```text
20N-D = -50+590t-2124t^2+2104t^3+2096t^4,
3D-50N = 150-1470t+4892t^2-5432t^3-5168t^4
```

are positive on `I`. For the first polynomial, its derivative is positive on
`I` by the lower bound `492739863/7812500`, and its value at `191/1000` is
`165890386791/62500000000`. For the second, its derivative is negative on `I`
by the upper bound `-2578173479/7812500`, and its value at `24/125` is
`641729382/244140625`. Hence

`1/20<y<3/50`.

Define the four Quit probabilities

```text
p_1 = y,
p_0 = (t+y)/(1-y),
p_3 = 2t/(1+2t),
q_i = 1-p_i,
q_2 = (1-4y-2t)/[(1-2y-t)(1+y)],
p_2 = 1-q_2.                                      (H)
```

The interval bounds imply

```text
1/4 < p_0 < 27/100,       1/20 < p_1 < 3/50,
2/5 < p_2 < 1/2,          27/100 < p_3 < 28/100.   (B)
```

For `p_2`, use
`47/125<1-4y-2t<209/500` and
`7224/10000<(1-2y-t)(1+y)<75154/100000`; these give
`1/2<q_2<3/5`. Thus every displayed hazard is strictly between zero and one.

The algebraic reason for `P` is transparent. With

```text
F_0(t,y)=(36t-10)y^2+t(18t-43)y+t(5-14t),
F_1(t,y)=4t(1+2t)y^2+(5+19t+2t^2)y+t(2t-3),
A_0=36t-10,  A_1=4t(1+2t),
```

direct expansion gives

```text
A_1 F_0-A_0 F_1 = D(t)y-N(t),
D(t)^2 F_1(t,N(t)/D(t)) = 40t^2 P(t).
```

Since `t>0`, the selected root therefore satisfies `F_0=F_1=0`. In the
hazard variables `(H)`, put `B=(1-2y-t)(1+y)>0`. The exact
cleared-denominator identities are

```text
q_2[4(p_1q_3+q_1p_3)+q_1q_3]-1
  =F_0/[B(1+2t)],
3p_0+q_0(2p_2+q_2q_3)-(1+4t)
  =(1-2y-t)F_1/[(1-y)B(1+2t)].
```

Every displayed denominator and the extra factor `1-2y-t` are positive on
`I`. Thus `F_0=F_1=0` are exactly

```text
q_2[4(p_1q_3+q_1p_3)+q_1q_3]=1,                 (E0)
3p_0+q_0(2p_2+q_2q_3)=1+4t.                     (E1)
```

The definitions of `p_2,p_3` independently give

```text
q_0q_1(2p_2+q_2q_1)=1,                           (E3)
q_3[1+2(p_0q_1-p_1)]=1,  with p_0q_1-p_1=t.      (E2)
```

Now define the value before the joint phase and the two preceding values by

```text
X^J = (1/q_2, q_3, 1, q_1),
X^2 = p_2 R_2 + q_2 X^J,
X^0 = p_0 R_0 + q_0 X^2.
```

The block-certificate value path is the four-row list
`U=(X^0,X^2,X^J,X^0)`. Its final row is the required repeated origin. The
joint phase is the product of the two private Boolean coins of players `1,3`;
no public or correlated randomization is used.

For complete reproducibility, take `m=2` in the checked block-certificate
interface and use the three hazard rows

```text
h^0=(p_0,0,0,0),
h^2=(0,0,p_2,0),
h^J=(0,p_1,0,p_3).
```

**Proposition 20 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed twice; not
checked in Lean).** The three product roots and value rows above form an exact
`IsQuittingBlockCertificate` for `FullCoreDeadlock.reward`. Consequently
`X^0` is a uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted behavioral
deviations. This cycle has one genuinely non-singleton phase and therefore is
not covered by the reduced singleton-lasso no-go.

**Proof.** The phase-value recursions at the first two phases are definitions.
At the joint phase, players `1` and `3` quit independently. Since a double quit
pays zero, the Continue values of inactive players `0,2` are

```text
C_0=4(p_1q_3+q_1p_3)+q_1q_3 X^0_0,
C_2=-p_1q_3+q_1q_3 X^0_2.
```

The first two phases give `X^0_0=1` and `X^0_2=1+2p_0`. Hence `(E0)` says
`C_0=1/q_2=X^J_0`, while `(E2)` says `C_2=1=X^J_2`. For the two active players,
the on-path value equals their Quit endpoint: `X^J_1=q_3` and `X^J_3=q_1`.
Thus all three policy recursions hold.

The four intended indifferences are also exact. Player `0` at its solo phase
has Quit value one and Continue value `X^2_0=q_2X^J_0=1`. Player `2` has Quit
value one and Continue value `X^J_2=1`. Equation `(E3)` gives

`X^0_3=q_0(2p_2+q_2q_1)=1/q_1`.

Therefore at the joint phase player `3` has

```text
Quit = q_1,
Continue = -p_1+q_1X^0_3 = q_1.
```

Equation `(E1)` gives `X^0_1=1+4t`. Since `p_3/q_3=2t`, player `1` has

```text
Quit = q_3,
Continue = -2p_3+q_3X^0_1 = q_3.
```

All remaining one-stage deviations are strictly unprofitable. At the solo
phase of player `0`, a spectator's Quit endpoint is `q_0`, whereas the three
Continue values are

`X^0_1=1+4t`, `X^0_2=1+2p_0`, and `X^0_3=1/q_1`,

all greater than one. At the solo phase of player `2`, a spectator's Quit
endpoint is `q_2<3/5`; the Continue values are `X^2_0=1` and

```text
X^2_1=2p_2+q_2q_3>4/5,
X^2_3=2p_2+q_2q_1>4/5.
```

At the joint phase, an inactive player's Quit endpoint is `q_1q_3<1`, while
the Continue values are `X^J_0=1/q_2>1` and `X^J_2=1`. This exhausts all four
players at all three product roots and proves exact root Nash.

Every displayed value is in the canonical reward box. Indeed `X^J` lies in
`[0,2]^4`; `R_2` and `X^J` lie in `[0,2]^4`, so the same is true of `X^2`;
and `R_0` lies in `[0,3]^4`, so `X^0` lies there as well. The table contains
the payoff `4`, so these bounds are below `quittingRewardBound reward`.
Every phase absorbs with positive probability. Finally every solo payoff is
one, so the nonnegative-solo branch of the block admissibility disjunction
holds for every player. The checked all-behavior consumer
`isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_isQuittingBlockCertificate`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/BlockPeriodicProfile.lean`) yields the
claimed fixed target `X^0`. ∎

In the literal field order of `IsQuittingBlockCertificate`, the proof supplies

```text
hazard_nonneg, hazard_le_one : the bounds (B), plus zero omitted hazards;
box                         : X^J in [0,2]^4, X^2 in [0,2]^4,
                              X^0 in [0,3]^4, and reward bound at least 4;
last                        : U_3=X^0=U_0;
succ                        : the two defining solo recursions and the joint
                              recursions proved from (E0),(E2) and the active
                              endpoint equalities;
gain                        : the four active indifferences and eight strict
                              spectator inequalities listed above;
absorb                      : already p_0>0 makes the first phase absorb;
admissible                  : r({i})_i=1>=0 for every player i.
```

This field audit uses the checked theorem's nonnegative-solo admissibility
branch; it does not assume an off-path Nash property or a player-deleted
contraction.

For orientation only, the selected algebraic root is approximately

```text
(p_0,p_2,p_1,p_3)
  =(0.264153804, 0.471602047, 0.057174860, 0.277327137),
X^0=(1,1.767503947,1.528307607,1.060642062).
```

These decimals are not used in the proof. A narrow search found no existing
deadlock block certificate or uniform-payoff declaration. The result settles
only this zero-multiquitter-payoff completion, not every
`IsFullCoreDeadlockCompletion`; arbitrary nonsingleton rows change the joint
phase equations. Its current value is mechanistic and conjecture-facing: one
positive collision/preemption phase crosses the exact boundary where every
finite reduced singleton lasso is checked to retain positive debt.

### Frozen Proposition 20 export-gate audit

The gate was rerun after both independent reviews and after refreshing the
shared conference files.

1. **Exact statement.** The player type is `Fin 4`; every raw reward row,
   algebraic root quantifier, hazard, value, phase, and fixed target is stated
   exactly in Section 22 and the export packet.
2. **Complete proof.** Root existence, probability bounds, four identities,
   three recursions, twelve endpoint checks, and all remaining certificate
   fields have no deferred mathematical lemma.
3. **Probability and agency.** The joint phase uses two private independent
   Boolean coins; the public phase is deterministic time modulo three. There
   is no public correlation. The checked consumer covers replacement of one
   player's whole behavioral strategy at every sufficiently long horizon.
4. **Adapter and consumer.** `FullCoreDeadlock.reward` is the named checked
   actual table. The new ordinary-mathematics adapter constructs an exact
   `IsQuittingBlockCertificate`; the checked consumer is
   `isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_isQuittingBlockCertificate`.
5. **Boundary tests.** The polynomial has exact opposite endpoint signs; all
   hazards and inactive inequalities have rational strict bounds; the smaller
   singleton triangle misses by `3/4`; and
   `ReducedIdealSingletonLasso.debt_pos` excludes every reduced one-owner
   lasso. The positive joint mass `p_1p_3` explains exact no-go evasion.
6. **Source and novelty.** The maintained frontier says that the checked
   full-core family debt bound produces no uniform payoff. A narrow exact-name
   search found no existing uniform-payoff theorem or block certificate for
   `FullCoreDeadlock.reward`. No paper claim is invoked. The strict named
   change is a uniform payoff for this literal hard completion, not for the
   whole completion family.
7. **Review.** `CODEX_GAUSS` and `CODEX_CEDAR` independently attempted
   falsification and found no objection.
8. **Lean handoff.** The packet gives a narrow theorem shape, exact root and
   finite field obligations, source imports, regression checks, and explicitly
   forbids assuming the desired certificate.

Therefore the packet is accepted for external formalization at mathematical
seal `M` only. The new adapter has no `L` or `A` seal until checked in Lean.

### 23. The certificate is locally open in the raw reward table

This section is a separate, unreviewed generalization. Nothing here changes
the frozen exact certificate of Proposition 20.

Let `R` be the finite-dimensional real vector space of all four-player raw
quitting reward tables. Concretely its sixty Euclidean coordinates are

`r_{S,i}` for `∅ ≠ S ⊆ {0,1,2,3}` and `i in {0,1,2,3}`.

No normalization or quotient is taken in this parameter space. Keep the phase
supports

```text
{0}, {2}, {1,3}
```

and write the four active hazards as `(a,b,c,d)=(p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3)`. The exact
differentiability domain is

`R × (0,1)^4`.

For a table `r'` and a point of this domain, the three on-path recursions have
a unique solution `U(r',a,b,c,d)`: their one-turn survival coefficient is

`lambda=(1-a)(1-c)(1-b)(1-d)<1`.

Explicitly, if `A_0,A_2,A_J` are the three one-stage absorbing payoff vectors
and `s_0=1-a`, `s_2=1-c`, `s_J=(1-b)(1-d)`, then

```text
U^0=(A_0+s_0 A_2+s_0s_2 A_J)/(1-s_0s_2s_J),
U^J=A_J+s_J U^0,
U^2=A_2+s_2 U^J.
```

The denominator is strictly positive everywhere on the stated domain. Thus
`U` is a smooth rational function of all sixty raw rewards and four hazards,
not merely a continuous selection near the base certificate. Let
`G_i(r',a,b,c,d)` be Quit minus Continue at the unique phase where player `i`
is active. The map `G : R × (0,1)^4 -> ℝ^4` is smooth. The exact active
certificate equations are

`G=(G_0,G_1,G_2,G_3)=0`.

To test local nondegeneracy at the literal table, it is convenient to use the
four polynomial closure residuals

```text
H_0=(1-c)(1+3b+3d-7bd)-1,
H_1=3a+(1-a)(2c+(1-c)(1-d))-1-4(a(1-b)-b),
H_2=(1-d)(1+2a(1-b)-2b)-1,
H_3=(1-a)(1-b)(2c+(1-c)(1-b))-1.
```

These are not extra equilibrium assumptions. `H_0` closes player `0` after
imposing its solo indifference, `H_2` does the same for player `2`, and `H_3`
closes player `3` after imposing its joint-phase indifference. For player `1`,
the direct closure residual is

```text
E_1=3a+(1-a)(2c+(1-c)(1-d))-1-2d/(1-d),
```

and the exact relation is `H_1=E_1-2H_2/(1-d)`. Hence `H=0` is precisely the
four active-indifference system for the literal table.

Put

```text
q_0=1-a, q_1=1-b, q_2=1-c, q_3=1-d,
K=1+3b+3d-7bd,
L=1+2a(1-b)-2b,
J=1-b+c(1+b),
J_d=2c+(1-c)(1-d).
```

In row order `(H_0,H_1,H_2,H_3)` and column order `(a,b,c,d)`, the exact
Jacobian is

```text
[ 0,             q_2(3-7d),       -K,              q_2(3-7b) ]
[ 3-J_d-4q_1,    4(a+1),          q_0(1+d),        -q_0q_2    ]
[ 2q_3q_1,      -2q_3(a+1),       0,               -L         ]
[-q_1J,         -q_0(J+q_1q_2),   q_0q_1(1+b),      0          ].       (J)
```

The coarse rational hazard box `(B)` already proves nonsingularity. Exact
interval evaluation of the twenty-four Leibniz terms of `det(J)` on

```text
1/4<a<27/100,  1/20<b<3/50,
2/5<c<1/2,     27/100<d<28/100
```

gives

```text
-167457230734419/6250000000000
  < det(J) <
-143570342643903/12500000000000.
```

In particular `-27<det(J)<-11`, so no approximate or floating-point
nonsingularity claim is being used.

For completeness, this closure Jacobian is nonsingular exactly when the
actual active-gain Jacobian is. Put `kappa=(1-lambda)^(-1)`. Direct solution
of the four scalar affine return maps gives the global identities

```text
G_0=-kappa H_0,
G_2=-kappa H_2,
G_3=-kappa H_3,
G_1=-q_3 kappa (H_1+2H_2/q_3).
```

At a zero of `H`, differentiating kills the terms in which a prefactor
multiplies a residual. The remaining row operation and all displayed factors
are invertible. More precisely, in the same row order,

```text
det(D_(a,b,c,d) G)
  = q_3 kappa^4 det(D_(a,b,c,d) H) ≠ 0.          (DG)
```

Therefore the actual active-gain Jacobian, rather than only an auxiliary
closure Jacobian, is nonsingular at the algebraic base certificate.

**Proposition 21 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed once; not
checked in Lean).** There is an open Euclidean neighborhood `O` of
`FullCoreDeadlock.reward` in `R` such
that every raw reward table `r'` in `O` has an exact three-phase
`IsQuittingBlockCertificate` with phase supports `{0},{2},{1,3}`. Consequently
every `r' in O` has a fixed uniform-equilibrium payoff against all unilateral
behavioral deviations.

**Proof.** The finite-dimensional implicit-function theorem applied on
`R × (0,1)^4` to `G(r',a,b,c,d)=0` and `(DG)` produces an open raw-table
neighborhood and a smooth interior hazard selection `p(r')` near the base
table. The equality constraints are not inferred from continuity:

- the four active endpoint gains are solved **exactly** as `G(r',p(r'))=0`;
- the three `succ` equalities hold **exactly** because `U(r',p(r'))` was
  defined as the unique solution of the on-path recursions; and
- the `last` equality holds **exactly** by appending `U^0` as the fourth row.

All eight inactive endpoint inequalities in Proposition 20 are strict, so
they persist by continuity after shrinking `O`. The four selected hazards
remain strictly inside `(0,1)` and every omitted hazard remains exactly zero,
so the phase supports do not change. The one-turn survival remains below one,
and every diagonal solo payoff remains positive. At the base table the value
box has upper bound three while `quittingRewardBound` is at least four; both
the values and the canonical bound are continuous. Thus the `box`, `absorb`,
and nonnegative-solo `admissible` fields persist as well. The same checked
block-certificate consumer gives the fixed target `U^0(r')`. ∎

This is a genuine raw-table neighborhood, not a theorem for the whole
`IsFullCoreDeadlockCompletion` class. Intersecting `O` with the affine slice
that keeps the singleton rows fixed still gives a relative neighborhood of
zero multiquitter rows, so Proposition 21 does allow small arbitrary
nonsingleton-payoff perturbations. Conversely, an unrestricted nonsingleton
row can destroy the strict spectator inequalities or the active-gain root;
no global extension is claimed.

The full-normal-core feature also persists after shrinking `O`: in the four
rows of the normalized singleton matrix, the strict witnesses at columns
`2,3,1,0` respectively have base values `-1,-3,-2,-1`. The normalized entries
depend continuously on the raw singleton coordinates, so these four witnesses
remain strictly negative. The literal normal-layer recursion therefore keeps
all four players at every layer. Thus the neighborhood is not merely a
perturbation into the already solved normal-core-cardinality-three branch.

A narrow source search found no existing local-openness theorem for a quitting
block certificate. The game-independent checked result
`fullJacobian_isUnit_iff`
(`MathUE/LinearAlgebra/CyclicSchur.lean`) gives the appropriate Schur
elimination for a future formalization of cyclic local equations, but it does
not provide this raw-table adapter, the implicit-function selection, or the
all-behavior consumer application.

### 24. A nonlocal rational polyhedral slice of the full-core family

Proposition 21 is local and uses an implicit theorem. There is also an exact
nonlocal class with rational hazards. Choose an arbitrary solo-baseline vector
`s in R^4` and require

`r({j})_i=s_i+M(i,j)`,

where `M=deadlockMatrix`. Thus the normalized singleton matrix is exactly `M`
for every `s`. Conversely, by
`normalizedSoloMatrix_eq_projectiveLCPMatrix`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/Normalization.lean`), every
`IsFullCoreDeadlockCompletion` has this form with
`s_i=r({i})_i`. Thus this singleton hypothesis is exactly the named family,
not an extra restriction. Require also `r({1,3})=s`.

All other nonsingleton coordinates are arbitrary except for the following
eight weak linear caps:

```text
r({0,1})_1-s_1 <= 15/4,   r({0,2})_2-s_2 <= 2,
r({0,3})_3-s_3 <= 3/4,

r({0,2})_0-s_0 <= 0,      r({1,2})_1-s_1 <= 1,
r({2,3})_3-s_3 <= 1,

21[r({0,1})_0-s_0] + 70[r({0,3})_0-s_0]
  + 5[r({0,1,3})_0-s_0] <= 273,
21[r({1,2})_2-s_2] + 70[r({2,3})_2-s_2]
  + 5[r({1,2,3})_2-s_2] <= 0.                              (C)
```

Use the same supports `{0},{2},{1,3}`, with the exact hazards

```text
(p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3)=(4/21,1/15,7/17,5/26),
(q_0,q_1,q_2,q_3)=(17/21,14/15,10/17,21/26).
```

Define

```text
X^J=s+(7/10,0,0,0),
X^2=s+(0,7/17,0,7/17),
X^0=s+(0,5/7,8/21,1/7),
U=(X^0,X^2,X^J,X^0).
```

**Proposition 22 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed twice).** Every raw reward table
with singleton rows `s_i+M(i,j)`, joint row `r({1,3})=s`, and caps `(C)` has an
exact `IsQuittingBlockCertificate` with the displayed hazards and values.
Hence `X^0` is a fixed uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted
behavioral deviations. In particular this is a class of genuine
`IsFullCoreDeadlockCompletion` tables for every `s in R^4`.

**Proof.** Coordinatewise addition of `s` commutes with every affine on-path
recursion because the event weights sum to one. After subtracting `s`, the
first two recursions are

```text
(0,7/17,0,7/17)
  =(7/17)M(*,2)+(10/17)(7/10,0,0,0),
(0,5/7,8/21,1/7)
  =(4/21)M(*,0)+(17/21)(0,7/17,0,7/17).
```

At the joint phase, the four event masses are

```text
p_1q_3=7/130, q_1p_3=7/39,
p_1p_3=1/78,  q_1q_3=49/65.
```

For player `0`, the excess above `s_0`, with denominator `390`, is

`63+210=273`, giving `273/390=7/10=X^J_0-s_0`.

For player `2`, direct substitution in the excess coordinates gives

```text
-2(7/130)-(7/39)+(49/65)(8/21)=0=X^J_2-s_2.
```

For active player `1`, its conditional Quit and Continue endpoints are

```text
q_3s_1+p_3s_1=s_1,
p_3(s_1-3)+q_3(s_1+5/7)=s_1.
```

For active player `3`, the two endpoints are likewise

```text
q_1s_3+p_1s_3=s_3,
p_1(s_3-2)+q_1(s_3+1/7)=s_3.
```

Thus the joint on-path value is exactly `X^J` and all four active hazards are
complementary. At the solo phases, owner `0` has `Quit=s_0=X^2_0` and owner
`2` has `Quit=s_2=X^J_2`.

It remains only to check the eight zero-hazard players. At phase `0`, a
spectator `i` gets

`q_0s_i+p_0 r({0,i})_i`

by Quitting. Comparison with
`(X^0_1-s_1,X^0_2-s_2,X^0_3-s_3)=(5/7,8/21,1/7)` gives exactly the first
three caps in `(C)`. At phase `2`, comparison of

`q_2s_i+p_2 r({2,i})_i`

with the excess coordinates `(0,7/17,7/17)` gives exactly the next three
caps.

At the joint phase, inactive player `i` gets by Quitting

```text
49s_i/65
  +(7/130) r({1,i})_i
  +(7/39)  r({3,i})_i
  +(1/78)  r({1,3,i})_i.
```

The four event weights sum to one. After subtracting `s_i`, comparison with
`X^J_0-s_0=7/10` and multiplication by `390` is the penultimate cap. For
`i=2`, comparison with `X^J_2-s_2=0` is the last cap. These cases are
exhaustive; every other raw nonsingleton coordinate is absent from both the
on-path law and every one-player endpoint comparison.

All hazards are interior and the first phase absorbs. For every player, some
opponent has positive hazard during the cycle, so the player-deleted one-turn
survival product is strictly below one. Thus the first admissibility branch
holds even when `s_i<0`.

The value path is the absorbing payoff expectation of the displayed product
cycle, hence each coordinate is bounded by the maximum absolute raw reward and
therefore by `quittingRewardBound`. The checked block-certificate consumer
finishes the proof. ∎

The caps are sharp and, under the displayed singleton and `{1,3}`-row
equalities, necessary and sufficient for **this fixed hazard/value block**.
Indeed the four active gains and all three recursions are identities before any
cap is imposed, while the eight remaining endpoint conditions are exactly the
eight caps. If one cap is violated while all other entries are held fixed, the
corresponding displayed spectator has strictly positive Quit-minus-Continue
gain at that phase. This supplies eight exact one-coordinate falsifiers, not
merely sufficient slack estimates. It does not assert necessity for some other
block, stationary profile, or behavioral construction.

This class is nonlocal and unbounded in the solo baselines and in many
raw-table directions: coordinates which never occur in a one-player endpoint
are completely free, and every listed cap permits arbitrarily negative
values. It is nevertheless only a special polyhedral slice of
`IsFullCoreDeadlockCompletion`: the singleton differences are fixed, the
`{1,3}` row must equal the solo baseline, and no claim is made for arbitrary
coalition rows. A narrow exact-name search found the generic block consumer
but no existing rational raw-table adapter with these hazards or caps.

It is also distinct from the frozen literal-table block. The literal
`FullCoreDeadlock.reward` has solo baseline `s=(1,1,1,1)` but
`r({1,3})=0`, while this polyhedron requires `r({1,3})=s`; the two supplied
blocks use different hazards. Thus Proposition 22 is a new raw-data adapter
relative to Proposition 20, not a new claim that the deadlock singleton matrix
has some equilibrium. A concurrently changing external Lean attempt for
Proposition 20 does not formalize this polyhedral theorem.

There is a simple exact witness that this polyhedron is not contained in the
Solan--Vieille unit-solo/capped-joint class. Take `s=(1,1,1,1)`, use the
prescribed singleton rows and `r({1,3})=s`, and set every other nonsingleton
coordinate equal to `1`, except

`r({0,1,2,3})_0=10`.

Every left side in `(C)` is then zero, so Proposition 22 applies. Each own solo
reward is exactly one, but the grand-coalition reward to its member `0` is
`10>1`; hence `QuittingCappedJointExit` and weak solo-exit preference both
fail. The grand-coalition coordinate is absent from the block and every
unilateral endpoint. This proves strict non-subsumption only for that named
capped-joint producer. It does not by itself exclude every other checked
producer or establish a new exhaustive frontier branch.

The second reviewer supplied a stronger exact member of the same polyhedron.
Take `s=(1,1,1,1)`, the forced singleton rows

```text
r0=(1,3,3,0), r1=(4,1,-1,-1),
r2=(0,2,1,2), r3=(4,-2,0,1),
```

and the nonsingleton rows

```text
r01=(-2,4,2,2)    r02=(1,0,-2,5)     r03=(1,-1,-1,-3)
r12=(5,-4,-1,5)   r13=(1,1,1,1)      r23=(-2,4,-3,1)
r012=(0,1,-3,0)   r013=(4,-4,5,-3)   r023=(1,-2,2,3)
r123=(-1,-2,-4,5) r0123=(-3,1,-1,4).
```

In cap order the left sides are

`(3,-3,-4,0,-5,0,-48,-347)`,

strictly or weakly below
`(15/4,2,3/4,0,1,1,273,0)`. Hence Proposition 22 applies. Every coalition has
the following strict destabilizer:

```text
empty:0 joins; 0:1 joins; 1:3 joins; 2:0 joins; 3:1 joins;
01:0 leaves; 02:1 joins; 03:0 leaves; 12:1 leaves; 13:0 joins;
23:0 joins; 012:0 leaves; 013:1 leaves; 023:1 joins;
123:1 leaves; 0123:0 leaves.
```

The current-to-deviation payoff pairs, in that order, are

```text
0->1, 3->4, -1->1, 0->1, -2->1, -2->4, 0->1, 1->4,
-4->2, 1->4, -2->1, 0->5, -4->-1, -2->1, -2->4, -3->-1.
```

Thus no pure sure-exit set exists. Proposed instant owners `0,1,2,3` are
broken respectively by outsiders `1,3,0,1`, with gains
`3->4,-1->1,0->1,-2->1`.

The singleton rows also exclude every `BalancedSingletonCycleCertificate`.
Delete zero-hazard phases, compose adjacent equal owners, subtract the
baseline from the remaining coarse values, and read the compressed cycle in
reverse order. Solo floors give nonnegative clearances; active equality gives
zero incoming owner clearance; and each singleton arc is exactly an
`idealSingletonClearance` update with inactive clipping. Identically zero debt
would then form a `ReducedIdealSingletonLasso`, contradicting the checked
`ReducedIdealSingletonLasso.debt_pos`. This is an ordinary adapter to the
checked no-go, not a new Lean declaration.

This witness is therefore outside the named sure-exit, instant-punishment,
balanced-singleton, and unit-solo/capped-joint producers. It does not prove
failure of every other conditional producer; in particular no global
exclusion of all stationary face-gap or alternative block certificates is
claimed.

### 25. Transverse thickening of the rational polyhedron

The rational block is not a singular exact coincidence. Parameterize its four
active hazards by `(a,b,c,d)` in player order `(0,1,2,3)`, and put
`q_a=1-a`, and similarly for the other coordinates. For an arbitrary raw
reward table define the on-path absorbing contributions

```text
A_0=a r({0}),
A_2=c r({2}),
A_J=b q_d r({1})+q_b d r({3})+bd r({1,3}),
lambda=q_a q_c q_b q_d.
```

On the open domain `lambda<1`, the unique cyclic values are the smooth
rational functions

```text
U^0=(A_0+q_a A_2+q_a q_c A_J)/(1-lambda),
U^J=A_J+q_b q_d U^0,
U^2=A_2+q_c U^J.
```

Let `G=(G_0,G_1,G_2,G_3)` be the four active Quit-minus-Continue endpoint
gains:

```text
G_0=r({0})_0-U^2_0,
G_2=r({2})_2-U^J_2,
G_1=q_d r({1})_1+d r({1,3})_1
      -d r({3})_1-q_d U^0_1,
G_3=q_b r({3})_3+b r({1,3})_3
      -b r({1})_3-q_b U^0_3.
```

At every table in Proposition 22, translation by `s` cancels from these
gains, and the on-path rows after translation are the same five fixed rows.
At

`(a,b,c,d)=(4/21,1/15,7/17,5/26)`

one has `G=0`. Exact differentiation in row order `(G_0,G_1,G_2,G_3)` and
column order `(a,b,c,d)` gives

```text
J = [ 0          -144/85    663/250   -1014/425
     -1701/850      0       -51/50     1014/175
     -294/125      27/10      0          676/375
      4368/2125   117/35    -442/375       0       ],

det J = -44014698/390625 != 0.                         (TJ)
```

This computation is exact rational algebra. No numerical approximation to a
root or determinant is used.

**Proposition 23 (ordinary mathematics, unreviewed).** Let `P_strict` be the
class of Proposition 22 tables for which all eight caps `(C)` are strict.
For every `r in P_strict`, there is a full-dimensional Euclidean neighborhood
`O_r` in the sixty-coordinate raw reward-table space such that every
`r' in O_r` has an exact block certificate with the same phase supports
`{0},{2},{1,3}` and hazards near the rational hazards above. Consequently
every `r' in O_r` has a fixed uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted
behavioral deviations. The union `O_* = union_(r in P_strict) O_r` is an open,
unbounded positive class containing `P_strict`.

**Proof.** The map `(r',a,b,c,d) -> G` is smooth on the open set where the
four hazards are interior and `lambda<1`. By `(TJ)`, the finite-dimensional
implicit-function theorem gives, near every base table `r in P_strict`, a
smooth interior hazard selection solving all four active gains exactly. The
three recursions are exact by the definition of `U`, and the path closes by
appending `U^0`.

At the base point the eight inactive gains are strictly negative because the
caps are strict, so they persist after shrinking `O_r`. The active hazards
remain interior, all omitted hazards stay exactly zero, and the first phase
continues to absorb. Every player still sees a positive opponent hazard, so
the deleted-clock admissibility branch persists without any sign condition on
the perturbed solo rewards. The closed recursion with `lambda<1` is the
eventual absorbing reward expectation, giving the canonical reward box. The
checked block consumer supplies the selected fixed target `U^0(r')`.

Each `O_r` is open in the full raw-table space, not merely relative to the
deadlock singleton slice. Their union is open and contains `P_strict`; it is
unbounded because the latter is nonempty and permits arbitrary solo
translations and free unused reward coordinates. For example, setting every
coordinate appearing on a left side of `(C)` one unit below its baseline makes
all eight caps strict. After an additional shrink, the same strict negative
singleton witnesses keep the normalized normal core equal to all four
players. ∎

Proposition 23 uses the same IFT mechanism as Proposition 21 but has a
different seed set: an unbounded nonlocal rational polyhedron rather than one
literal reward table. It is still a local
thickening around each seed, not a producer for every full-core completion.
No export or Lean claim is made before independent recalculation of `(TJ)` and
the full certificate-persistence audit.

### 26. A global four-parameter joint-row producer

The active equations can in fact be solved explicitly away from the rational
base point. Let `(a,b,c,d) in (0,1)^4`, retain the notation of Section 25, and
put

```text
K=q_a q_c,       h=q_b q_d,       L=1-Kh,
B_i=b q_d M(i,1)+q_b d M(i,3),
T_i=a M(i,0)+q_a c M(i,2)+K B_i.
```

Every denominator below is strictly positive. Define the excess joint row
`g=g(a,b,c,d)` coordinatewise by

```text
g_0= -[c M(0,2)L+q_c(B_0L+hT_0)]/(q_cbd),
g_2= -[B_2L+hT_2]/(bd),
g_1= [d M(1,3)L+q_dT_1]/[d(1-Kq_d)],
g_3= [b M(3,1)L+q_bT_3]/[b(1-Kq_b)].              (GR)
```

For an arbitrary baseline `s`, prescribe singleton rows
`r({j})_i=s_i+M(i,j)` and the joint row
`r({1,3})=s+g(a,b,c,d)`. Define `U^0,U^2,U^J` by the rational recursions in
Section 25. Require only the following eight spectator caps:

```text
q_a s_i+a r({0,i})_i <= U^0_i              for i != 0,
q_c s_i+c r({2,i})_i <= U^2_i              for i != 2,

h s_i+b q_d r({1,i})_i+q_b d r({3,i})_i
  +bd r({1,3,i})_i <= U^J_i                for i in {0,2}.      (CP)
```

Here, for example, `r({0,i})_i` denotes receiver `i`'s payoff at the pair
coalition. Every raw coordinate not displayed in the singleton and joint rows
or `(CP)` is arbitrary.

**Proposition 24 (ordinary mathematics, unreviewed).** For every interior
hazard quadruple, every baseline, and every raw reward table satisfying the
displayed row equalities and `(CP)`, the three-phase profile with supports
`{0},{2},{1,3}` is an exact `IsQuittingBlockCertificate`. Its initial value
`U^0` is therefore a fixed uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted
behavioral deviations. For every hazard quadruple and baseline the class is
nonempty and unbounded.

**Proof.** The joint on-path contribution in excess coordinates is
`B_i+bdg_i`. Solving the closed recursions gives

```text
U^0_i-s_i=(T_i+Kbdg_i)/L,
U^J_i-s_i=[B_iL+bdg_i+hT_i]/L,
U^2_i-s_i=cM(i,2)+q_c(U^J_i-s_i).
```

Player `0`'s active equation is `U^2_0=s_0`, and player `2`'s is
`U^J_2=s_2`; solving these two scalar equations gives `g_0,g_2` in `(GR)`.
At the joint phase, players `1` and `3` require respectively

```text
d g_1=d M(1,3)+q_d(U^0_1-s_1),
b g_3=b M(3,1)+q_b(U^0_3-s_3).
```

Clearing `L` gives the last two formulas in `(GR)`, because their coefficients
are `d(1-Kq_d)` and `b(1-Kq_b)`. Thus all four active endpoint gains vanish
exactly. The three, three, and two remaining zero-hazard endpoint comparisons
are precisely `(CP)`, so they are exhaustive.

All hazards are interior. The absorption, player-deleted admissibility, value
box, path closure, and all-behavior consumer now follow exactly as in
Proposition 22. The class is nonempty because all coordinates on the left of
`(CP)` may be chosen sufficiently negative, independently by receiver and
coalition; unused coordinates and the baseline remain unbounded. ∎

At the rational hazards of Proposition 22, exact substitution gives

```text
K=10/21, h=49/65, L=25/39,
B=(7/10,-7/13,-56/195,-7/65),
T=(0,125/273,200/819,25/273),
g=(0,0,0,0).
```

Thus Proposition 22 is exactly one rational fiber of Proposition 24, and
`(CP)` reduces to its eight caps after clearing positive denominators. The
formulas also pass a nonzero exact test. At

`(a,b,c,d)=(1/3,1/4,2/5,3/7)`

they give

```text
g=(-61/9,-553/243,3,-22/7),
U^0-s=(0,44/81,2/3,-8/21),
U^J-s=(2/3,-79/81,0,-11/14),
U^2-s=(0,-5/27,0,-1/14),
```

and direct substitution gives all four active gains equal to zero. This test
also shows that the construction is not merely the translated `g=0` slice.

The joint-row parameterization is also locally nondegenerate. At that rational
point the derivative of `G` with respect to the four joint-row excesses is
diagonal, in row order `(0,1,2,3)`, with entries

`(-1/85,12/65,-1/50,13/225)`

and determinant `2/796875`. Combining this with `(TJ)` gives

`det(Dg)=-1122374799/25 != 0`.

Hence the explicit map from hazards to the required joint row is a local
diffeomorphism near `g=0`, consistent with Proposition 23's full-dimensional
thickening. No global surjectivity of `(GR)` is claimed. A narrow source search
found the generic block verifier but no named full-core joint-row
parameterization of this form.

The joint-row formulas admit the simpler equivalent form

```text
g_0=[c-3q_c(b q_d+q_b d)]/(q_c b d),
g_2=[2b q_d+q_b d-2a q_b q_d]/(bd),
g_1=[q_d(2a+q_a c)-3d]/[d(1-q_a q_c q_d)],
g_3=[q_b(-a+q_a c)-2b]/[b(1-q_a q_c q_b)].        (GR')
```

These identities follow from `(GR)` by cancellation of the common cyclic
term `Kh`; they expose the exact inverse boundary below.

### 27. Uniqueness of the zero joint-row fiber

**Proposition 25 (ordinary mathematics, unreviewed).** The rational hazards

`(a,b,c,d)=(4/21,1/15,7/17,5/26)`

are the unique point of `(0,1)^4` for which the joint-row excess in
Proposition 24 is zero. Equivalently, for the fixed singleton matrix `M`,
joint row `r({1,3})=s`, and phase supports `{0},{2},{1,3}`, there is exactly
one interior hazard quadruple satisfying all four active endpoint equations.

**Proof.** Put `B=b/q_b` and `D=d/q_d`. From `g_3=g_1=g_2=0` in `(GR')`,
respectively,

```text
B=(-a+q_a c)/2,
D=(2a+q_a c)/3,
2B+D=2a.
```

Substitution gives `4q_a c=7a`, and hence

```text
c=7a/(4q_a),   B=3a/8,   D=5a/4.                 (Z1)
```

The remaining equality `g_0=0` is

```text
c/q_c=3q_b q_d(B+D)
     =3(B+D)/[(1+B)(1+D)].                        (Z2)
```

Using `(Z1)`, equation `(Z2)` clears to

`21a^2+416a-80=0`.

Its discriminant is `424^2`, so the two roots are `4/21` and `-20`. Only
`a=4/21` lies in `(0,1)`. Equations `(Z1)` then give

```text
c=7/17, B=1/14, D=5/21,
b=B/(1+B)=1/15, d=D/(1+D)=5/26.
```

Direct substitution gives `g=0`, proving existence and uniqueness. ∎

The conclusion concerns only this three-phase support pattern. It does not
assert uniqueness among all block certificates, stationary profiles, or
behavioral equilibria for the same reward table.

### 28. Export-gate audit for Proposition 22 only

This audit excludes Propositions 21, 23, 24, and 25.

1. **Exact statement.** Proposition 22 quantifies over one four-player raw
   reward table and one arbitrary baseline `s`, and lists every singleton,
   joint-row, cap, hazard, value, and semantic conclusion.
2. **Complete proof.** The three recursions, four active gains, eight inactive
   gains, probability bounds, box, absorption, deleted-clock admissibility,
   and path closure are explicit. No lemma is deferred.
3. **Probability and deviations.** The joint phase uses two private independent
   Boolean coins; the public state is date modulo three. The named block
   consumer covers replacement of one player's entire behavioral strategy and
   preserves the one fixed target `X^0`.
4. **Adapter and consumer.** The raw actual-data adapter is exactly the
   singleton equality, joint-row equality, and eight finite linear caps. The
   existing checked consumer is
   `isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_isQuittingBlockCertificate`.
5. **Boundary tests.** Each cap has a one-coordinate strict falsifier. The
   stronger exact table above satisfies all caps yet has no sure exit, instant
   owner, or balanced singleton certificate. The strict subfamily is nonempty;
   arbitrary unused coordinates and baselines show nonlocal unboundedness.
6. **Source and novelty.** The integrated full-core family provides only the
   sharper positive debt/gap bound, while the reduced-lasso theorem covers no
   joint phase. Proposition 20 concerns the disjoint literal row
   `r({1,3})=0` at baseline one. The stronger table lies outside the checked
   sure-exit, instant, balanced-singleton, blocker-switch, and Solan--Vieille
   A.1--A.2/capped-joint classes. Its diagonal stationary calculation excludes
   every common-box playerwise face assignment, but not the newer arbitrary
   coordinatewise rectangles in `ConditionalFaceGap.lean`; no such broader
   exclusion is claimed. A narrow exact-name search found no theorem
   subsuming this full rational polyhedron. Solan--Solan Theorem 5.1(1) is the
   non-Q branch and does not provide this standard-Q deadlock adapter.
7. **Independent review.** Cedar Round 6 and Gauss Round 7 independently
   attempted falsification of every certificate field and unrestricted-
   behavior scope. Both verdicts are valid ordinary mathematics with no
   unresolved objection to Proposition 22.
8. **Lean handoff.** The handoff should define the raw polyhedral predicate,
   rational hazard and value rows, prove the certificate by finite cases, and
   invoke the existing consumer. It must not depend on the failed untracked
   Proposition 20 formalization or encode the desired semantic conclusion as
   a structure field.

All eight gates are complete for Proposition 22 at its exact scope. A narrow
packet may be exported with `M` evidence only and with the arbitrary-rectangle
face-gap nonoverlap explicitly left open.

### 29. A sharp global boundary of the joint-row map

The local inverse in Proposition 24 does not extend to global surjectivity.
There is already an exact obstruction on one coordinate axis.

**Proposition 26 (ordinary mathematics, unreviewed).** In the global map
`(GR')`, impose

`g_0=g_2=g_3=0`.

Then the set of possible remaining values is exactly

`{g_1 : g_1>-9/5}`.

Consequently no interior hazards with supports `{0},{2},{1,3}` solve the four
active endpoint equations for joint-row excess `(0,g_1,0,0)` when
`g_1<=-9/5`. This is a boundary of this support pattern, not a no-go for other
block supports or for uniform equilibrium.

**Proof.** Use the positive variables

```text
u=a,  v=q_a c,  k=q_a q_c=1-u-v,
B=b/q_b,  D=d/q_d.
```

Thus `u,v,k,B,D>0` and `u+v+k=1`. In these variables, the four coordinates
of `(GR')` are equivalently

```text
BD g_0=(v/k)(1+B)(1+D)-3(B+D),
g_1=(2u+v-3D)(1+D)/[D(D+u+v)],
BD g_2=2B+D-2u,
g_3=(-u+v-2B)(1+B)/[B(B+u+v)].       (O)
```

The equalities `g_3=g_2=0` give

`v=u+2B` and `D=2(u-B)`.

Put `t=B/u`. Positivity gives `0<t<1`, and `k>0` gives
`0<u<1/[2(1+t)]`. The remaining equality `g_0=0` reduces exactly to

```text
F_t(u):=2t(1-t)(1+2t)u^2
       +(2-t)(7+8t)u-5(1-t)=0.       (F)
```

For every `0<t<1`, `F_t(0)<0`, `F_t(1/[2(1+t)])>0`, and `F_t` is strictly
increasing on the positive half-line. Hence `(F)` has a unique root in the
admissible interval. At that root, `(O)` gives

`g_1=(8t-3)(1+2u(1-t))/[8u(1-t)].    (A)`

If `t>=3/8`, this is nonnegative. Suppose `0<t<3/8`. The inequality
`g_1>-9/5` is equivalent to

`u>R_t:=(15-40t)/[(80t+42)(1-t)].`

The denominator is positive, and direct substitution into `(F)` gives

```text
F_t(R_t)=
 -240t(1-t)(69+122t-16t^2)
 /[(80t+42)^2(1-t)^2] < 0.
```

Since `F_t` is strictly increasing and `F_t(u)=0`, one has `u>R_t`, proving
the strict lower bound.

It remains to prove sharpness and exact coverage. The unique positive root
`u(t)` is continuous. As `t` tends to zero, `(F)` gives
`u(t)->5/14`, and `(A)` gives `g_1(t)->-9/5`. As `t` tends to one,
`u(t)->0` and `(A)` tends to positive infinity. The continuous image of the
connected interval `(0,1)` is an interval; the lower bound and these two
endpoint limits therefore show that it is exactly `(-9/5,infinity)`. For each
such `t`, recover valid interior hazards by

```text
a=u, c=u(1+2t)/(1-u),
b=tu/(1+tu), d=2u(1-t)/(1+2u(1-t)).
```

The admissible bound on `u` makes `0<c<1`, completing both directions. ∎

The rational zero-row point of Proposition 25 lies strictly inside this
axis range. Proposition 26 identifies the first exact place where the same
support pattern fails: the boundary is caused by a phase hazard tending to
zero (`t=B/u->0`), not by a singularity of the value recursion.

### 30. Universal bounded-charge reduction to a positive phantom

The preceding fixed blocks do not by themselves change the arbitrary-game
obligation. The selected punishment-floor predecessor orbit does. Write

```text
V_t = quittingPunishmentFloorForwardValue reward t,
P_t = quittingPunishmentFloorForwardRoot reward t,
alpha_t = quittingRootAbsorptionMass(P_t).
```

The checked declarations
`quittingPunishmentFloorForward_policy`,
`quittingPunishmentFloorForward_isZeroNash`, and
`quittingPunishmentFloor_le_forwardValue` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorForward.lean`
give, respectively,

```text
V_{t+1}=Bellman(V_t,P_t),
P_t is an exact one-stage Nash root against tail V_t,
punishmentValue_i <= V_t(i).
```

The orientation is forward-predecessor, not chronological play. This is why
the finite-packet consumer reverses a returned block.

**Proposition 27 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; universal
residual).**
Let the player set be finite and nonempty. If the quitting game has no
uniform-equilibrium payoff, then there is a payoff vector `b` such that:

1. `V_t` converges coordinatewise to `b`;
2. `sum_t alpha_t<infinity`, and every marginal quit probability in `P_t`
   tends to zero, so `P_t` tends to the all-Continue root;
3. `punishmentValue_i<=b_i` and `r({i})_i<=b_i` for every player `i`; and
4. for at least one player `i`, `0<r({i})_i<=b_i`.

In particular, every counterexample must leave an exact positive
all-Continue phantom boundary on this canonical selected orbit. Ruling out or
restarting precisely this datum would close the conjecture through the
existing charged-packet/path consumer.

**Proof.** Apply the checked alternative
`quittingGame_uniformPayoff_or_punishmentFloorForwardChargeBound` from
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorForward.lean`.
The assumed failure of a uniform payoff rules out its first disjunct, so some
finite `C` bounds every partial sum of the nonnegative sequence `alpha_t`.
Hence `sum_t alpha_t` is finite and `alpha_t->0`.

Put `M=quittingRewardBound reward`. The policy identity and
`abs_quittingRootSuccessorPayoff_sub_tail_le_two_mul_absorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Marked/TimeAdvance.lean`) give

`|V_{t+1}(i)-V_t(i)| <= 2M alpha_t`                    `(TV)`

for every coordinate. Thus every coordinate has finite total variation and
converges to some `b_i`. The canonical reward box is closed, so `b` remains
in it. Passing to the limit in the checked punishment-floor inequality gives
`punishmentValue_i<=b_i`.

For each player, its marginal quit probability is at most the probability
that somebody quits, namely `alpha_t`. Therefore all marginals tend to zero
and `P_t` tends to all-Continue. The one-stage expected-payoff and endpoint-
Nash inequalities are finite polynomials in the tail and the Boolean product
probabilities. Passing to the limit in the exact Nash condition for `(V_t,P_t)`
shows that all-Continue is an exact one-stage Nash root against continuation
`b`. A unilateral pure Quit at that root yields `r({i})_i`, whereas Continue
yields `b_i`; hence `r({i})_i<=b_i`.

Finally, if every own singleton reward were nonpositive, the checked theorem
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_zeroSolo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/ZeroSoloDisjunct.lean`) would give a
uniform payoff, contrary to hypothesis. Thus some `r({i})_i` is positive, and
the preceding inequality makes the same coordinate of `b` positive. ∎

This reduction does **not** manufacture the required return. The exact next
question is: given the limit datum `(b,all-Continue)` above and the literal
source orbit approaching it with finite total charge, must every neighborhood
of `b` contain a positive-charge exact Nash--Bellman packet which returns to
the punishment-floor carrier at a source that can be concatenated? A negative
answer on one exact table, with all possible returns excluded, meets the kill
criterion; the canonical orbit's failure alone does not.

### 31. Equality face or finite attainment

The positive phantom in Proposition 27 cannot remain both strict and merely
asymptotic. This gives a universal split at the exact boundary where an LCP or
tangent producer can act.

**Proposition 28 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; universal
split).** In the
bounded-charge branch of Proposition 27, at least one of the following holds:

1. some player is tight at the phantom,
   `r({i})_i=b_i`; or
2. the selected predecessor orbit reaches the phantom in finite time: there
   is `T` such that `P_t` is all-Continue and `V_t=b` for every `t>=T`.

Thus a nonattained phantom necessarily has a nonempty active singleton face.
The two remaining producer obligations are correspondingly precise: build a
positive-charge local/tangent return from the active face, or return from the
finitely attained strict phantom through its finite exact descent to the
punishment floor.

**Proof.** Proposition 27 gives `r({i})_i<=b_i` for all `i`. If one equality
holds, the first branch holds. Otherwise finiteness of the player set gives a
uniform strict gap

`eta=min_i [b_i-r({i})_i]>0`.

The exact-root theorem
`isQuittingRootSupportApproxNash_zero_of_isZeroNash`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorForward.lean`)
says that every action used by `P_t` is support-optimal with zero error
against `V_t`. By Proposition 27, `(V_t,P_t)` tends to
`(b,all-Continue)`. The endpoint difference for player `i` is continuous in
this finite product datum, and at the limit it is exactly

`QuitValue_i-ContinueValue_i=r({i})_i-b_i<=-eta`.

Hence for all sufficiently large `t`, every player's endpoint difference is
strictly negative. Quit cannot then have positive probability under the
zero-error support condition. Therefore `P_t` is all-Continue for every large
`t`. The policy identity gives `V_{t+1}=V_t` at each such date, so the tail of
`V` is constant. Its limit is `b`, proving finite attainment. ∎

This proposition uses the whole canonical orbit and the exact universal
charge alternative; it is not a classification of one fixed block. It also
shows why a tangent analysis is indispensable only on the equality face. In
the strict branch the obstacle is purely nonlocal: a finite exact path runs
chronologically from `b` down to the punishment-floor anchor, but no checked
theorem supplies the return seam from that anchor to `b`.

### 32. The strict branch forces a paid active seam

Some strict finite descents already close after periodic normalization. The
only obstruction is a positive seam in a coordinate which actually quits.

Let `chi_i=quittingPunishmentValue reward i=V_0(i)`, and in Proposition 28's
strict branch choose the first `T` after which `V_t=b` and `P_t` is
all-Continue. Read the finite word chronologically in reverse:

```text
W_k=V_{T-k}, Q_k=P_{T-1-k}  (0<=k<T),
W_0=b, W_T=chi.
```

Call player `i` **active** if `Q_k(i)` has positive Quit probability for some
phase `k`.

**Proposition 29 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; conditional
universal producer).** If

`b_i=chi_i` for every active player `i`,

then the quitting game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff. Consequently, in
every still-unsolved strict phantom branch there is an active player `i` with

`0<b_i-chi_i`.

**Proof.** The reversed checked edge identities say

`W_k=Bellman(W_{k+1},Q_k)`

and every `Q_k` is an exact local Nash root against `W_{k+1}`. Let `c_k` be
the all-Continue mass of `Q_k` and put `s=product_{k<T} c_k`. There is at least
one active player. Indeed Proposition 27 supplies a player `j` with positive
own singleton reward; in the strict branch the checked bound
`quittingPunishmentValue_le_max_solo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Stationary/MinMax.lean`) gives

`chi_j<=r({j})_j<b_j`,

so `b!=chi`, whereas an all-Continue word would make every `W_k` equal.
Hence `s<1`.

Write `A` for the vector of absorbing rewards collected during one traversal
of the word. The finite policy recursion gives

`b=A+s chi`.

The actual value `D_0` of periodic repetition is therefore

```text
D_0=A+sD_0=(b-s chi)/(1-s),
D_0-b=s(b-chi)/(1-s),
D_0-chi=(b-chi)/(1-s).                 (PR)
```

Write `sigma_k=product_{ell=k}^{T-1} c_ell`, so `sigma_0=s` and
`sigma_T=1`. At phase `k`, the periodic entering value differs from `W_k` by
`sigma_k(D_0-chi)`. The tail read by root `Q_k` is the next-phase value, whose
correction is `sigma_{k+1}(D_0-chi)`. Both corrections are coordinatewise
nonnegative. For an active player `i`, the hypothesis `b_i=chi_i` makes its
coordinate correction identically zero at every phase, so all of its exact
endpoint equalities and inequalities are unchanged. A passive player always
Continues. Raising only that player's continuation coordinate leaves its Quit
payoff unchanged and weakly raises its Continue payoff, so Continue remains
optimal. Therefore the repeated word is support-wise exact Nash at its actual
periodic continuation values.

Equation `(PR)` and `W_k>=chi` show that every periodic continuation is above
the punishment floor. Positive absorption in one phase makes the repeated
word's total absorption charge divergent. Reusing this exact periodic path at
every positive tolerance satisfies all three hypotheses of the checked
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_supportRationalDivergentPaths`,
so a uniform-equilibrium payoff exists. Equivalently, the corrected finite
word is a positive exact punishment-floor admissible cycle and is consumed
directly by
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_positive_admissible_cycle`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PositiveAdmissibleCycle.lean`).

For the contrapositive, `b>=chi` by Proposition 27. If the game is still
unsolved, the displayed hypothesis must fail, giving an active `i` with
`b_i>chi_i`. ∎

Proposition 29 removes all seam-free strict phantoms from the universal hard
branch. What remains is exact and source matched: either a tight singleton
face at a nonattained phantom, or a finite exact descent in which some player
both quits and carries positive phantom debt. The latter is the canonical
paid-row reentry obstruction; varying an unrelated finite support word does
not address it.

### 33. Quantitative active-seam objective

The exact zero-seam condition has a sharp approximate version which ignores
large seams on players who never quit. Continue to use the strict word in
Section 32, and define

```text
E=max { (b_i-chi_i)/(1-s) : i is active }.        (E)
```

The active set is nonempty and `b>=chi`, so `E>=0`.

**Proposition 30 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; quantitative
universal objective).** Periodic repetition of the corrected word is support-wise
`E`-optimal at every actual continuation, is individually rational above
`chi`, and has divergent total absorption. If `E>0`, the checked
support-witness compiler therefore gives a terminal behavioral Nash error at
most

`2E+sqrt(E)(2+7M)`, where `M` is the quitting reward bound.

For an arbitrary game, a family of punishment-floor exact predecessor words
with coordinatewise nonnegative seams and active-seam ratios `E_n->0` is
therefore a universal-payoff producer. Hence any counterexample must keep
`E` bounded away from zero along every such source-matched family; a lower
bound on the full seam is not enough.

**Proof.** In the notation above, the entering-value correction at phase `k`
is `sigma_k(b-chi)/(1-s)`, while the tail read by root `Q_k` is corrected by
`sigma_{k+1}(b-chi)/(1-s)`. It is the latter correction which enters the
endpoint comparison. It is coordinatewise nonnegative and its `i` coordinate
is at most `(b_i-chi_i)/(1-s)`. Endpoint Quit payoff for player `i` is
independent of its own continuation coordinate, whereas endpoint Continue
payoff increases by the opponents' all-Continue mass times that correction.

If `i` Quits with positive probability at a phase, it is active, so its old
zero-error Quit inequality deteriorates by at most `E`. Every Continue
inequality only improves. This is exactly
`IsQuittingRootSupportApproxNash` at error `E`; passive players incur no Quit-
support obligation at all. The corrected values dominate the original
`W_k`, hence dominate `chi`, and the positive phase repeats forever. The
displayed terminal error is now precisely
`exists_isεAsymptoticNash_of_divergentAbsorption_supportRationalPath`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/SupportWitnessPathCompiler.lean`) with
support error `E` and rationality error zero. The `E=0` case is Proposition
29.

For a family `E_n->0`, choose a word below every requested positive support
tolerance and use monotonicity of the support inequalities. This supplies the
paths required by
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_supportRationalDivergentPaths`.
∎

Proposition 30 is the weakest exact selection objective currently visible on
the strict branch: control only the seam coordinates actually paid by Quit
support, normalized by the word's absorbed mass. It is strictly weaker than
an approximate full return `b≈chi` and matches the causal support retained by
the checked all-behavior compiler.

### 34. Signed paid-window dichotomy for the whole canonical orbit

The same argument applies to late windows without a coordinatewise monotone
seam and therefore reaches Proposition 28's nonattained branch. Since
`P_t->all-Continue`, choose `m` late enough that every player's Continue
probability is positive at every subsequent root. For `m<n` with positive
absorption in the window, put

```text
s_mn=product_{t=m}^{n-1}(1-alpha_t),
z_mn=(V_n-V_m)/(1-s_mn),
A_mn={i : P_t(i) has positive Quit mass for some m<=t<n},
E_mn=max_i { |z_mn(i)|,                 if i in A_mn;
             max(0,-z_mn(i)),           if i notin A_mn }.       (PW)
```

The denominator is positive. The asymmetric passive term is deliberate:
raising a passive coordinate helps its only supported action, Continue.

**Proposition 31 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; universal
window dichotomy).** Periodically reversing the exact forward window `[m,n)` gives a
root path which

1. is support-wise `E_mn`-optimal at every actual continuation;
2. stays above `punishmentValue_i-E_mn` for every player and phase; and
3. has divergent total absorption.

For `E_mn>0`, the checked compiler gives terminal Nash error at most

`3E_mn+sqrt(E_mn)(2+7M)`.

Consequently, if one can choose positive-charge late windows with
`E_mn->0`, the arbitrary game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff. Conversely,
every counterexample must have constants `epsilon_0>0` and `m_0` such that
`E_mn>=epsilon_0` for every positive-charge window with `m_0<=m<n`.

**Proof.** Read the window chronologically from `V_n` to `V_m`. Its only seam
is from `V_m` back to `V_n`. Periodic policy evaluation changes an entering
phase value by `sigma_k z_mn`, where `sigma_k` is joint survival from that
phase to the seam. The tail actually read by the phase root is corrected by
the next survival factor `sigma_{k+1}z_mn`. Both factors lie in `[0,1]`. This
is the same affine calculation as `(PR)`, with no sign assumption.

For player `i`, the endpoint difference `Quit-Continue` changes by minus the
opponents' all-Continue mass times this scalar correction. If `i` Quits
somewhere in the word, `(PW)` bounds either sign by `E_mn`. If `i` is passive,
only Continue is supported; a positive correction improves its inequality,
while a negative one costs at most `max(0,-z_mn(i))`. Since every Continue
probability is positive, these are all support obligations. This proves (1).

Every original `V_t` is above the punishment floor. A negative correction in
coordinate `i` has magnitude at most `max(0,-z_mn(i))<=E_mn`, proving (2).
Positive window absorption repeats once per period, proving (3). Applying
`exists_isεAsymptoticNash_of_divergentAbsorption_supportRationalPath` with
support and rationality errors both equal to `E_mn` gives

`2E_mn+E_mn+sqrt(E_mn)(2+7M)`.

If `E_mn=0`, the periodically reversed window already satisfies the exact
support and floor conditions, so use it directly at every positive requested
tolerance. This handles the zero case before calling the compiler, whose
support parameter is required to be strictly positive. If a sequence of
positive such defects tends to zero, the resulting terminal Nash errors tend
to zero and the checked terminal all-errors selection theorem produces a
uniform payoff. Negating the existence of such a sequence gives the final
uniform lower bound after some date. ∎

For the finitely attained strict word, `z=(b-chi)/(1-s)>=0`, so `(PW)` reduces
exactly to Proposition 30. For a nonattained phantom, Proposition 31 says the
remaining issue is not recurrence in the ambient payoff norm: it is a
uniformly positive **support-paid normalized tangent** on every late source
window. This is the precise chronological obstruction the universal thesis
must now eliminate.

A narrow source comparison found the checked full-seam interface
`QuittingFiniteSingleSeamProjectiveLasso`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/SingleSeamProjectiveLasso.lean`). It
bounds the absolute Bellman seam in every coordinate. Proposition 31 does not
claim a new compiler; its mathematical change is the upstream support-aware
adapter `(PW)`, which discards positive seam on a player who never Quits in
the word. No Lean or export seal is claimed.

### 35. Exact comparison with the two current boundary consumers

I inspected the exact declarations
`VanishingDebtAtomChronologicalConsumer` and
`PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/UniformExistenceBoundary.lean`, the
structure `QuittingChronologicalDebtShadowingCertificate` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/ChronologicalDebtShadowing.lean`,
and `QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleEdge` and
`quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`.
The following comparison prevents Proposition 31 from being mistaken for one
of those missing producers.

**Proposition 32 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; exact
interface audit).**
Let a positive-charge finite product-root word be repeated periodically.

1. Its joint survival vanishes on every tail. Its deleted-player survival
   vanishes on every tail for every player exactly when, for every player
   `i`, some other player has positive Quit probability somewhere in the
   period. Equivalently, for a nonempty player set, the period has at least two
   distinct active players.
2. Proposition 31 assumes only positive joint charge, so its periodic path
   need not satisfy the `opponent_survival` field of
   `QuittingChronologicalDebtShadowingCertificate`. It also does not itself
   provide that structure's moving executable candidate data, generated
   secants, every-suffix prescribed-discrepancy and adverse-direct-forcing
   bounds, or small initial candidate debt. Thus Proposition 31 is a direct
   support-path reduction, not an instance of
   `VanishingDebtAtomChronologicalConsumer`.
3. The canonical predecessor edges are oriented from tail `V_t` to current
   `V_{t+1}` in `quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation`. Hence the
   exact window is a relation path from `V_m` to `V_n`; chronological play
   reads it in the reverse order. This is not the exact return from current to
   tail required by `QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn`.
4. If `E_mn=0`, periodic normalization does create an exact positive
   floor-admissible cycle: active coordinates have zero correction and
   passive coordinates have nonnegative correction. Selecting a positive-
   charge edge of that cycle and the remainder of the rotated cycle gives a
   `QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn`. For `E_mn>0`, Proposition 31 gives only
   approximate support inequalities and therefore no exact admissible edge or
   return.

**Proof.** Over one period, deleted-player survival for `i` is the product of
the finitely many opponent-Continue masses. Repetition tends to zero exactly
when that one-period product is strictly below one. Since every factor lies in
`[0,1]`, this happens exactly when one factor is below one, namely when some
opponent of `i` Quits with positive probability in one phase. Requiring this
for every `i` is equivalent to having at least two distinct active players.
Positive joint charge only says that at least one player is active.

For a minimal exact separation, take two players, the identically zero reward
table, and the stationary root in which player `0` Quits with probability
`1/2` while player `1` always Continues. With zero continuation all supported
actions are exact best responses, all floor and seam errors are zero, and
joint survival is `(1/2)^N`. But player `0`'s opponent-Continue mass is `1` at
every phase, so its deleted-player survival is identically one. Thus even an
exact zero-error support path with divergent joint charge need not supply the
chronological certificate on the same roots.

The relation orientation follows literally from `src edge=edge.tail` and
`tgt edge=edge.current`. The forward policy identity supplies the path
`V_m -> V_n`, not its reverse. When `E_mn=0`, the signed defect definition
forces `z_i=0` for every active player and `z_i>=0` for every passive player.
The argument of Proposition 29 then makes every corrected Bellman edge exact
and floor-admissible. The cycle has positive total charge, hence contains a
positive edge; cutting the cycle after that edge supplies the required return
path. For positive defect, the checked conclusion is only approximate and no
such cutting argument applies. ∎

This audit confirms the strategic boundary. The atom interface needs a new
chronological reprojection/shadowing theorem, including every-player-deleted
contraction; the paid-row interface needs an exact reached edge and an exact
return to its literal tail. Independently selected finite stacks or a
counterfactual full-replacement endpoint do not supply either. Proposition 31
instead reaches the existing support-rational divergent-path consumer when
its paid defect vanishes. Its lower-bound branch therefore still requires a
different endpoint theorem, unless the persistent tangent can be upgraded to
one of these exact data sets.

### 36. Gap-compensated paid windows

Proposition 31 overcharges a passive player's negative seam. Near the phantom,
that player may have both a strict Continue-over-Quit gap and strict slack
above the punishment floor. Both buffers can absorb part of the correction
without creating any compiler error.

Continue in the bounded-charge branch and put

```text
chi_i = quittingPunishmentValue reward i,
a_i   = b_i-r({i})_i,
f_i   = b_i-chi_i.
```

Proposition 27 gives `a_i,f_i>=0`. Let `G_t(i)` be player `i`'s exact
Continue-minus-Quit endpoint difference at root `P_t` against tail `V_t`.
Since `(V_t,P_t)->(b,all-Continue)`, one has `G_t(i)->a_i`. Choose a
nonnegative sequence `rho_m->0` such that, for every `t>=m` and player `i`,

```text
|G_t(i)-a_i| <= rho_m,     |V_t(i)-b_i| <= rho_m.       (rho)
```

For a late positive-charge window `[m,n)`, write
`d_mn(i)=max(0,-z_mn(i))` and define

```text
S_mn = max_i { |z_mn(i)|,                         if i in A_mn;
               max(0,d_mn(i)-a_i+rho_m),          if i notin A_mn },

R_mn = max_i { d_mn(i),                           if i in A_mn;
               max(0,d_mn(i)-f_i+rho_m),          if i notin A_mn }.   (GC)
```

Here `S` is the support defect and `R` the floor-rationality defect. The
uncompensated defect `(PW)` is a common upper bound for both, up to `rho_m`.

**Proposition 33 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; sharper
universal window dichotomy).** After a finite date, every player active in the canonical orbit
belongs to the tight phantom set

`T={i : r({i})_i=b_i}`.

For every sufficiently late positive-charge window, periodic reversal is
support-wise `S_mn`-optimal, stays above
`punishmentValue_i-R_mn` at every continuation, and has divergent total
absorption. Whenever `S_mn>0`, the checked terminal error is at most

`2S_mn+R_mn+sqrt(S_mn)(2+7M)`.                    `(GC-error)`

The zero-support-error case is obtained by applying the same compiler at an
arbitrarily small positive support tolerance. Consequently, late windows
with `S_mn->0` and `R_mn->0` produce a uniform-equilibrium payoff. Every
counterexample must instead have `epsilon_0>0` and `m_0` such that

`max(S_mn,R_mn)>=epsilon_0`

for every positive-charge window with `m_0<=m<n`.

**Proof.** Eventual Continue support follows from `P_t->all-Continue`. If
`a_i>0`, then `(rho)` makes `G_t(i)>0` eventually. Continue is then strictly
better than Quit, so exact support optimality forces player `i`'s Quit
probability to be zero. Thus only the tight set `T` can remain active.

Use the entering and root-tail corrections from Proposition 31. Every
correction factor lies in `[0,1]`. If `i` is active in the word, charging
`|z_i|` controls either sign of every supported endpoint comparison, and
charging `d_i` controls its possible loss below the exact floor. If `i` is
passive, Continue is its only supported action and its old exact endpoint
slack is

`G_t(i)>=a_i-rho_m`.

A negative tail correction of magnitude at most `d_i` can therefore create
support loss no larger than
`max(0,d_i-a_i+rho_m)`. A positive correction helps. Likewise the original
continuation satisfies, by `(rho)`,

`V_t(i)>=b_i-rho_m=chi_i+f_i-rho_m`,

so a negative entering correction creates floor loss no larger than
`max(0,d_i-f_i+rho_m)`. These are exactly `(GC)`. Positive window charge
still gives divergent absorption after repetition. Substitution of support
error `S_mn` and rationality error `R_mn` in
`exists_isεAsymptoticNash_of_divergentAbsorption_supportRationalPath`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/SupportWitnessPathCompiler.lean`) gives
`(GC-error)`. If `S_mn=0`, exact support optimality implies every positive
requested support tolerance, so take that tolerance to zero independently of
`R_mn`. The sequence and lower-bound alternatives are exact negations. ∎

Two scalar endpoint tests show the improvement and its boundary. A passive
coordinate with `a_i=f_i=1` and normalized correction `z_i=-1/2` paid an
error `1/2` in Proposition 31 but pays zero asymptotic error in `(GC)`; both
its action and floor buffers absorb the loss. If either `a_i=0` or `f_i=0`,
the same negative correction remains charged in the corresponding account.
Thus the persistent obstruction is now confined to absolute drift on tight
active players, negative drift on a tight passive action/floor coordinate, or
negative drift exceeding a genuine strict buffer. This is a universal
narrowing of the paid tangent, not another fixed-support certificate.

The source overlap is substantial and must be stated narrowly.
`QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness.exists_chargeTangentData_of_windows`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/ChargeTangentPacket.lean`)
already extracts a coherent `QuittingChargeTangentData`—singleton occupation,
boundary, and absorption-normalized endpoint displacement—from arbitrary
escaping positive windows of the optimized exact-D tail. The floor half of
`(GC)` is the finite-window analogue of
`QuittingChargeTangentData.punishmentValue_le_singletonMixture_iff_neg_tangent_le_slack`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/ConditionedSlackThreshold.lean`). Strict
inactive singleton slack is also already used to preserve nearby outsider
Nash inequalities in
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.eventually_exists_positiveChargeExactEdge_of_outsideSolo_lt_boundary`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Analytic/ChargeTangent/TwoOwner/EdgeGateCollapse.lean`).
Proposition 33 does not claim those tangent or slack phenomena as new. Its
specific contribution is the direct canonical punishment-floor-orbit window
adapter with separate quantitative support and rationality accounts feeding
the support-rational path compiler.

### 37. Minimal-floor tangent dispatch

The checked tangent dispatch
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.chargeTangentData_underfunded_or_active_funded`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/ChargeTangentPacket.lean`)
uses the phantom boundary itself as the floor in the complementary singleton-
mixture compiler. Proposition 33 shows that this is stronger than necessary:
a negative tangent can be paid by the gap between the phantom and the minimal
coordinatewise security floor.

For `data : QuittingChargeTangentData reward`, abbreviate

```text
solo_i = r({i})_i,
chi_i  = quittingPunishmentValue reward i,
ell_i  = max(solo_i,chi_i),
a_i    = data.boundary_i-solo_i,
f_i    = data.boundary_i-chi_i.
```

The data fields give `a_i,f_i>=0` and
`data.tangent_i = singletonMixture(data.mass)_i-data.boundary_i`.

**Proposition 34 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; sharper
universal tangent dispatch).** Assume the player set is nonempty. For every charge-tangent datum,
at least one of the following holds:

1. the game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff;
2. the singleton mixture falls below the minimal security floor in some
   coordinate,
   `singletonMixture(data.mass)_i < ell_i`; or
3. some active owner has strictly positive tangent,
   `0<data.mass_i` and `0<data.tangent_i`.

Equivalently, alternative 2 is

`data.tangent_i < -min(a_i,f_i)`.                     `(MF)`

Thus in a counterexample every tail-derived charge tangent has either positive
motion on an occupied owner or a negative coordinate which exceeds the
smaller security buffer, equivalently breaches at least one of the own-solo
and punishment-floor constraints. An arbitrary negative passive tangent is
not a valid obstruction.

**Proof.** Suppose alternatives 2 and 3 both fail. Apply the checked theorem
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_complementarySingletonMixture`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/ThreePlayer/SingletonMixtureCompiler.lean`)
with mass `data.mass` and floor `ell`. Mass nonnegativity and total one are
fields of the datum. Failure of alternative 2 says the singleton mixture
dominates `ell`. By definition, `ell` dominates every own singleton reward
and every behavioral punishment value.

If `data.mass_i>0`, `positive_mass_pins_boundary` gives `a_i=0`. Failure of
alternative 2 and `(MF)` then gives `data.tangent_i>=0`, while failure of
alternative 3 gives `data.tangent_i<=0`. Hence the active tangent is zero.
The tangent identity makes the singleton mixture equal `data.boundary_i`, and
the positive-mass pin makes that boundary coordinate equal `solo_i`. This is
exactly the active-pinning hypothesis of the compiler. All its hypotheses
hold, proving alternative 1. Finally,

```text
ell_i-data.boundary_i
  = max(-a_i,-f_i)
  = -min(a_i,f_i),
```

so the displayed formulations of alternative 2 are equivalent. ∎

This is strictly sharper than the existing sign dispatch at its exact input
type. For a two-player algebraic test, take singleton columns

```text
r({0})=(1,1/2),   r({1})=(0,0),
mass=(1,0),       boundary=(1,1),
tangent=(0,-1/2).
```

The punishment bound `quittingPunishmentValue_le_max_solo` makes the minimal
floor exactly `(1,0)`, which the mixture `r({0})` dominates. The old dispatch
still labels the second coordinate negative; Proposition 34 consumes it
because the full loss is buffered and the only active tangent is zero. If
`r({0})_1` is changed from `1/2` to `-1/2`, the second tangent becomes
`-3/2` and the mixture lies below the own-solo floor `0`; alternative 2 is
genuine. These are tests of the dispatch boundary, not counterexample claims.

The positive-active arm is also nonvacuous. Take two singleton columns
`r({0})=(0,1)` and `r({1})=(1,0)`, zero collision reward, mass
`(1/2,1/2)`, boundary `(0,0)`, and tangent `(1/2,1/2)`. Both active owners
are pinned to their zero own-solo rewards, and
`quittingPunishmentValue_le_max_solo` supplies the boundary punishment
inequalities. Alternative 3 holds for both owners. Again this tests the exact
algebraic arm, not nonexistence.

For the checked optimized exact-D tail, the existing
`exists_chargeTangentData_of_windows` adapter already supplies data of exactly
this type. For the canonical punishment-floor orbit, the analogous compact
window adapter is straightforward ordinary finite-dimensional extraction but
has not been recorded as a Lean declaration here. Proposition 34 identifies
the only conjecture-facing tangent limits still needing a producer:
active-owner motion or minimal-floor underfunding. The first asks for
collision/preemption transport; the second asks for punishment-floor repair.
Buffered passive negative drift is closed by the existing singleton-mixture
consumer and should not remain on the hard frontier.

### 38. Uniformly charged approximate return is enough

The new formalizer question
[`../questions/EXACT_ADMISSIBLE_RETURN.md`](../questions/EXACT_ADMISSIBLE_RETURN.md)
asks for an exact path from the current state of a positive floor-admissible
edge back to its tail. Exact state equality is sufficient for the checked
positive-cycle consumer, but the already checked projective-lasso compiler
shows that it is stronger than the semantics require.

Fix `c` with `0<c<=1`. Call a finite sequence

```text
s_0 --e_0--> s_1 --e_1--> ... --e_(K-1)--> s_K,   K>=1,
```

a `c`-charged admissible near-return at value error `eta` when:

1. every `s_t` is a `QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleState reward`;
2. every `e_t` is an edge of
   `quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation reward`, with tail
   `s_t` and current `s_(t+1)`;
3. some edge has literal absorption charge at least `c`; and
4. writing `v_t` for the payoff coordinate of `s_t`,
   `max_i |v_K(i)-v_0(i)|<=eta`.

Only the payoff coordinates approximately return. The stored simplex/root
coordinates of `s_0` and `s_K` need not be close.

**Proposition 35 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; approximate admissible
return compiler).** Assume the player set is nonempty. If there is one fixed
`c in (0,1]` such that, for every `eta>0`, a `c`-charged admissible near-return
at value error `eta` exists, then the quitting game has a uniform-equilibrium
payoff.

**Proof.** Fix a desired lasso error `delta>0` and choose a near-return at

`eta=delta*c`.

Let `q_t` be edge `e_t`'s absorption charge and let

`A=1-product_(t<K)(1-q_t)`

be the whole-block absorption probability. Every `q_t` belongs to `[0,1]`.
If `q_j>=c`, then all other survival factors are at most one, so

`A >= q_j >= c`.                                      `(UR1)`

Read the exact forward relation path in chronological reverse order, exactly
as in `quittingReversedForwardCycle`. The edge identities give every
nonclosing cyclic Bellman equation exactly. At the closing phase the residual
in coordinate `i` is

```text
continueMass(root_0) * (v_0(i)-v_K(i)),
```

so its absolute value is at most `eta=delta*c`, hence at most `delta*A` by
`(UR1)`.
