To match the exact checked constructor rather than hiding its support
account, set

```text
supportError = delta*(1-c),
seamError    = delta*c.
```

Both are nonnegative and their sum is `delta`. Every edge is exact Nash, so
its root is support-optimal at error zero and therefore at `supportError`.
The endpoint distance is at most `seamError`, and

```text
seamError = delta*c <= delta*A
          = (supportError+seamError)*A.
```

Every state dominates the behavioral punishment floor, so the rationality
inequality required by the lasso holds even at error zero. The edge with
`q_j>=c>0` supplies the absorbing phase. Thus
`quittingFiniteSingleSeamProjectiveLasso_of_reversedForwardBlock`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/ForwardBlockSingleSeam.lean`)
constructs a `QuittingFiniteSingleSeamProjectiveLasso reward K delta`.
Doing this for every positive `delta` and applying
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_singleSeamProjectiveLassos`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/SingleSeamProjectiveLasso.lean`)
gives a uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted behavioral
deviations. ∎

**Corollary 35A (ordinary mathematics; fixed-edge payoff-closure
criterion).** Suppose there is one floor-admissible exact edge `e` with
positive charge `q`. Suppose moreover that, for every `eta>0`, there is an
exact path in `quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation reward` from
`e.current` to a state whose payoff coordinate is within `eta` (in sup norm)
of the payoff coordinate of `e.tail`. Then the quitting game has a
uniform-equilibrium payoff.

Indeed, prepend `e` to the requested return path. The resulting path starts
at `e.tail`, contains an edge of charge `q`, and ends `eta`-close in payoff to
`e.tail`. Proposition 35 applies with the fixed constant `c=q`; literal
absorption gives `0<q<=1`. Notice that the return path is allowed to end with
an arbitrary stored simplex coordinate. Thus the exact target needed from the
floor-clipped branch is now a closure statement in payoff space:

```text
payoff(e.tail) belongs to closure(payoffs reachable from e.current).
```

It is not an exact return in the full Nash--Bellman state space. Conversely,
compactness of the cofinal family of clipped reset edges by itself supplies
no such closure statement. The tails and roots lie in compact boxes—using
`abs_quittingTailConditionedValue_le`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/PhantomBoundaryConditioning.lean`),
`abs_quittingPunishmentValue_le_quittingRewardBound`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Stationary/MinMax.lean`), and simplex
compactness—and exact complementarity is closed by
`isClosed_isεQuittingRootEndpointNash_simplex`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/ComplementarityClosed.lean`).
Hence one may extract a limiting positive floor-admissible edge. But neither
closedness nor the edge equation creates a path from its current endpoint
back toward its tail. This is precisely the missing reachability datum, not a
failure of compactness.

The quantifiers matter. One fixed positive edge followed by exact returns is
the special case with endpoint error zero and `c` no larger than that edge's
charge. More generally, the return paths may depend arbitrarily on `eta`, may
have unbounded length, and may use different supports. What must not vanish is
the block absorption floor. If the available charge floor is `c_eta->0`, the
same calculation only bounds the normalized seam by `eta/c_eta`; ordinary
endpoint recurrence alone is therefore insufficient.

This gives a strictly weaker conjecture-facing target than the literal exact
return requested in `questions/EXACT_ADMISSIBLE_RETURN.md`: construct
source-matched admissible paths from a paid current state whose terminal
**value** approaches the paid tail, while retaining one uniformly positive
edge. It does not yet show that the floor-clipped edges from Section 37 admit
such paths. Those edges already have a uniform positive charge; the remaining
question is now payoff recurrence rather than exact state recurrence.

The weakening is strict even before taking a limit. In the one-player game
with `r({0})_0=1`, the behavioral punishment value is one. At tail value one,
Quit and Continue both pay one, so every marginal root is exact Nash and its
successor value is again one. Give the tail state the stored all-Continue
simplex root and the current state the stored sure-Quit root. They are distinct
admissible states joined by an absorption-one exact edge, but their payoff
coordinates agree exactly. This is a zero-error `c=1` near-return and hence a
one-phase lasso, although the edge is not a closed path in the full state type.
Thus recurrence of the semantically inert tail root coordinate is not
necessary.

The source audit is narrow. `PositiveAdmissibleCycle.lean` consumes an exact
closed path. `ForwardBlockSingleSeam.lean` proves the exact reversed-block
seam calculation, and `SingleSeamProjectiveLasso.lean` compiles lassos at all
errors. `FiniteForwardProjectiveLasso.lean` obtains a close charged pair from
arbitrarily large charge inside one compact packet. A symbol/phrase search for
`approximate return`, `close admissible`, and `positive approximate cycle`
found no declaration packaging the fixed-positive-edge, value-only recurrence
criterion above. Proposition 35 is a direct adapter to existing checked
machinery, not a new semantic compiler.

### 39. The paid-row frontier needs only payoff-closure return

The weakening above applies directly to the named paid-row boundary. Define
an **approximate paid-row value-return consumer** by copying every input
quantifier of `PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer`:

```text
for every frontier, positive-support mover, full-replacement endpoint,
strict debt separation, observer != mover, gain>0, and eventually available
paid first-disagreement row,
```

but replace its output `Nonempty QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn reward` by
the following data:

1. one `QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleEdge reward` named `edge`;
2. `0 < edge.toBoxEdge.absorptionCharge`; and
3. for every `eta>0`, a floor-admissible relation path from `edge.current` to
   some state `z_eta` such that

```text
max_i |payoff(z_eta)_i-payoff(edge.tail)_i| <= eta.       (APR)
```

The edge and its positive charge are fixed after the paid-row data are fixed;
only the return path and endpoint may depend on `eta`.

**Proposition 36 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; approximate paid-row
capstone).** In
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_finiteSupportRankExitConsumers`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/UniformExistenceBoundary.lean`),
the hypothesis `PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer reward` may be
replaced by the approximate paid-row value-return consumer above. The other
three chronological-exit hypotheses are unchanged.

**Proof.** Repeat the checked finite-support-rank proof verbatim through its
four-way alternative. The positive-slope, support-entry, and circulation
branches still use their supplied
`QuittingChronologicalDebtShadowingCertificate`s. In the paid-row branch,
the copied source quantifiers supply `edge` and `(APR)`. Corollary 35A applied
to those data gives a uniform-equilibrium payoff, contradicting the assumed
positive minimum terminal debt exactly as the existing exact-return branch
does. The target selected by the single-seam lasso consumer is fixed by the
checked terminal all-errors selection; the varying paths do not vary that
final target after selection. ∎

This strictly weakens the current open arrow at its output interface. The
named checked consumer asks for an exact return to `edge.tail`, including its
stored all-Continue root. Proposition 36 asks only that the **payoff** of that
tail lie in the closure of payoffs reachable from `edge.current`. The
one-player example in Section 38 shows the difference is real even at zero
payoff error. No producer for `(APR)` is asserted: the paid first-disagreement
row remains counterfactual until an actual floor-admissible edge is supplied,
and compactness alone does not give reachability from its current endpoint.

A narrow comparison used only
`QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn`,
`PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer`, and
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_finiteSupportRankExitConsumers`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/UniformExistenceBoundary.lean`).
The repository's current boundary therefore remains valid as a sufficient
exact interface, but it is not minimal for the unrestricted behavioral
conclusion.

### 40. Aggregate charged near-returns

Proposition 35 still packages its uniform absorption through one edge. The
single-seam calculation uses only the absorption of the **whole block**.
This matters in the diffuse branch, where every individual edge may be small
while a finite packet has macroscopic cumulative charge.

For a finite admissible path with edge absorption charges `q_0,...,q_(K-1)`,
write

```text
A(path)=1-product_(t<K)(1-q_t),
C(path)=sum_(t<K)q_t.
```

**Proposition 37 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; aggregate charged
near-return compiler).** Assume the player set is nonempty. Each of the
following hypotheses implies that the quitting game has a
uniform-equilibrium payoff.

1. There is one fixed `c>0` such that, for every `eta>0`, some nonempty finite
   floor-admissible exact relation path has endpoint payoff distance at most
   `eta` and `A(path)>=c`.
2. There is one fixed `C>0` such that, for every `eta>0`, some such path has
   endpoint payoff distance at most `eta` and `C(path)>=C`.

The paths, their lengths, roots, supports, and individual edge charges may all
depend on `eta`. No individual edge needs a uniform positive lower bound.

**Proof.** For part 1, replace `c` by `min(c,1)` if necessary, so
`0<c<=1`. Given desired lasso error `delta>0`, choose a path with endpoint
error `eta=delta*c`. Reverse it and use

```text
supportError=delta*(1-c),
seamError=delta*c.
```

Exactly as in Proposition 35, the closing seam is at most `delta*c`, while
the required weighted allowance is `delta*A(path)>=delta*c`. Exact Nash and
the punishment floors supply the support and rationality fields. Since
`A(path)>0`, at least one edge has positive absorption and supplies the
constructor's absorbing phase. The checked single-seam lasso consumer then
gives the unrestricted-behavior uniform payoff.

For part 2, every `q_t` lies in `[0,1]` and the elementary induction

```text
product_(t<K)(1-q_t) * (1+sum_(t<K)q_t) <= 1            (CS)
```

holds. The induction step is

```text
(1-q)(1+S+q) <= 1+S,
```

because the difference from the right side is `-q*S-q^2<=0`. Therefore

```text
A(path) >= C(path)/(1+C(path)) >= C/(1+C).
```

Part 1 applies with the fixed positive constant `c=C/(1+C)`. ∎

This is the natural value-recurrence condition on the charged relation:
macroscopic **path charge** must occur between two arbitrarily close payoff
coordinates. A fixed positive edge plus payoff-closure return (Corollary 35A)
is one special case. Another is a diffuse finite packet whose tiny edge
charges sum to a fixed amount before its payoff nearly returns. Merely having
large charge somewhere and recurrence somewhere else is insufficient; both
properties must hold on the same source-matched path.

The source comparison remains narrow. The inequality `(CS)` is elementary
probability accounting. `FiniteForwardProjectiveLasso.lean` already extracts
close charged pairs from arbitrarily large charge inside one compact forward
packet. Proposition 37 does not claim that compactness result as new. Its
change is the direct relation-level endpoint accepted by the paid/floor-clip
boundary: a uniformly charged family of approximate **payoff** loops, without
one fixed edge and without recurrence of stored root coordinates.

### 41. Restartable charged classes remove return altogether

There is a second weakening, logically independent of payoff recurrence. An
exact return is one way to repeat positive charge. It is enough that a class
of admissible states can always launch another charged block and land back
somewhere in the same class.

For `C>0`, call a set `R` of floor-admissible states **`C`-restartable** if

1. `R` is nonempty; and
2. for every `s in R`, there are `t in R` and a finite exact admissible path
   from `s` to `t` whose charged-relation `chargeSum` is at least `C`.

Neither the path length nor its endpoint is fixed. The set need not be finite,
closed, compact, or payoff recurrent.

**Proposition 38 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; restartable-class
producer).** If the punishment-floor admissible relation has a
`C`-restartable class for some `C>0`, then the quitting game has a
uniform-equilibrium payoff.

**Proof.** Choose `s_0 in R`. Inductively, after choosing `s_n in R`, choose
one certified path `p_n` from `s_n` to `s_(n+1) in R`. For every `N`, append
the first `N` paths. Additivity of `ChargedRelation.Path.chargeSum` gives

```text
chargeSum(p_0 ++ ... ++ p_(N-1)) >= N*C.               (RC)
```

Every appended path is still exact and floor admissible. Applying
`QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.pathToFinitePrefix`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`)
preserves its charge exactly and produces a
`QuittingPunishmentFloorFinitePrefix reward`. Given any nonnegative charge
target `B`, Archimedean growth and `C>0` choose `N` with `B<=N*C`; `(RC)`
then supplies a finite prefix of charge at least `B`. The checked theorem
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_arbitrarilyCharged_floorPrefixes`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorFinitePrefix.lean`)
gives the uniform-equilibrium payoff. ∎

This replaces recurrence by **restartability**. A positive admissible return
is the special case in which one macro-block (the positive edge followed by
its return) restarts from a singleton set `R={edge.tail}`. A finite
restartable set also contains a repeated state after finitely many selected
macro-steps, hence yields an exact positive cycle. The new logical room is an
infinite moving class: the charged blocks may visit distinct states forever
without any full-state or payoff return.

The zero boundary is sharp for this argument. If `C=0`, `(RC)` does not make
prefix charge diverge; a nonempty class of all-Continue self-edges would
satisfy the zero-cost version in every game. Nonemptiness is also necessary
to choose `s_0`. Positive block charge makes each chosen macro-path nonempty,
but no individual edge needs a fixed lower bound.

This is a direct game-semantic adapter of the generic checked notion
`HasRestartableExtension` and its compatible-prefix theorem
`exists_compatible_edgeBudgetedFinitePrefixes_of_restartableExtension`
(`MathUE/Topology/CompactEdgeBudgetedPrefixRelation.lean`). That generic
result works with one binary relation edge and a uniform edge cost.
Proposition 38 treats an exact admissible **path** as the macro-edge, retains
its concrete Nash--Bellman payload by concatenation, and feeds the existing
arbitrarily-charged floor-prefix consumer. It is not a producer of the
restartable class.

Consequently the paid-row four-exit boundary can be weakened in another
direction: after fixing the paid-row source tuple, it is enough to produce
some positive `C` and a `C`-restartable floor-admissible class. The paid branch
then closes by Proposition 38; the other three chronological branches are
unchanged. This hypothesis does not follow from a counterfactual row, and no
such source adapter is claimed here.

### 42. The exact floor-clip viability target

Restartability becomes concrete on a uniform singleton-deficit carrier. Fix
`eta>0` and define

```text
D_eta = {floor-admissible states s |
           there exists i,
             payoff(s)_i <= r({i})_i-eta}.
```

The deficit owner may depend on the state. This permits blocker/owner
switching and is weaker than preserving the one outsider selected by the
cofinal floor-clip theorem.

**Proposition 39 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; singleton-deficit
viability producer).** Assume the exact punishment-floor predecessor relation
has a nonempty viable subset `R subset D_eta`: for every `s in R`, there are
`t in R` and an exact admissible edge from `s` to `t`. Then the quitting game
has a uniform-equilibrium payoff.

**Proof.** Put `M=quittingRewardBound reward` and

```text
c=eta/(eta+2M).
```

The reward bound is nonnegative, so `c>0`. For `s in R`, choose a deficit
owner `i` and an exact edge from `s` to `t in R`. Its root is exact
endpoint Nash against `payoff(s)`. The checked estimate
`gap_div_le_quittingRootAbsorptionMass_of_isZeroEndpointNash`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/FixedTailUniformAbsorption.lean`)
applied with the same `eta`, owner `i`, and the canonical reward bound gives

```text
c <= absorptionCharge(s -> t).
```

Thus `R` is a `c`-restartable class using one-edge blocks. Proposition 38
gives a uniform-equilibrium payoff. ∎

The strict gap is essential. At `eta=0`, every all-Continue self-edge over a
tail dominating its singleton rows preserves the weak carrier but has zero
charge, so restartability does not follow. For positive `eta`, no continuous
root selection, compactness, or fixed owner is required: existential
one-step viability and the local gap estimate already give the uniform charge
needed for indefinite iteration.

This identifies the smallest exact forward-invariance question exposed by
the floor-clipped reset family. The theorem
`exists_fixedOutsider_punishment_ge_singleton_or_cofinal_floorClippedReset`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Chronology/Conditioned/Diffuse/FloorClip.lean`)
produces cofinally many isolated tail states in one fixed `D_eta` and positive
edges leaving them. It does **not** prove that the successor of any such edge
lies in `D_eta`, even with a different deficit owner. Hence compactifying the
isolated edges still stops exactly before Proposition 39's viability
hypothesis. A positive proof may switch the deficit label after every edge; a
negative obstruction must show that every exact floor-admissible predecessor
of some reachable deficit state leaves the entire union `D_eta`.

### 43. Arbitrary finite deficit depth is already enough

Proposition 39 asks for one compatible infinite selection only because it
passes through the restartable-class interface. The checked arbitrarily
charged-prefix consumer needs less: independently selected finite paths of
unbounded depth already suffice.

**Proposition 40 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed; finite-depth
singleton-deficit producer).** Fix `eta>0`. Suppose that for every natural
number `N` there is an exact punishment-floor-admissible path

```text
s_0 -> s_1 -> ... -> s_N
```

such that `s_t in D_eta` for every `t<N`. The final state `s_N` need not lie in
`D_eta`; paths for different `N` need not have the same source, be nested, or
admit any compatible infinite selection. Then the quitting game has a
uniform-equilibrium payoff.

**Proof.** Put `M=quittingRewardBound reward` and

```text
c=eta/(eta+2M)>0.
```

For every `t<N`, choose a possibly state-dependent deficit owner witnessing
`s_t in D_eta`. The exact edge with tail `s_t` has an exact endpoint-Nash root,
so `gap_div_le_quittingRootAbsorptionMass_of_isZeroEndpointNash`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/FixedTailUniformAbsorption.lean`)
gives edge charge at least `c`. Hence the length-`N` path has charged-relation
path sum at least `N*c`. The checked constructor
`QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.pathToFinitePrefix`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`)
turns it into an exact floor prefix of at least that charge. Given any target
charge `B`, choose `N` with `B<=N*c`; then
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_arbitrarilyCharged_floorPrefixes`
(same file) supplies a uniform-equilibrium payoff. ∎

Equivalently, every counterexample has **finite deficit extinction depth** at
every `eta>0`: for each strict gap there is some `N` for which no such
length-`N` path exists. This is a universal obstruction, not a compactness or
selection issue. One may define the descending finite viability kernels by

```text
K_0 = all floor-admissible states,
K_(n+1) = {s in D_eta | some exact admissible edge from s has successor in K_n}.
```

Proposition 40 says that a counterexample must have `K_N=empty` at some finite
stage. Closedness/compactness could otherwise recover a compatible infinite
path, but that recovery is unnecessary for the consumer. The checked
floor-clip theorem presently supplies only a nonempty first layer and positive
edges leaving it; it does not prevent finite extinction at any later layer.
Thus the smallest remaining floor-clip producer obligation is arbitrary
finite-depth deficit persistence, not a continuous root selection or a single
forward-invariant carrier.

### 44. A counterexample has a uniform deficit-visit budget on every orbit

The preceding producer has a sharper counterexample-side dual. It applies to
all exact floor-admissible paths, including paths which repeatedly leave and
re-enter the deficit carrier.

**Proposition 41 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed;
selection-independent
deficit extinction).** Let `witness` be a
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness reward`, put

```text
B = quittingPunishmentFloorPrefixChargeBound reward,
c_eta = eta/(eta+2M),       M=quittingRewardBound reward,
```

and fix `eta>0`. For every finite exact floor-admissible path

```text
s_0 -> s_1 -> ... -> s_N,
```

if `J={t<N | s_t in D_eta}`, then

```text
c_eta * card(J) <= path.chargeSum <= B.              (DV)
```

Consequently every infinite coherent exact floor-admissible path visits
`D_eta` only finitely many times, with a bound independent of the path. In
particular, along every such path and for every player `i`,

```text
max(0,r({i})_i-payoff(s_t)_i) -> 0.
```

Equivalently, every exact predecessor selection approaches the closed
singleton-dominating region in the one-sided coordinatewise distance, even
if its states or roots do not converge.

**Proof.** For `t in J`, choose an owner witnessing `s_t in D_eta`. The root
stored on the edge sourced at `s_t` is exact endpoint Nash against the payoff
of `s_t`. Therefore
`gap_div_le_quittingRootAbsorptionMass_of_isZeroEndpointNash`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/FixedTailUniformAbsorption.lean`)
gives edge charge at least `c_eta`. Summing only those nonnegative edge
charges proves the first inequality in `(DV)`.

Decode the entire path by
`QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.pathToFinitePrefix`.
`pathToFinitePrefix_charge`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`)
identifies its prefix charge with `path.chargeSum`, while
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.prefixCharge_le`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Terminal/TerminalExploitabilityWitness.lean`)
bounds that charge by `B`. This proves `(DV)`.

Since `c_eta>0`, the natural-valued visit count in successive finite prefixes
cannot grow without bound. It is monotone, so it eventually stabilizes; the
infinite path eventually avoids `D_eta`. Apply this to every positive
`eta`. If the displayed coordinate deficit failed to tend to zero for some
player, it would be at least one fixed positive `eta` along infinitely many
indices, putting all those states in `D_eta`, a contradiction. ∎

This is stronger than finite consecutive extinction: a counterexample cannot
permit sparse deficit replenishment along one reached exact orbit. The
cofinal floor-clip theorem is not contradicted because its reset tails are
independently selected conditioned states, not vertices already known to lie
on one common exact predecessor path. Thus the remaining source-matching
obligation can be stated sharply: transport cofinally many fixed-gap reset
tails onto one coherent exact path, or produce finite paths whose number of
fixed-gap visits exceeds the universal capacity bound. Merely producing
infinitely many isolated positive edges does neither.

## Proved claims (ordinary mathematics)

- Exact surjection from planned-time laws to live behavioral hazards and joint
  payoff equivalence (Section 1).
- Exact pure-payoff continuity and cluster-set characterization (Section 2).
- Exact adjusted escape identity for every finite-deadline mixed Nash profile
  (Proposition 3; independently reviewed by `CODEX_CEDAR`).
- Existence, in ordinary mathematics, of a finite-deadline Nash behavioral
  realization at every deadline (Section 4).
- The uniform finite-game equilibrium signature necessary for any genuine
  counterexample (Proposition 4).
- Monotonicity and the persistent-limit obstruction for one coherent
  backward-induction selection (Proposition 5; independently reviewed by
  `CODEX_GAUSS`).

- Propositions 27--31 survived Cedar's independent falsification review. They

- Propositions 32--33 survived Cedar's independent falsification review. The

- Proposition 35 replaces exact admissible return by uniformly charged

- Proposition 36 changes the paid-row output interface in the checked

- Proposition 37 further weakens the datum from a fixed paid edge to a

- Proposition 38 is a checked-consumer reduction, not a construction of a

- Proposition 40 weakens viability to arbitrary finite deficit depth. Hence a
  genuine counterexample must extinguish every fixed strict singleton-deficit

  arbitrary re-entry: in a counterexample, the total number of visits to each
  fixed strict singleton-deficit carrier is uniformly bounded on every exact
  floor-admissible path. The remaining floor-clip gap is therefore literal
  source matching of the cofinal isolated resets to one path, not root choice
  inside an already reached state.

## Checks and open objections

- The target payoff must remain fixed after terminal profiles are selected;
  the checked compact payoff-selection theorem handles this only after terminal
  approximate Nash profiles exist at every error.
- A mixed planned-time representation must preserve simultaneous quitting and
  the mass at `Never` exactly.
- Glicksberg cannot be invoked naively because `u_i` is visibly discontinuous
  when all finite quit times jointly escape to infinity.
- The planned-time representation uses private independent laws only. It does
  not manufacture a public correlation device.
- Weak convergence of deadline equilibria does not preserve `Never` atom
  masses: finite mass can escape to `∞`. Proposition 3 is a finite-scale
  identity and does not silently take such a limit.
- An adjusted deficit need not vanish for one deadline; Example B has exact
  value `1/2`.
- Proposition 4 is only a necessary condition for nonexistence. A uniform
  positive adjusted deficit on the finite-equilibrium family would not by
  itself cover arbitrary behavioral profiles and therefore would not be an
  all-behavior counterexample.
- The equality between the prescribed payoff and the maximum permitted
  pure-time value is the most important review point. It uses the exact
  stopping-law mixture identity and support of each finite law inside `K_N`.
- Proposition 18 uses the mesh-refined balanced singleton compiler, not the
  exact solo-periodic compiler. Confusing these would silently discard the
  general table's profitable collision rows.

### 45. Simon necessity: a floor-clipped bootstrap repairs the survival jump

This section answers the exact question in
`questions/SIMON_NECESSITY_SURVIVAL_JUMP.md`. It does **not** yet claim the
full corrected Theorem 3 equivalence. Its output is the missing no-jump lemma
at the first crossing, with the game-theoretic hypotheses which make it true.

#### Source and formal status

I inspected Simon, *The Structure of Non-Zero-Sum Stochastic Games* (2007),
Theorem 3, Section 4.4, pp. 18--19, in the primary local PDF
`literature/Simon2007/The-structure-of-non-zero-sum-stochastic-g_2007_Advances-in-Applied-Mathemat.pdf`,
and Simon, *A Topological Approach to Quitting Games* (2012), Lemma 2.1 and
Theorem 2.1, pp. 184--185. The 2012 paper corrects “stationary” to “stationarily
generated” approximate equilibria and restricts the uniform `rho` lemma to a
fixed compact near-feasible set. It summarizes the necessity argument but
does not spell out the first-crossing repair below.

The relevant non-built transcriptions are all in `Literature/Simon2007.lean`:

- `equilibrium_tail_rational` makes a reached tail `delta`-rational when
  `alpha <= delta * survival`;
- `exists_scale_without_sure_quitter_of_not_instant` excludes a sure quitter
  from every sufficiently accurate rational one-stage equilibrium row;
- `lemma5_corrected_2012` states the corrected uniform motion and no-sure-
  absorption bounds, but ends in `sorry`;
- `exists_supportPurifiedPrefixPath` packages the later uniformly reached
  prefix purification, including its seam and variation budgets; and
- `theorem3_corrected_2012` ends in `sorry`.

Thus Proposition 42 is ordinary mathematics, not a checked Lean declaration.
It uses the same one-stage definitions as the transcription and isolates the
previously missing implication. `CODEX_CEDAR` independently falsified it in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_CEDAR__ROUND_10.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_CEDAR__ROUND_10.md)
and `CODEX_GAUSS` independently repeated the check in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_14.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_14.md).
Neither found a mathematical objection.

#### Monotonicity alone is exactly insufficient

Take one active clock and define rational total quitting probabilities

```text
q_0=15/16,                 q_t=1/2  for t>=1.
```

Then the joint survival sequence is

```text
S_0=1,                     S_t=2^(-(t+3)) for t>=1.
```

For `theta=3/4` and `rho=1/2`, the printed interval is

```text
[theta*rho/3,theta/3]=[1/8,1/4].
```

No `S_t` lies in this interval: the first step jumps from `1` to `1/16`.
The profile uses independent behavioral clocks, has no sure quitter, and has
zero probability of eternal continuation. Therefore the sentence “there
must be a stage” is false for survival products alone. This is not a game
counterexample, because the crossing row violates the uniform-`rho` bound
once it has a rational continuation and is an accurate one-stage equilibrium.

#### The exact clipped-row lemma

Normalize payoffs by a positive scalar so that zero, every terminal payoff,
and every quitting min--max payoff lie in a common coordinate interval of
diameter at most `1`. This loses no approximate-equilibrium or orbit quantifier: the
accuracy parameter is rescaled by the same positive scalar. The normalization
only ensures that the clipped vector below is within distance `1` of the
feasible tail, as required by corrected Lemma 2.1.

Write `chi_i=MinMaxQuit G i`. Let `p` be an `alpha`-equilibrium profile with
`alpha>=0`, let
date `t` be reached under `p` with survival `u>0`, put

```text
r = QuitTailPayoff G p (t+1),       a = p_t,
bar_r_i = max(r_i,chi_i-eta).
```

Fix positive `beta,e,d`, and define `a^sharp` by the following simultaneous
support purification against `bar_r`:

```text
a^sharp_i = 0  if Quit_i(a) < Continue_i(bar_r,a)-beta,
              1  if Continue_i(bar_r,a) < Quit_i(a)-beta,
              a_i otherwise.
```

Assume

```text
alpha < u*beta*d,          beta+2e <= eta,
eta <= sigma,              eta <= rho,                 (B1)
```

where `sigma` is a no-sure-quitter scale supplied by failure of the instant
branch, and `rho` is the corrected uniform scale supplied by failure of the
stationarily generated and instant branches. Choose `d` so that changing a
row by less than `d` changes every forced-Quit and forced-Continue endpoint by
less than `e` on the normalized compact continuation set.

**Proposition 42 (ordinary mathematics, independently reviewed;
first-crossing
bootstrap).** Under `(B1)`,

```text
dist(a^sharp,a) < d,
a^sharp in E_eta(bar_r),
a^sharp_i != 1 for every i,
a^sharp_i <= a_i for every i,                         (B2)
```

and, writing `n` for the number of players and `Q` for total one-row
absorption probability,

```text
Q(a)-Q(a^sharp) <= n*alpha/(u*beta),
1-Q(a) >= rho-n*alpha/(u*beta).                       (B3)
```

**Proof.** First consider a coordinate declared bad-Quit in the definition of
`a^sharp`. If `r_i>=chi_i-eta`, player `i` can Continue now and then follow
her prescribed tail, attaining the continuation coordinate `bar_r_i=r_i`.
If `r_i<chi_i-eta`, the definition of `chi_i` implies that against the fixed
opponents' tail there is a unilateral tail response paying strictly more than
`chi_i-eta=bar_r_i`. Hence in both cases there is a behavioral deviation which
Continues at date `t` and realizes a forced-Continue endpoint at least
`Continue_i(bar_r,a)`.

The prescribed conditional payoff at date `t` is

```text
a_i*Quit_i(a)+(1-a_i)*Continue_i(r,a).
```

Since `bar_r_i>=r_i`, the second endpoint is at most
`Continue_i(bar_r,a)`. Bad-Quit therefore makes the displayed deviation gain
strictly more than `a_i*beta`. The global equilibrium inequality, multiplied
by reach probability `u`, gives

```text
a_i < alpha/(u*beta).                                  (B4)
```

If coordinate `i` is bad-Continue against `bar_r`, then it is also bad-
Continue against the smaller actual continuation `r`. Forcing Quit at date
`t` gains strictly more than `(1-a_i)*beta`, so

```text
1-a_i < alpha/(u*beta).                                (B5)
```

Equations `(B4)`--`(B5)` and `alpha<u*beta*d` give
`dist(a^sharp,a)<d`. Uniform endpoint continuity and
`beta+2e<=eta` give `a^sharp in E_eta(bar_r)` by the standard two-endpoint
purification calculation. The vector `bar_r` is `eta`-rational. If a bad-
Continue coordinate existed, then `a^sharp_i=1`; monotonicity from `eta` to
`sigma` would contradict the no-sure-quitter conclusion at scale `sigma`.
Thus no coordinate is bad-Continue, so `a^sharp<=a` coordinatewise.

Only bad-Quit coordinates are deleted, and each deletion is bounded by
`(B4)`. The elementary product inequality

```text
Q(a)-Q(a^sharp) <= sum_i (a_i-a^sharp_i)
```

proves the first inequality in `(B3)`. The clipped vector is within distance
`1` of the feasible vector `r`; monotonicity from `eta` to `rho` lets the
corrected uniform-`rho` lemma apply to `a^sharp`, giving
`Q(a^sharp)<=1-rho`. Combining the two estimates proves the second inequality
in `(B3)`. ∎

The crucial point is attainability: `bar_r` is not inserted as a fictitious
terminal payoff. For each bad-Quit test, its raised coordinate is implemented
by that player's actual unilateral best response against the prescribed
opponents after all players Continue at the crossing row. This is why the
global equilibrium inequality legitimately pays for the deleted Quit mass.

#### Exact interval selection

Let `S_t` be the original profile's survival before date `t`, and suppose
`S_T<=theta/3<S_(T-1)` for the first time. Apply Proposition 42 with
`u=S_(T-1)`. If

```text
alpha <= rho*theta*beta/(6*n),                         (B6)
```

then strictness of `u>theta/3` and `(B3)` give

```text
1-Q(p_(T-1)) > rho/2,
theta*rho/6 < S_T <= theta/3.                          (B7)
```

Thus the printed lower endpoint `theta*rho/3` need not be preserved; the
constant-factor repaired interval `[theta*rho/6,theta/3]` is guaranteed. All
earlier survivals are larger. Choosing the global equilibrium error also below
`eta*theta*rho/6` makes every tail through `T` `eta`-rational by
`equilibrium_tail_rational`. At that point
`exists_supportPurifiedPrefixPath` applies with the honest common survival
floor `theta*rho/6`; its seam and deleted-mass errors tend to zero with
`alpha`.

This removes the circularity: corrected Lemma 2.1 is first applied to the
attainable floor-clipped crossing row, not to the as-yet-unproved rational
actual tail. Its output supplies the missing lower survival bound; only then
is conditional rationality applied to the actual tail.

#### What remains before the full equivalence

Proposition 42 repairs the only survival-selection defect recorded in
`literature/Simon2007/defects.md` and survived independent falsification by
`CODEX_CEDAR` and `CODEX_GAUSS`. The full `(i)->(iii)` or `(i)->(ii)` assembly must still pin the constants linking
the bounded finite-orbit variation hypothesis to `theta`, the prefix quitting
mass, the purification seam, and the large-approximate-path compiler. The
transcription contains the relevant later declarations, including
`exists_supportPurifiedPrefixPath` and
`exists_cyclicOrbit_of_large_approximatePath`, but
`theorem3_corrected_2012` is not assembled and remains `sorry`.

Next concrete check: independently recalculate Proposition 42's min--max
deviation at a bad-Quit coordinate, especially the case of a nonattained tail
best response, and then write the full constant ledger from a bounded
`FiniteNearOrbitCondition` failure to the approximate-prefix contradiction.

### 46. The repaired crossing yields the full equilibrium-to-cycle direction

The preceding bootstrap can be inserted directly into the existing
approximate-path compiler. A bounded-orbit hypothesis and its derived
`theta` are unnecessary.

**Proposition 43 (independently reviewed conditional ordinary mathematics;
conditional on corrected Lemma 2.1 and Proposition 42).** Let a finite
quitting game have neither
stationarily generated approximate equilibria nor instant approximate
equilibria. If it has approximate equilibria, then it satisfies
`CyclicOrbitCondition`: for every `epsilon>0` it has a positive-absorption
cyclic `F_epsilon` orbit whose tail vectors are `epsilon`-rational.

Consequently Proposition 42 fills the missing `hequilibriumCyclic` direction
in the proof skeleton of `Literature.Simon2007.theorem3`; with the corrected
hard-branch hypothesis it supplies the corresponding direction of
`theorem3_corrected_2012`. This is not a Lean claim: corrected Lemma 2.1 and
the final theorem still contain `sorry`. `CODEX_GAUSS` independently checked
the full constant ledger and downstream compiler use in Round 15 and found no
mathematical objection.

**Proof with constant ledger.** Positive scaling commutes with terminal
payoffs, quitting min--max values, one-stage payoffs, support inequalities,
and every orbit edge, while scaling the error by the same factor. Normalize
the common payoff diameter as in Proposition 42 and rescale back at the end.

Fix `epsilon>0`. From corrected Lemma 2.1 choose a player `j` with positive
solo payoff `v_j>0` and a uniform scale `rho in (0,1)`. From failure of the
instant branch choose a no-sure-quitter scale `sigma>0`. Choose

```text
0 < eta <= min(epsilon/5,rho,sigma),
beta=eta/4,                 e=eta/8.
```

Then `4*eta<=epsilon` and `beta+2e<=eta`. Compact endpoint continuity supplies
`d>0` such that a row displacement below `d` changes every forced endpoint by
less than `e`. Let `M>0` bound all terminal rewards. Invoke
`exists_cyclicOrbit_of_large_approximatePath` at working accuracy `eta` and
target accuracy `epsilon`; write `H>0` for its required exact-variation
threshold.

Choose constants

```text
0 < U < min(1,exp(-4*H/rho^2)),
s = rho*U/2,
0 < L_0 < min(1,rho/2,H/(2*rho),eta/(8*M)).            (C1)
```

Finally choose an equilibrium accuracy `alpha>0` so small that

```text
alpha < U*v_j/2,
alpha < eta*s,
alpha < s*beta*d,
n*alpha/(s*beta) < L_0,                               (C2)
```

where `n` is the number of players. Approximate-equilibrium existence supplies
an `alpha`-equilibrium profile `p`.

The positive-solo forcing lemma
`exists_tailSurvival_lt_of_equilibrium_positiveSolo`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) and the first inequality in `(C2)` give a date
whose survival is below `U`. Let `T` be the first such date. Thus

```text
S_T < U <= S_(T-1).                                   (C3)
```

Apply Proposition 42 at the crossing row. Its reach is `u=S_(T-1)>=U`, and
the last two inequalities in `(C2)` imply

```text
n*alpha/(u*beta) < rho/2.
```

Therefore the crossing row has Continue mass greater than `rho/2`, so

```text
S_T > rho*U/2=s.                                      (C4)
```

All rows before `T` are now uniformly reached. Applying Proposition 42 to
each such row, with `u>=S_T>s`, gives

```text
1-Q(p_t) >= rho-n*alpha/(s*beta) > rho/2              (C5)
```

for every `t<T`. This is the step which rules out a second hidden macroscopic
jump after the first-crossing repair.

For `0<=x<=1-rho/2`, the elementary logarithmic inequality

```text
-log(1-x) <= x/(1-x) <= 2*x/rho
```

and `(C3)`--`(C5)` imply

```text
sum_(t<T) Q(p_t) > (rho/2)*(-log U) > 2*H/rho.        (C6)
```

By `(C2)` and `(C4)`, every actual tail through date `T` is `eta`-rational via
`equilibrium_tail_rational`. Apply `exists_supportPurifiedPrefixPath` with
common survival floor `s`. Put

```text
L=n*alpha/(s*beta).
```

The resulting approximate `F_eta` path `z` starts at the feasible tail at
date `T` and satisfies

```text
z.totalError <= 2*M*L < eta/4,                        (C7)
z.exactVariation >= rho*(sum_(t<T)Q(p_t)-L) > H.      (C8)
```

Here `(C7)` uses `L<L_0<eta/(8M)`, while `(C8)` uses `(C6)` and
`L<L_0<H/(2rho)`. The start point has norm at most `M`. Every purified row is
`eta`-rational and has no sure quitter because `eta<=sigma`. Therefore every
hypothesis of `exists_cyclicOrbit_of_large_approximatePath` holds, and it
returns the required positive-absorption `epsilon`-cyclic orbit. ∎

### 47. Largest-hazard exposure proves the normalized-motion branch

The apparent rate obstruction in the preceding question is an artifact of
bounding every one-stage deviation by `gamma` and then dividing by the
largest hazard. The exact pure-time formula retains the deviator's own hazard,
which cancels that denominator for every player other than the selected
largest quitter. A slowly diverging exposure window handles the selected
quitter.

**Proposition 44 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics;
normalized-motion half of corrected Lemma 2.1).** Let `G` be a finite quitting
game with no instant approximate equilibria. Suppose there are positive
`gamma_k -> 0`, vectors `r_k` which are `gamma_k`-rational, and rows
`p_k in E_(gamma_k)(r_k)` such that, writing

```text
q_k = Q(p_k),
y_k = f(r_k,p_k),
```

one has `q_k>0` and

```text
||y_k-r_k|| < gamma_k*q_k.                           (D1)
```

Then `G` has stationarily generated approximate equilibria in the exact 2012
sense: for every punishment slack `delta>0` and equilibrium slack
`epsilon>0`, some row repeated through a finite horizon greater than one,
followed by an actual `delta`-punishment of one player, is an
`(epsilon+delta)`-equilibrium against every unilateral behavioral deviation.

This proposition requires no lower bound on `max_j p_k^j/gamma_k`.

**Static identities.** Fix one sufficiently large `k` and abbreviate
`gamma=gamma_k`, `p=p_k`, `q=q_k`, and `r=r_k`. Define the payoff conditional
on absorption in the stationary row by

```text
h_n = quittingRewardPart(p,n)/q.
```

Then `f(h,p)=h`, while `(D1)` and
`f(r,p)-r=q(h-r)` give

```text
||h-r|| < gamma.                                     (D2)
```

For player `n`, put

```text
c_n = product_(ell != n) (1-p_ell),
A_n = forced-Quit payoff,
B_n(x) = forced-Continue payoff with continuation x,
g_n = A_n-B_n(h).
```

Failure of the instant branch supplies a fixed positive scale below which no
rational approximate row has a sure quitter, via
`exists_scale_without_sure_quitter_of_not_instant`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`). Hence, after discarding finitely many `k`, every
`p_n<1`. The two support inequalities and `(D2)` imply, with
`zeta=gamma+||h-r||<2gamma`,

```text
g_n <= zeta,                                         (D3)
p_n>0  ==>  -g_n <= zeta.                            (D4)
```

Indeed only the continuation term changes when `r` is replaced by `h`, and
its coefficient is `c_n<=1`. The stationary fixed-point identity is

```text
h_n = p_n A_n+(1-p_n)B_n(h),
B_n(h)-h_n = -p_n g_n.                               (D5)
```

**Exact pure-time calculation.** Against stationary opponents, quitting at
date `t` has payoff `V_(n,t)` satisfying, when `c_n<1`,

```text
V_(n,t)-h_n
 = c_n^t(1-p_n)g_n
   - p_n*g_n*(1-c_n^t)/(1-c_n).                      (D6)
```

When `c_n<1`, the formula at `t=infinity` drops the first term. If `c_n=1`,
the finite geometric-sum form of `(D6)` remains valid, but the stationary
Never payoff is handled separately; for the only possible positive-hazard
player `(D5)` forces `g_n=0`. Arbitrary behavioral deviations reduce to
the supremum of these deterministic quit times and `Never` by
`sSup_range_quittingTerminalPayoff_update_eq_pureTime`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/BehaviorPureTimeExtremality.lean`). This
is unrestricted strategy-class coverage, not a bounded-controller check.

Choose `j` with largest marginal

```text
m=p_j=max_n p_n>0.
```

For `n!=j`, opponents include `j`, so `1-c_n>=m`, while `p_n<=m`. If
`g_n>=0`, `(D6)` is at most `g_n<=zeta`; if `g_n<0`, it is at most

```text
p_n*(-g_n)/(1-c_n) <= zeta.                          (D7)
```

The zero-hazard case causes no exception because then the numerator in
`(D7)` is zero.

For `j`, a deterministic Quit before date `L` gains at most `zeta` when
`g_j>=0`, and at most

```text
L*m*zeta                                               (D8)
```

when `g_j<0`, using the finite geometric sum rather than dividing by
`1-c_j`. If `j` continues through all `L` stationary dates, a
`delta`-punishment gives every continuation response a tail payoff at most
`chi_j+delta`. Rationality of `r` and `(D2)` give

```text
chi_j+delta-h_j <= delta+gamma+||h-r||.               (D9)
```

Thus every pure-time deviation of `j`, including `Never`, pays at most

```text
h_j + L*m*zeta + delta+gamma+||h-r||.                 (D10)
```

**Tail replacement and choice of horizon.** Let `B` bound in absolute value
all terminal and behavioral terminal payoffs; then also `|h_n|<=B`. The
prescribed stationary-prefix payoff differs from `h` only on survival through
all `L` stationary dates, hence coordinatewise by at most

```text
2B(1-q)^L <= 2B(1-m)^L.                              (D11)
```

For `n!=j`, an arbitrary punishment tail can change a deviation which first
reaches that tail by at most another `2B(1-m)^L`, because `j` remains among
the fixed opponents. Combining `(D7)` and `(D11)`, the regret of `n!=j` is at
most

```text
zeta+4B(1-m)^L.                                      (D12)
```

Choose `gamma<1/4`, put `R=1/sqrt(gamma)`, and take

```text
L=ceil(R/m).
```

Then `L>=3`, so the displayed stationary-prefix horizon `L-1` is greater than
one, and

```text
R <= Lm < R+1,
(1-m)^L <= exp(-Lm) <= exp(-R),
Lm*zeta < 2sqrt(gamma)+2gamma.                        (D13)
```

The excess over `delta` in `(D10)`--`(D12)` therefore tends to zero. For the
given `epsilon`, choose `k` so large that both

```text
zeta+4B exp(-R) < epsilon,
Lm*zeta+gamma+||h-r||+2B exp(-R) < epsilon.
```

An actual punishment within `delta` exists from the infimum definition of
`chi_j` (`exists_punishmentWithin`, `Literature/Simon2007.lean`). The resulting
profile is an `(epsilon+delta)`-equilibrium by `(D10)`--`(D13)` and pure-time
extremality. This proves the proposition. ∎

**Boundary tests.** If a nonselected player has `p_n=0`, the dangerous
Continue term is exactly zero. If the selected player's opponents never quit,
then `c_j=1` and `(D5)` gives `g_j=0`, so the geometric denominator is never
used. If `m/gamma -> 0`, the calendar length grows faster, but only the
dimensionless exposure `Lm` occurs in the error bounds; `(D13)` is unchanged.

**What this changes.** The normalized-motion alternative in the compactness
proof of Simon (2012), Lemma 2.1 is no longer a source-level black box: its
contrapositive compiles explicitly to the stationarily generated branch. The
remaining parts of the corrected lemma are the compact `Q(p)->1` extraction
to the instant branch and the sign-pattern clause. No Lean or integration
seal is claimed for Proposition 44; the cited production pure-time theorem
supplies the semantic strategy-class endpoint, while the notation adapter to
the literature transcription remains to be written by a formalization agent.

splice, and the inclusive horizon in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_16.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_16.md).
`CODEX_CEDAR` independently repeated the falsification audit in

Both verdicts are valid ordinary mathematics after the explicit
`gamma_k`-rationality statement above. The Gauss review supplied the harmless

### 48. Near-total absorption rounds quantitatively to the instant branch

The other quantitative clause of corrected Lemma 2.1 does not need a
subsequence-only compactness argument. A finite product gives an explicit
coordinate to round, and endpoint stability preserves the approximate row.

**Proposition 45 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics;
total-absorption half of corrected Lemma 2.1).** Let `K` be a bounded set of
continuation vectors for a
finite `N`-player quitting game, with `N>=1`. Suppose that for arbitrarily
small `gamma>0` there are `r in K` and `p in E_gamma(r)` such that `r` is
`gamma`-rational and

```text
Q(p)>1-gamma.                                         (E1)
```

Then the game has instant approximate equilibria. Quantitatively, if all
terminal rewards and all coordinates in `K` are bounded in absolute value by
`C`, one obtains a sure-quitter row in

```text
E_(gamma+4C*gamma^(1/N))(r).                          (E2)
```

**Proof.** Choose a witnessing scale with `0<gamma<1`. Since

```text
product_n(1-p_n)=1-Q(p)<gamma,
```

some player `j` satisfies

```text
1-p_j<gamma^(1/N).                                   (E3)
```

Let `p_hat` be obtained by replacing `p_j` by `1`. For player `j`, both of
her own forced endpoints overwrite the `j` coordinate, so they are unchanged
by this rounding. For `n!=j`, each forced endpoint is affine in `p_j`; both
endpoint values lie in `[-C,C]` after enlarging `C` once to cover one-stage
continuation values. Hence changing `p_j` by less than `gamma^(1/N)` changes
each endpoint by at most `2C*gamma^(1/N)`. The support of every other player
is unchanged, while the new Continue-support condition for `j` is vacuous.
Transporting the two endpoint inequalities of `p in E_gamma(r)` therefore
gives `(E2)`.

Put `eta=gamma+4C*gamma^(1/N)`. Rationality is monotone from `gamma` to
`eta`, `eta->0`, and `(p_hat)_j=1`. Given any target error and punishment
slack, choose `gamma` so that `2eta` is below the target error and choose an
actual punishment within the displayed slack. The compiler
`instantProfile_isQuitEpsilonEquilibrium`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) gives a `(2eta+delta)`-equilibrium. In production
semantics the same all-behavior sure-quitter mechanism is captured by
`quittingInstantPunishmentεEquilibriumExistence_of_sureQuitter`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/InstantPunishmentEquivalence.lean`).
Thus instant approximate equilibria exist. ∎

The distance-one feasible neighborhood in the corrected lemma is a compact
choice of `K`, so its continuation bound `C` is uniform. Proposition 45 proves
the `Q(p)<=1-rho` compactness alternative with an explicit modulus; no claim
that one fixed quitter works at every accuracy is needed.

Together, Propositions 44--45 prove the full quantitative clause (2) of
corrected Lemma 2.1 by contrapositive, subject only to the ordinary notation
adapters stated above. Both independent reviews found Proposition 45 valid;
the Cedar review emphasizes that the production sure-quitter theorem confirms
the mechanism but is not itself the missing `E_eta(r)` notation adapter. If no
single positive `rho` works on the compact carrier, select a sequence of
scales tending to zero. Along an infinite subsequence either normalized motion
fails, invoking Proposition 44, or the absorption cap fails, invoking
Proposition 45. Both contradict the respective hard-branch exclusions.

The remaining necessity obligation is now the positive-normal-solo part of
clause (1), not clause (2). Proposition 43 never uses the later harmed-player
relation. The 2007 proof derives positivity from failure of stationary
equilibria, while the 2012 correction changes the strategic hypothesis to
failure of stationarily generated equilibria; that logical adapter must be
proved rather than inferred.

The downstream proof uses a positive solo payoff only to force survival below

attempt was therefore to obtain positivity from the checked zero-solo
disjunct on a putative counterexample branch. The implication is formally
true but useless: its premises are inconsistent in the production semantics.

**Failed Proposition 46 (vacuous implication; retained as a minimal failed
shortcut).** Assume Propositions 44--45. On the branch with no uniform-
equilibrium payoff, no instant approximate equilibria, and no stationarily
generated approximate equilibria, the hard-branch implication

```text
terminal approximate equilibria at every error
    ==> CyclicOrbitCondition
```

follows without clause (1) of corrected Lemma 2.1.

**Proof.** The checked zero-solo theorem
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_zeroSolo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/ZeroSoloDisjunct.lean`) says that if
every own singleton payoff is nonpositive, the all-Continue profile gives the
zero uniform-equilibrium payoff. Therefore failure of uniform-payoff existence

On the compact distance-one feasible carrier, if no common positive `rho`
satisfies the two quantitative conclusions, choose scales tending to zero and
witnessing rows. One of the two failed inequalities occurs along an infinite
subsequence. Failure of normalized motion contradicts exclusion of the

common corrected `rho` exists.

Formally, Proposition 43 now applies using the positive solo player just
obtained and
this common `rho`. Its survival forcing declaration
`exists_tailSurvival_lt_of_equilibrium_positiveSolo`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) assumes positivity, not normality or a harmed
player. All later calls use only the quantitative `rho`, the independent
no-sure scale, and the approximate-equilibrium profile. Thus the harmed-player

However, the premise combination already contains a contradiction. By

(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Terminal/TargetTail/TerminalUniformPayoffSelection.lean`)
and `quittingApproximateEquilibriumExistence_iff_behavior`

`SuppliedQuittingSimonFiniteOrbitNecessity`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/SimonFiniteOrbit/SuppliedCorrespondence.lean`),
whose premise deliberately contains approximate-equilibrium existence but no

What survives is narrower and exact. The harmed-normal-player half of clause

payoff** is essential to
`exists_tailSurvival_lt_of_equilibrium_positiveSolo`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`). Propositions 42--45 therefore do not yet supply

```text
not stationarily generated + not instant
    ==> exists player j with 0 < solo payoff(j) ?
```

The 2012 paper states this as part of Lemma 2.1 and says the corrected

### 50. Positive-absorption stationary profiles already generate the corrected branch

The sign seam can be narrowed further without any one-stage support
purification. A global stationary approximate equilibrium already controls
all deviations before the splice. Positive absorption is enough to hide the
tail from every nonpunished player, while individual rationality controls the
punished player's tail.

**Proposition 47 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; absorbing
stationary-to-generated adapter).** Suppose that for arbitrarily small `eta>0` a finite
quitting game has a stationary `eta`-equilibrium with product row `p`, against
all behavioral deviations, and with one-stage absorption probability
`Q(p)>0`. Then the game has stationarily generated approximate equilibria in
the corrected 2012 sense.

**Proof.** Let `h` be the terminal payoff vector of the infinite stationary
profile. Since that profile is an `eta`-equilibrium and the punishment value
`chi_i` is the infimum over opponent plans of player `i`'s behavioral best-
reply value,

```text
h_i >= chi_i-eta                                      (F1)
```

for every player. This uses the full best-reply supremum and is unaffected by
nonattainment of either infimum or supremum.

Choose `j` with largest marginal `m=p_j>0`. Repeat `p` for exactly `L>=3`
dates and, conditional on complete survival, switch to an actual
`delta`-punishment of `j`. The production version is
`quittingStationaryPrefixThenRoots` together with
`IsQuittingRootSequencePunishmentWithin`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/Existence/StationarilyGeneratedBranch.lean`).
Let `B` bound in absolute value every terminal payoff, hence every profile
payoff and every coordinate of `h`.

The prescribed spliced payoff differs from `h` only if all players survive
the first `L` dates. Therefore

```text
|P_i-h_i| <= 2B(1-Q(p))^L <= 2B(1-m)^L.              (F2)
```

Fix a nonselected player `i!=j` and any behavioral deviation. Compare it with
the identical deviation against the infinite stationary opponents. The two
opponent profiles differ only after date `L`; reaching that date requires the
fixed opponent `j` to Continue on every prefix date, an event of probability
at most `(1-m)^L`, independently of `i`'s behavior. Hence the deviation payoff
in the splice is at most its stationary value plus `2B(1-m)^L`. The global
stationary `eta`-Nash inequality and `(F2)` give regret at most

```text
eta+4B(1-m)^L.                                       (F3)
```

For player `j`, take an arbitrary deviation in the splice and form an
auxiliary deviation against the infinite stationary opponents: use the same
behavior for the first `L` dates, then, if the game remains live, resume the
prescribed stationary marginal. The auxiliary payoff is at most `h_j+eta`.
Conditional on reaching the splice, its stationary tail value is exactly
`h_j`, whereas the actual punishment holds every continuation response to at
most `chi_j+delta<=h_j+eta+delta` by `(F1)`. Thus the actual deviation payoff
is at most `h_j+2eta+delta`, without needing any opponent hazard for `j`.
Using `(F2)`, its regret is at most

```text
2eta+delta+2B(1-m)^L.                                (F4)
```

Given independent positive target slack `epsilon` and punishment slack
`delta`, first choose an absorbing stationary witness with `eta<epsilon/4`.
Then choose `L>=3` so large that

```text
4B(1-m)^L < epsilon/2.
```

Equations `(F3)`--`(F4)` are then below `epsilon+delta`. With the inclusive
prefix convention, use horizon `L-1>1`. This is exactly
`QuittingStationarilyGeneratedApproximateEquilibria`; all deviation
quantifiers are behavioral from the start. ∎

`CODEX_GAUSS` independently reconstructed `(F1)`--`(F4)`, including the
selected player's arbitrary continuation behavior, min--max nonattainment,
the inclusive horizon `L-1`, and the quantifier order, in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_17.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_17.md).
The verdict is valid ordinary mathematics, with no Lean seal.
`CODEX_CEDAR` supplied a second independent falsification audit in

It independently checked the three behavioral couplings, `(F1)` without
attainment, all marginal boundary cases, and the constants, with the same
