**Sharp scope.** Proposition 47 does not say that the stationary branch alone
implies the corrected generated branch. It requires positive absorption at
arbitrarily small stationary-equilibrium errors. Consequently, if a game has
no stationarily generated approximate equilibria, then at some fixed positive
accuracy every stationary approximate equilibrium must be the unique
zero-absorption row, namely all Continue. Under the negation of positive solo,
the 2007 helper constructs exactly this all-Continue stationary equilibrium.
The sole surviving case is therefore an **isolated all-Continue stationary
boundary**: no positive-absorption stationary approximate equilibria at one
fixed accuracy, and no instant branch. Proving this boundary impossible, or
exhibiting it exactly, is strictly smaller than the original sign clause.

Next concrete check: characterize the tangent product rows near all Continue.
Either a positive-hazard stationary approximate row exists, feeding
Proposition 47, or its exact first-order singleton complementarity system must
force a sure-quitter/punishment witness. A merely local one-stage Nash root is
insufficient; the adapter must retain unrestricted stationary deviations or
land directly in the instant consumer.

### 51. Punishment removes the sign restriction from the no-harm argument

The harmed-normal-player half of corrected Lemma 2.1 can in fact be proved
directly from failure of the generated branch. The 2007 stationary proof
needed the selected owner's solo payoff to be nonnegative because its
infinite rare-quit profile let the owner deviate to Never for zero. A finite
rare-quit prefix followed by an actual punishment removes exactly that sign
restriction.

**Proposition 48 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; corrected
no-harm adapter).** Let `j` be a normal player, so `v_j>=chi_j`, and suppose

```text
v_i <= r({j})_i                 for every i!=j.       (G1)
```

Then the game has stationarily generated approximate equilibria. Therefore,
if stationarily generated approximate equilibria do not exist, every normal
player `j` strictly harms another normal player:

```text
exists k!=j normal with r({j})_k < v_k.              (G2)
```

No instant-branch hypothesis is needed.

**Proof.** Fix independent positive equilibrium slack `epsilon` and punishment
slack `delta`. Let `B>0` bound every terminal and profile payoff. Choose a
small `a in (0,1)` and repeat for `L>=3` dates the row in which only `j` quits,
with probability `a`. If all `L` dates survive, switch to a `delta`-punishment
of `j`. Write

```text
s=(1-a)^L.
```

In the infinite stationary rare-`j` profile, every player's payoff is
`r({j})_i`. For an outsider `i!=j`, a deterministic deviation which quits at
a prefix date receives `v_i` if `j` continues at that date and the joint
payoff `r({i,j})_i` if `j` quits simultaneously. By `(G1)` and the bound `B`,
its gain over `r({j})_i` is at most `2Ba`. Quitting later only adds earlier
dates at which `j` may already have quit for the baseline payoff; Never earns
the baseline exactly. Hence

```text
every outsider deviation against the infinite rare row
    pays at most r({j})_i+2Ba.                        (G3)
```

This includes every behavioral deviation by
`sSup_range_quittingTerminalPayoff_update_eq_pureTime`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/BehaviorPureTimeExtremality.lean`).

Replacing the infinite continuation after date `L` changes an outsider's
deviation payoff by at most `2Bs`, because reaching the splice requires the
fixed opponent `j` to survive all `L` dates. The prescribed payoff also
changes by at most `2Bs`. Thus outsider regret in the spliced profile is at
most

```text
2Ba+4Bs.                                               (G4)
```

For the selected owner `j`, every deviation quitting before the splice earns
exactly `v_j`. Every response which reaches the punishment tail earns at most
`chi_j+delta<=v_j+delta`. The prescribed payoff is a mixture of `v_j`, with
weight `1-s`, and a bounded punishment-tail payoff, with weight `s`; it is at
least `v_j-2Bs`. Hence owner regret is at most

```text
delta+2Bs.                                             (G5)
```

First choose `a` with `2Ba<epsilon/2`, then choose `L>=3` with
`4B(1-a)^L<epsilon/2`. Equations `(G4)`--`(G5)` give an
`(epsilon+delta)`-equilibrium and horizon `L-1>1`, with the punishment slack
quantified independently. This proves the generated branch.

For `(G2)`, suppose a normal `j` harms no other normal player. Then `(G1)`
holds for normal outsiders by hypothesis. For an abnormal outsider `i`, the
proved `lemma3` (`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) gives

```text
r({j})_i >= chi_i > v_i,
```

because `j!=i`. Proposition 48 then contradicts failure of the generated
branch. ∎

This closes the harmed-player relation in ordinary mathematics under the
corrected hypothesis, and more strongly without instant exclusion. It still
does not supply the positive-normal-solo existence used by Proposition 43.
Round 17 independently checked `(G3)`--`(G5)`, the pure-time-to-behavioral
reduction, punishment nonattainment, and the abnormal-outsider use of
`lemma3`; it found no mathematical objection. The standalone implication
from failure of the generated branch to a positive solo remains unavailable,
but Section 52 shows that the positive-existence proof should not ask for it.
Round 14 is a second independent validation, including negative owner payoff
and simultaneous collision. It emphasizes the exact formal-status boundary:
the literature `MinMaxQuit` used by `lemma3` and production
`quittingPunishmentValue` have no named checked adapter, so `(G2)` is ordinary
mathematics rather than an integrated Lean consequence.

### 52. Zero solo is a solved bypass, not a missing sign case

The preceding section still phrased positive-normal-solo existence as a seam.
That is the wrong universal obligation. The zero-solo case does not need to
enter the finite-orbit necessity theorem at all: it already has a checked
uniform-equilibrium payoff. On the complement, positivity and normality are
automatic.

**Proposition 49 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; bifurcated sign
interface).** Every finite quitting reward table satisfies

```text
IsQuittingZeroSolo reward
or
exists j, IsQuittingNormalPlayer reward j
       and 0 < quittingSoloSelfPayoff reward j.       (H1)
```

Consequently, assuming Propositions 42--45, if a game has neither
stationarily generated nor instant approximate equilibria and does have
behavioral approximate equilibria, then

```text
IsQuittingZeroSolo reward
or
CyclicOrbitCondition reward.                         (H2)
```

The first disjunct is consumed directly by
`quittingGame_isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_zero_of_zeroSolo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/ZeroSoloDisjunct.lean`); the second
is the conclusion of Proposition 43. Thus `(H2)`, rather than an unqualified
inhabitant of `SuppliedQuittingSimonFiniteOrbitNecessity`, is the exact
necessity interface required by this positive-existence route.

**Proof.** If `IsQuittingZeroSolo reward` holds, take the first disjunct. If
it fails, finite classical logic gives a player `j` with positive own
singleton payoff. Such a player is normal. Indeed the proved `lemma3`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) says that every abnormal player has strictly
negative solo payoff, contradicting positivity. This proves `(H1)`.

For `(H2)`, split on zero solo. In the zero-solo branch stop at the first
disjunct. Otherwise the definition-level negation alone supplies a positive
solo player; Proposition 43 uses only that positivity, not normality,
`lemma3`, or the harmed-player relation.
Propositions 44--45 and the exclusions of the generated and instant branches
supply the common quantitative `rho` on the compact feasible carrier.
Proposition 42 repairs every survival crossing, and the constant ledger of
Proposition 43 yields the positive cyclic orbit. ∎

`CODEX_GAUSS` independently checked both conclusions in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_18.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_18.md).
The review's proof of `(H2)` is the adapter-free proof now written above;
`lemma3` is used only for the stronger normal-player label in `(H1)`.

**Quantifier and boundary audit.** This does not assert that every zero-solo
game is stationarily generated or has a positive cyclic orbit. It deliberately
changes the consumer to a disjunction. A one-player negative-solo game tests
the first branch; a positive solo payoff can never belong to an abnormal
player by `lemma3`. The 2012 source states the stronger positive-normal-solo
claim inside corrected Lemma 2.1, but the all-game uniform-payoff proof needs
only `(H2)`. There is not yet a named production adapter for the literature
`lemma3`; this is ordinary source-to-project mathematics, not an `L` or `A`
claim.

This removes the sign seam from the positive-existence route. It does not
repair the existing negative-obstruction capstone, whose supplied premise
demands the cyclic condition even on zero-solo games. That stronger standalone
necessity theorem should remain open unless zero-solo is separately shown to
enter one of its excluded branches.

### 53. Positive absorption refines the off-standard-Q LCP gate

Proposition 47 can be applied directly to the checked stationary gate. The
abstract family does not record absorption, but outside zero solo its Nash
inequality forces absorption at every error below a positive solo payoff.

**Proposition 50 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; generated/LCP
trichotomy).** Every finite quitting reward table satisfies

```text
IsQuittingZeroSolo reward
or QuittingStationarilyGeneratedApproximateEquilibria reward
or StandardQMatrixSide reward.                       (I1)
```

Equivalently, a non-zero-solo game outside `StandardQMatrixSide` lies in the
corrected generated branch. Hence any non-zero-solo, non-generated hard game
is on the named standard-Q side.

**Proof from the packaged stationary gate.** Apply
`hasQuittingStationaryApproximateEquilibria_or_standardQMatrixSide`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/StationaryExistence.lean`).
The standard-Q output is the third disjunct. In the stationary output, split
on zero solo. If zero solo holds, take the first disjunct. Otherwise choose a
player `j` and `s>0` with `s=r({j})_j`.

For every `epsilon` with `0<epsilon<s`, the checked stationary output supplies
an unrestricted behavioral terminal `epsilon`-Nash profile generated by one
product root. That root must have positive absorption. Indeed zero product
absorption forces every marginal Quit probability to vanish, so the profile
is all Continue and pays zero. Player `j` can instead Quit at date zero and
receive `s>epsilon`, contradicting the Nash inequality. Thus arbitrarily
small errors admit positive-absorption stationary Nash profiles, exactly the
premise of Proposition 47. The generated branch follows. ∎

Round 18 independently checked this abstract proof and the zero-player,
zero-absorption, and varying-root boundaries. In particular, no audit of the
first-layer, homogeneous, or analytic-endpoint constructors is needed:
positive solo forces absorption directly from the global stationary Nash
inequality.

**Scope.** This is a generated-interface refinement of
`exists_stationaryUniformEquilibriumPayoff_or_standardQMatrixSide`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/StationaryExistence.lean`).
It is not a new uniform-payoff class: all three off-Q regimes were already
checked at stationary uniform-payoff strength, and
`standardQMatrixSide_of_not_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff` already places a
putative full counterexample on the standard-Q side. The new content is the
positive-absorption audit needed to feed the corrected Simon generated
branch, not merely the abstract stationary family.

Next concrete check: ask for a second independent audit of the abstract
positive-solo argument and then test whether the resulting non-zero-solo
standard-Q hard branch gives any additional input to the source-matched
multi-edge recurrence producer. No constructor-level positivity audit is
needed for `(I1)`.

### 54. Logarithmic decoding reduces the projective-Q-bar side to the corrected stationary alternative

The standard-Q side itself has a checked algebraic split:

```text
standardQMatrixSide_qbar_or_residual
    : StandardQMatrixSide reward
      -> ProjectiveQBarMatrixBranch reward or ResidualHardClass reward.
```

This is in `UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/Gate.lean`.  On the
first branch, `ProjectiveQBarMatrixBranch.full_projectiveQBar` supplies the
exact hypothesis of
`exists_continuous_zeroPerfect_of_projectiveQBar`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/PrincipalQContinuousPath.lean`).
The checked conclusion is a continuous singleton absorption path satisfying
both sequential zero-perfection inequalities. It is not an ordinary strategy
profile and the declaration makes no equilibrium claim.

The following decoder lands at the exact local branch S.3, not at terminal
Nash. This distinction is forced by the checked refutation
`not_quittingSequentialPerfectionErrorExponent`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/ErrorExponentRefutation.lean`).

**Proposition 51 (unreviewed proof draft; continuous path to branch S.3).**
Let `I` be finite and let

```text
reward : nonempty quitting coalitions -> Payoff I
path   : AbsorptionPath I
```

satisfy

```text
IsContinuousAbsorptionPath path
and IsSequentiallyPerfectAbsorptionPath reward path 0.
```

Then `QuittingSequentiallyεPerfectAbsorbingExistence reward`: for every
`delta>0` there is a product-root sequence which absorbs almost surely and
whose row at every date is `delta`-perfect against its actual next-tail
payoff.

**Proof.** Write

```text
pi_i(t) = path.value t {i},
s_i     = reward({i})_i,
gamma(t)= absorptionPathPayoff reward path t.
```

First remove a small analytic ambiguity in the path interface. Since the
total path mass is exactly `t`, monotonicity gives, for `0<=u<v<=1`,

```text
0 <= pi_i(v)-pi_i(u) <= v-u.                         (J1)
```

Thus every singleton coordinate is one-Lipschitz and absolutely continuous.
The same argument applies to every coalition coordinate. Condition A.4 says
that a nonsingleton coordinate has zero lower-right derivative at every
interior time; at every ordinary differentiability point this is its ordinary
derivative. Absolute continuity therefore makes every nonsingleton coordinate
constant, and its value is zero because the total path mass at time zero is
zero. Hence the path is literally singleton-supported, not merely
derivative-singleton-supported.

Sequential perfection gives

```text
gamma_i(t) >= s_i                                    (J2)
```

for every `t<1`. At almost every point at which `pi_i` has positive derivative,
the lower-right derivative in the path definition is positive, so the second
sequential-perfection clause gives the reverse inequality. Consequently the
Stieltjes measure `d pi_i` is carried by

```text
{t : gamma_i(t)=s_i}.                                (J3)
```

This is the measure-support form of continuous-time indifference; no
pointwise converse from a zero derivative is being assumed.

Fix a logarithmic mesh `h>0` and put

```text
t_k       = 1-exp(-k h),
A_{k,i}   = integral_[t_k,t_{k+1}) (1-t)^(-1) d pi_i(t),
p_{k,i}   = 1-exp(-A_{k,i}).                         (J4)
```

Since the singleton measures sum to Lebesgue measure, for every `k`

```text
A_{k,i} >= 0,             sum_i A_{k,i}=h.           (J5)
```

Let the root at date `k` give player `i` Quit marginal `p_{k,i}`. Its joint
Continue probability is exactly `exp(-h)`, by
`exp_neg_sum_eq_prod_exp_neg`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/LogarithmicBlockDiscretization.lean`).
Therefore the root sequence absorbs almost surely, from every suffix.

For comparison inside one block, normalize the path's player-`i` mass by the
entering survival:

```text
mu_{k,i}=(pi_i(t_{k+1})-pi_i(t_k))/(1-t_k).
```

The factor `(1-t)^(-1)` in `(J4)` lies between the two endpoint factors, so

```text
exp(-h) A_{k,i} <= mu_{k,i} <= A_{k,i}.              (J6)
```

The product row's exact unique-`i` mass has the same bounds by
`logarithmicBlockUniqueMass_bounds`. Summing the abstract common-interval
estimate `sum_abs_sub_of_between_same_nonneg` gives singleton `L1` error at
most

```text
h(1-exp(-h))                                          (J7)
```

per conditional block. The row's collision mass is at most `h^2/2` by
`collisionMass_logarithmicBlock_le_sq`. Starting from any date `k`, the
successive block weights are `1,exp(-h),exp(-2h),...`. Thus the complete
discrete tail law and the continuous tail law at `t_k` differ, in full outcome
`L1`, by at most

```text
D(h)=h + h^2/(2(1-exp(-h))).                         (J8)
```

Let `M` bound the absolute value of every coordinate of every terminal reward,
and let `V_k` be the actual payoff vector of the discrete root-sequence suffix
starting at `k`. Almost-sure absorption and `(J8)` give the uniform estimate

```text
||V_k-gamma(t_k)||_infinity <= E(h):=M D(h)          (J9)
```

for every `k`. Notice that the error is uniform arbitrarily far down the
infinite sequence; this is why the logarithmic clock is used.

It remains to check one-stage perfection. Over one log block, removing a
fraction at most `1-exp(-h)` of a bounded terminal distribution changes its
conditional expectation by at most `2M(1-exp(-h))<=2Mh`. Also, in the product
row, the probability that at least one opponent quits is at most
`sum_j p_{k,j}<=h`. Hence, for player `i`,

```text
|QuitPayoff_{k,i}-s_i| <= 2Mh,                       (J10)
|ContinuePayoff_{k,i}-V_{k+1,i}| <= 2Mh.             (J11)
```

If `p_{k,i}>0`, then `A_{k,i}>0`; by `(J3)` there is a point of the block at
which `gamma_i=s_i`. The preceding conditional-expectation modulus, `(J9)`,
and `(J10)`--`(J11)` show that the two pure endpoint payoffs at row `k` differ
by at most

```text
delta(h)=E(h)+6Mh.                                   (J12)
```

Both actions are in support because `0<p_{k,i}<1`, so all four clauses of
`QuittingPlayerRowεPerfect` follow. If `p_{k,i}=0`, `(J2)` replaces the equality
point and gives `QuitPayoff<=ContinuePayoff+E(h)+4Mh`; Continue is the sole
supported action. Thus `(J12)` still implies every clause. Since
`delta(h)->0` as `h->0`, choose `h` with `delta(h)<delta`. Equations
`(J4)`--`(J12)` then give the required completely absorbing, sequentially
`delta`-perfect root sequence. ∎

**Exact source boundary.** The elementary estimates used above are checked,
but the Stieltjes construction `(J4)`, the support statement `(J3)`, and the
identification of `(J9)` with the semantic root-sequence tail are not Lean
declarations. Proposition 4.6 and the payoff-path remark in Section 4 of
Ashkenazi--Golan--Krasikov--Rainer--Solan, *Absorption paths and equilibria in
quitting games* (arXiv v1; Proposition 4.8 in the published numbering), assert
the corresponding weak approximation. The tracked paper transcription
`Literature/AshkenaziGolanKrasikovRainerAndSolan2022.lean` deliberately leaves
that topology and decoder as comments. Proposition 51 is a self-contained
quantitative realization for the continuous case, not a claim that those
paper comments are already formalized.

The global step is separate. The production file
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/ErrorExponentRefutation.lean`
defines the open proposition
`QuittingSequentialPerfectionStationaryAlternative`: a sufficiently accurate
completely absorbing sequentially perfect sequence either generates a global
approximate equilibrium or the game has stationary approximate equilibria at
every accuracy. This is the corrected Solan--Vieille alternative; the stronger
unconditional first disjunct printed as Theorem 3.5 in the 2022 paper is
formally refuted.

**Proposition 52 (proved conditional reduction; no claim that its premise is
available).** Assume `QuittingSequentialPerfectionStationaryAlternative`.
If

```text
ProjectiveQBarMatrixBranch reward,
not IsQuittingZeroSolo reward,
not QuittingStationarilyGeneratedApproximateEquilibria reward,
```

then the quitting game has a behavioral terminal approximate equilibrium at
every positive accuracy.

**Proof.** The branch field `full_projectiveQBar`, the checked continuous-path
theorem, and Proposition 51 give branch S.3 at every row tolerance. Apply the
assumed stationary alternative at a tolerance small enough that its one-sixth
power is below the desired terminal error. If the supplied root-sequence
profile is globally Nash at that error, keep it. Otherwise the alternative
gives `QuittingStationaryεEquilibriumExistence reward`.

The second case contradicts the third displayed hypothesis. Indeed non-zero
solo supplies a player with positive singleton self-payoff `s`. At every
stationary equilibrium error below `s`, zero absorption would mean the
all-Continue profile, which that player defeats by Quit-now. Thus arbitrarily
small stationary witnesses have positive absorption, and Proposition 47
converts them to
`QuittingStationarilyGeneratedApproximateEquilibria reward`. Only the first
case remains. ∎

**Conjecture-facing consequence and exact survivor.** In the hard branch used
by Proposition 43, instant and generated approximate equilibria are already
excluded and non-zero solo supplies its positive-solo hypothesis. Proposition
52 therefore supplies the only additional input Proposition 43 asks for:
behavioral approximate equilibria. Conditional on the corrected stationary
alternative and the already stated finite-orbit package, the entire
`ProjectiveQBarMatrixBranch` is removed. After
`standardQMatrixSide_qbar_or_residual`, the sole matrix regime still requiring
a producer is `ResidualHardClass`.

This is not yet an unconditional reduction. The paper's arXiv-v1 Theorem 4.13
(published Theorem 4.15, with an additional all-Continue exclusion) states a
direct absorption-path/equilibrium equivalence after excluding sure
first-stage equilibria. The first-stage exception does line up with the
checked theorem
`quittingInstantPunishmentεEquilibriumExistence_of_sureQuitter`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/InstantPunishmentEquivalence.lean`),
and non-zero solo excludes all-Continue at every error below a positive solo
payoff by `isεAsymptoticNash_quittingAlwaysContinue_iff`
(`UniformEquilibrium/ProofView/Concepts/Stochastic/Models/Quitting/SimpleBranches.lean`).
However, the paper's displayed proof passes through the same global
sequential-perfection issue whose unconditional form is false. I therefore do
not use Theorem 4.13/4.15 as an established decoder here; Proposition 52 keeps
the corrected stationary-alternative premise explicit.

**Boundary tests.** A one-player negative-solo path `pi(t)=t` is continuously
zero-perfect, and Proposition 51 produces locally perfect absorbing rows, but
Never gains globally. This is exactly the zero-solo branch and proves that
Proposition 51 cannot be strengthened to direct Nash without a global
alternative. The checked two-player local/global counterexample in
`ErrorExponentRefutation.lean` likewise has exact row perfection and unit
global regret, but its game has the all-Continue stationary equilibrium, so it
tests rather than refutes the stationary disjunct. A falsifier for the present
Q-bar route must survive all three filters simultaneously: continuous
zero-perfect path, non-zero solo, and absence of stationary approximate
equilibria.

Next concrete check: independently verify `(J3)` and the geometric tail sum
in `(J8)`, then attack the **restricted** stationary alternative only for the
logarithmic rows `(J4)`. This is strictly weaker than the open universal
`QuittingSequentialPerfectionStationaryAlternative` and is the smallest
remaining assertion needed to remove the projective-Q-bar branch.

### 55. The continuous deleted-clock identity isolates one exceptional owner

The logarithmic decoder has an exact continuum limit which explains why
local sequential perfection is almost, but not quite, global optimality. The
missing term is a single deleted-clock transversality value, not a sum of local
row errors.

**Proposition 53 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; exact continuum
Snell identity).** Under the hypotheses and notation of Proposition 51, pass to
logarithmic time

```text
tau=-log(1-t).
```

There are measurable rates `a_j(tau) in [0,1]`, defined almost everywhere,
such that

```text
sum_j a_j(tau)=1,
gamma(tau)=integral_[tau,infinity)
  exp(-(u-tau)) sum_j a_j(u) reward({j}) du,           (K1)
a_i(tau)>0 -> gamma_i(tau)=s_i almost everywhere.    (K2)
```

For player `i`, define its deleted-clock survival

```text
R_i(T)=exp(-integral_0^T (1-a_i(u)) du).              (K3)
```

Then for every finite `T`,

```text
gamma_i(0)
 = integral_0^T R_i(u) sum_{j!=i} a_j(u) reward({j})_i du
   + R_i(T) gamma_i(T).                              (K4)
```

Consequently every deterministic finite Quit time is weakly unprofitable in
the continuum model. Never has exact gain

```text
Never_i-gamma_i(0) = -lim_{T->infinity} R_i(T)gamma_i(T).  (K5)
```

The limit exists. At most one player can have a positive deleted-clock
survival limit. If player `i` does, then

```text
gamma(T) -> reward({i}),
lim R_i(T)gamma_i(T)=R_i(infinity)s_i,
reward({i})_k >= s_k for every player k.              (K6)
```

Thus the continuum path has positive Never debt if and only if there is a
unique exceptional owner `i` with

```text
R_i(infinity)>0,
s_i<0,
reward({i})_k>=s_k for every k.                       (K7)
```

If that owner is normal, Proposition 48 already gives stationarily generated
approximate equilibria. Therefore in the hard non-generated branch the sole
transversality obstruction is a **negative abnormal owner whose singleton row
weakly dominates every solo payoff**.

**Proof.** Proposition 51 showed that the singleton coordinates are
one-Lipschitz and absolutely continuous. After the logarithmic change of
variables, the measures

```text
(1-t)^(-1)d pi_j(t)
```

have densities `a_j` with respect to `d tau`, and their sum is `d tau`. This
gives the rate simplex and `(K1)`. The measure-support equality `(J3)` gives
`(K2)`.

Differentiate `(K1)` at Lebesgue points. For coordinate `i`,

```text
gamma_i' = gamma_i-sum_j a_j reward({j})_i
         = (1-a_i)gamma_i-sum_{j!=i}a_j reward({j})_i, (K8)
```

where `(K2)` replaces `a_i s_i` by `a_i gamma_i`. Since
`R_i'=-(1-a_i)R_i`, equation `(K8)` yields

```text
(R_i gamma_i)'=-R_i sum_{j!=i}a_j reward({j})_i.
```

Integration proves `(K4)`. Replacing the terminal continuation
`gamma_i(T)` by Quit-now payoff `s_i` can only lower the right side, by
`gamma_i(T)>=s_i`; this proves every finite stopping-time comparison. The
integral term converges because it is an expectation of bounded rewards under
a subprobability law. Hence `R_i(T)gamma_i(T)` has a limit and `(K5)` follows.
Behavioral randomization adds no value beyond these deterministic stopping
times.

If `R_i(infinity)>0`, then

```text
integral_0^infinity sum_{j!=i}a_j(u)du<infinity.      (K9)
```

The opponent mass in the all-clock residual distribution after time `T` is
at most the tail of `(K9)`, so it tends to zero. Formula `(K1)` then gives
`gamma(T)->reward({i})`. Sequential lower bounds pass to the limit, proving
the last inequality in `(K6)`. Two distinct players cannot both satisfy
positive deleted survival: `(K9)` for `i` makes the total `a_j`-mass finite,
while positive survival for `j` would require its complement, and hence the
`a_i`-mass, finite; this contradicts `sum a=1` on an infinite time interval.
Equations `(K5)`--`(K6)` now give `(K7)`. Finally, the row inequalities in
`(K7)` are exactly `(G1)` of Proposition 48, so a normal exceptional owner is
already in the generated branch. ∎

**Relation to checked compilers.** The discrete counterpart of `(K4)` is the
Snell comparison in `QuittingInfinitePathQuitErrorCertificate`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/InfinitePathSupersolution.lean`). Its
consumer requires every player's opponent survival to vanish; Proposition 53
explains the exact continuum failure when that hypothesis is absent.
`divergent_opponentClocks_or_positive_exceptionalSuffix`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/OpponentClockDichotomy.lean`) is the
checked discrete probability split, but its positive-survival branch does not
itself supply a stationary fallback. The new content here is the continuous
path's limit identification `(K6)`, which reduces that branch to one raw
singleton-row configuration.

There is a tempting checked producer for part of this configuration, but its
missing hypothesis is exact and important.
`isQuittingStationaryUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_nonnegative_column`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/LaterLayerAbnormal.lean`)
consumes the nonnegative normalized column supplied by `(K6)` **and** a
distinct blocker `j` with

```text
normalizedSoloMatrix reward i j <= 0.                (K10)
```

If the exceptional owner `i` is abnormal, Simon's proved `lemma3`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) gives the opposite strict inequalities

```text
normalizedSoloMatrix reward i j > 0 for every j!=i. (K11)
```

Thus the named two-scale nonnegative-column producer cannot close the actual
hard exceptional case. Algebraically, the nonnegative owner column makes the
pure point mass at `i` a homogeneous solution of the **full** normalized
matrix, but the projective-Q-bar gate excludes homogeneous solutions only on
the normal-player principal matrix. The abnormal pure solution therefore does
not contradict `ProjectiveQBarMatrixBranch.no_homogeneous`. This is the first
precise point at which the full-matrix continuous path and the normal-core LCP
gate fail to compose.

**Small tests.** For the one-player negative-solo path, `a_i=1`, `R_i=1`, and
`(K5)` gives debt `-s_i` exactly. If at least two players retain infinite rate
mass, then every deleted clock vanishes and all Never debts are zero. If one
owner asymptotically carries all rate but has `s_i>=0`, its surviving Never
atom is harmless. None of these statements assumes that the ideal continuum
law is already a behavioral profile.

**Route correction after this checkpoint.** Constructing the path on the full
player set creates the abnormal exceptional owner in `(K11)`, but it is not
forced by projective Q-bar. Section 56 instead restricts the path construction
to the principal set of production-normal players and then lifts it. The
exceptional owner is then normal and Proposition 48 applies. Thus the old
nonlocal-restart question is superseded on the Q-bar branch; it remains a
valid diagnostic for an arbitrary supplied continuous path.

almost-everywhere support implication, the absolutely convergent deleted-clock
identity, the transversality sign, uniqueness of a positive deleted survival,
and both raw-data endpoint adapters in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_19.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_19.md).
The verdict is valid ordinary mathematics with no objection and no Lean or

51's discrete decoder was outside that review.

### 56. Restricting to normal owners closes the projective-Q-bar branch

Proposition 53's abnormal exceptional owner is an artifact of constructing the
continuous path on the full player set. Projective Q-bar is inherited by every
principal subset, so the path can instead be constructed only on the players
whose production punishment floor is covered by their solo payoff. Simon's
abnormal-player inequality then protects every omitted coordinate.

**Proposition 54 (twice independently reviewed ordinary mathematics;
universal projective-Q-bar producer).** Let the finite player set be nonempty. If

```text
IsProjectiveQBarMatrix (normalizedSoloMatrix reward),                 (L1)
```

then there exists a uniform-equilibrium payoff of the quitting game. More
precisely, either the game is zero-solo, it is stationarily generated, or the
logarithmic product profiles below are unrestricted behavioral terminal
approximate equilibria whose terminal payoffs converge to one fixed vector.

**Step 1: the normal principal set is nonempty off zero solo.** Put

```text
N={i : I | IsQuittingNormalPlayer reward i}.
```

If `IsQuittingZeroSolo reward`, the checked theorem
`quittingGame_isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_zero_of_zeroSolo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/ZeroSoloDisjunct.lean`) finishes.
Otherwise choose `j` with solo payoff `s_j>0`. The checked inequality

```text
quittingPunishmentValue reward j <= max(s_j,0)=s_j
```

is `quittingPunishmentValue_le_max_solo`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Stationary/MinMax.lean`), so `j` belongs to `N`.

Restrict the reward table to `N`: map a nonempty coalition of subtype players
to its ambient image and retain only the coordinates in `N`. Its normalized
singleton matrix is the principal restriction of the ambient normalized
singleton matrix. For every nonempty finite subset of `N`, its ambient image
is a nonempty finite subset of `I`; hence `(L1)` and invariance of projective
LCP solutions under a finite reindexing give

```text
IsProjectiveQBarMatrix (normalizedSoloMatrix reward_N).               (L2)
```

This is a direct unpacking of `IsProjectiveQBarMatrix`, defined in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/MatrixClasses.lean`; no named
subtype-restriction adapter is currently claimed.

Apply `exists_continuous_zeroPerfect_of_projectiveQBar`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/PrincipalQContinuousPath.lean`)
to `(L2)`. Embed its singleton coordinates in the ambient player set and set
every other coalition coordinate to zero. Call the ambient path `P` and its
conditional payoff `gamma`. For an owner in `N`, both sequential-perfection
inequalities are exactly those of the restricted path. If `k` is outside
`N`, then `k` is abnormal. The ordinary source-to-project identification of
the two quitting min--max notions and Simon's proved `lemma3`
(`Literature/Simon2007.lean`) give, for every owner `j` in `N`,

```text
reward({j})_k >= chi_k > s_k.                                         (L3)
```

The residual law of `P` is a probability mixture of these normal singleton
rows. Therefore `gamma_k(t)>s_k` for every `t<1`, while the singleton
derivative of the omitted player is zero. Thus `P` is an ambient continuous
sequentially zero-perfect path. This lift is ordinary mathematics: the
Literature `MinMaxQuit` and production `quittingPunishmentValue` still have no
named checked adapter.

**Step 2: the only harmful transversality branch is generated.** Apply the
logarithmic-time representation of Proposition 53 to `P`. Every omitted
player has rate zero almost everywhere, so its deleted survival is
`exp(-T)` and tends to zero. Hence the unique possible exceptional owner lies
in `N` and is normal. If some player's continuum `Never` deviation is
profitable, `(K7)` supplies a normal exceptional owner `i` whose singleton
column weakly dominates every solo payoff. Proposition 48 then gives
`QuittingStationarilyGeneratedApproximateEquilibria reward`. The checked
theorems
`quittingApproximateEquilibriumExistence_of_stationarilyGenerated` and
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_approximateEquilibriumExistence`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/Existence/StationarilyGeneratedBranch.lean`)
finish this branch.

It remains to treat the complementary case. There every continuum pure Quit
time and `Never` pays player `i` at most `gamma_i(0)`. Notice that positive
deleted survival is allowed here: by `(K5)` it is harmless whenever its solo
payoff is nonnegative.

**Step 3: a global opponent-only law estimate.** Fix `h>0` and use the roots
of Proposition 51. In logarithmic block `k`, write

```text
A_{k,j}=integral_[kh,(k+1)h) a_j(u) du,
p_{k,j}=1-exp(-A_{k,j}),
H^i_k=sum_{j!=i} A_{k,j}=h-A_{k,i}.                                  (L4)
```

When player `i`'s clock is deleted, both the continuum and discrete opponent
survivals at the start of block `k` are exactly

```text
Q^i_k=exp(-sum_{ell<k} H^i_ell)=R_i(kh).                              (L5)
```

Conditional on that survival, the continuum mass of a singleton opponent
`j` first quitting in the block and the discrete product-row mass of unique
opponent `j` both lie in the same interval

```text
[exp(-H^i_k) A_{k,j}, A_{k,j}].                                      (L6)
```

For the continuum law this follows by bounding its within-block deleted
survival. For the product row the exact mass is

```text
exp(-H^i_k)(exp(A_{k,j})-1).
```

Consequently the singleton `L1` discrepancy in the block is at most

```text
H^i_k(1-exp(-H^i_k)).                                                  (L7)
```

The product row's mass on collisions among at least two opponents is at most
`(H^i_k)^2/2`. Since `0<=H^i_k<=h`,

```text
(H^i_k)^2/2
 <= (h exp(h)/2)(1-exp(-H^i_k)).                                     (L8)
```

Now telescope `(L5)`:

```text
sum_k Q^i_k(1-exp(-H^i_k))=1-R_i(infinity)<=1.                       (L9)
```

Equations `(L7)`--`(L9)` show that the complete discrete and continuum
opponent-only outcome laws have `L1` discrepancy at most

```text
L(h)=h+h exp(h)/2.                                                     (L10)
```

This estimate is global over the whole infinite tail; no per-row error is
summed without its deleted-survival weight.

Let `M` bound the absolute value of every terminal reward coordinate. If
player `i` is forced to Quit in discrete block `n`, the earlier opponent law
is covered by `(L10)`. At the forced row, simultaneous opponent quitting has
probability at most `1-exp(-H^i_n)<=h`; replacing the resulting coalition
reward by the continuum solo payoff costs at most `2Mh`. Therefore, uniformly
in `n`,

```text
discretePureQuit_i(n)
 <= continuumPureQuit_i(nh)+F(h)
 <= gamma_i(0)+F(h),

F(h)=M h(3+exp(h)/2).                                                  (L11)
```

For `Never`, the two opponent-only laws have the same survival atom
`R_i(infinity)`, on which terminal payoff is zero, so `(L11)` holds without
the final `2Mh` term. This explicitly covers zero denominators and positive
`Never` atoms.

Proposition 51 gives for the prescribed product profile `sigma_h`

```text
|terminalPayoff_i(sigma_h)-gamma_i(0)| <= E(h),
E(h)=M[h+h^2/(2(1-exp(-h)))] -> 0.                                   (L12)
```

Combining `(L11)`--`(L12)`, every pure-time deviation gains at most
`F(h)+E(h)`. The exact theorem
`sSup_range_quittingTerminalPayoff_update_eq_pureTime`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/BehaviorPureTimeExtremality.lean`)
extends this bound to every unilateral behavioral deviation. Given
`epsilon>0`, choose `h` so that `F(h)+E(h)<epsilon` and `E(h)<epsilon`.
Then `sigma_h` is an unrestricted terminal `epsilon`-Nash profile and its
terminal payoff is `epsilon`-close in every coordinate to the single target
`gamma(0)`. The checked fixed-target consumer
`quittingGame_isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_terminalNash_all_errors_approxTarget`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Terminal/TargetTail/TerminalTargetSemantics.lean`)
makes `gamma(0)` a uniform-equilibrium payoff. This completes all branches. ∎

**Quantifiers, tests, and scope.** The target `gamma(0)` is fixed before
`epsilon`; only `h` and the profile move. The comparison is uniform over every
finite Quit time and `Never`, then covers unrestricted behavior by the exact
pure-time extremality theorem. A one-player negative-solo game tests why the
zero-solo split precedes the normal restriction: its normal set may be empty.
A one-player positive-solo game has `R(infinity)=1`, but `(K5)` makes Never
weakly worse, so the fixed-target branch works. A forced Quit simultaneous
with opponent collisions is precisely the separate `2Mh` term in `(L11)`.
A negative normal exceptional owner does not enter the direct decoder; it is
dispatched by Proposition 48.

Proposition 54 proves no statement about `ResidualHardClass`, arbitrary
continuous paths, or the open universal
`QuittingSequentialPerfectionStationaryAlternative`. It supersedes
Proposition 52 only on the full projective-Q-bar branch. The genuinely new
ordinary-mathematics interfaces are the normal-subtype path lift and the
global opponent-only comparison `(L5)`--`(L11)`. The path theorem, local
logarithmic estimates, pure-time behavioral extremality, generated-branch
consumer, zero-solo consumer, and fixed-target terminal consumer are named
checked declarations, but Proposition 54 as assembled has no `L`, `A`, or
integration seal and must not be exported before two independent
unrestricted-deviation reviews.

**Literature novelty boundary.** The existence conclusion is not a new class
relative to the absorption-path paper. AGKRS, *Absorption paths and equilibria
in quitting games*, arXiv-v1 Theorem 4.13 (published Theorem 4.15) claims that,
apart from a sure first-stage equilibrium, a sequentially zero-perfect
absorption path is equivalent to approximate-equilibrium existence; its
Theorem 5.2 supplies such a path under the principal-Q condition. The project
transcription in
`Literature/AshkenaziGolanKrasikovRainerAndSolan2022.lean` intentionally does
not declare Theorem 4.13 because the displayed proof passes through the same
global sequential-perfection issue whose stronger printed form is false.
Proposition 54 is therefore a proposed rigorous repair and explicit
all-behavior decoder for the projective-Q-bar conclusion, not a claim that the
existence class was absent from the paper.

Next concrete check: independently recompute `(L6)`--`(L10)` for a path with
one positive deleted-survival atom, and falsify the ambient lift at a normal
player whose singleton coordinate has zero rate on a positive-measure set.
The lift needs only the lower sequential inequality there; any hidden upper
condition would invalidate the restriction argument.
