### 57. Only the production-normal principal matrix needs projective Q-bar

The proof of Proposition 54 uses ambient full projective Q-bar exactly once:
to derive `(L2)`. It can therefore begin at `(L2)` instead. Keep this
strengthening separate so the reviewed Proposition 54 remains frozen.

For a reward table, define the production-normal subtype and its singleton
matrix by

```text
N_reward={i : I | IsQuittingNormalPlayer reward i},
M^N(i,j)=normalizedSoloMatrix reward i.1 j.1
         for i,j in N_reward.                                       (M1)
```

This is not the recursively screened `normalCore` or
`normalizedNormalPlayerMatrix` in the current LCP gate.

**Proposition 55 (unreviewed strengthening; production-normal Q-bar
producer).** If

```text
IsProjectiveQBarMatrix M^N,                                         (M2)
```

then the quitting game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff.

**Proof.** Split on `IsQuittingZeroSolo reward`. The zero-solo case is checked.
Off zero solo, `quittingPunishmentValue_le_max_solo` makes `N_reward`
nonempty exactly as in Step 1 of Proposition 54. Define the restricted reward
table on this subtype. Its normalized singleton matrix is `(M1)`, so `(M2)`
feeds `exists_continuous_zeroPerfect_of_projectiveQBar` directly. Every
omitted ambient player is abnormal and is protected by `(L3)`. From that point
on Steps 2--3 of Proposition 54 use only the lifted path, sequential
perfection, and the reward bound; they never inspect an ambient matrix class.
The generated/transversality fork and fixed-target decoder therefore finish
unchanged. ∎

**Counterexample-facing boundary.** Combining Proposition 55 with the checked
zero-solo consumer, every putative counterexample must satisfy

```text
not IsProjectiveQBarMatrix M^N.                                      (M3)
```

Equivalently, some nonempty principal subset consisting entirely of
production-normal players has a nonprojective normalized singleton matrix.
This is additional raw-data structure not present in `ResidualHardClass`,
whose defining negative field concerns the full ambient matrix. Proposition
55 may therefore also consume residual-class tables for which `(M2)` holds.
No explicit residual table separating full failure from `(M2)` has yet been
verified, so strict set-theoretic separation of those two named classes is not
claimed here.

**Source boundary.** Published AGKRS Remark 5.5 already observes abstractly
that a continuous equilibrium on a subset `J` lifts when omitted players get
nonnegative normalized payoffs. The new adapter here is the canonical choice
`J=N_reward`, for which Simon's abnormal-player lemma supplies that omitted-
coordinate inequality, together with Proposition 54's rigorous global
decoder. Thus Proposition 55 sharpens the project-facing matrix hypothesis but
does not claim that the general subset-lift idea is absent from the paper.

Next concrete check: determine whether the sign pattern furnished by Simon's
abnormal-player lemma already forces ambient projective Q-bar from `(M2)`. If
so, Proposition 55 is only a cleaner equivalent interface. If not, produce the
smallest exact reward table with `(M2)` and failed ambient Q-bar, then test
whether it actually lies in `ResidualHardClass`.

### 58. Cross-face LCP escape is algebraic, not yet chronological

This section starts from the exact residual witness rather than another
arbitrary completion. The files and declarations inspected are:

- `pairedSingletonMatrix_standardQ`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonLCP.lean`);
- `pairedSingletonMatrix_not_projectiveQBar`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonResidualHard.lean`);
- `ProjectiveLCPSolution`, `StandardLCPSolution.toProjective`,
  `isProjectiveQMatrix_iff_standard_or_homogeneous`, and
  `IsProjectiveQBarMatrix`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/MatrixClasses.lean`);
- `NonnegativeBoundaryDirection.supported_on_zero` and
  `exists_nonnegativeBoundaryDirection`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/PrincipalQBoundaryDirection.lean`);
- `AbsorptionPathJumpRelation` and
  `IsPlayerSequentiallyPerfectAbsorptionPath`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/ContinuousPath.lean`); and
- `periodTwo_isQuittingBlockCertificate`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonBlockCertificate.lean`).

A narrow search for `principalMatrix`, `ProjectiveLCPSolution`, `jump`, and
`supported_on_zero` found no existing adapter from a cross-face standard-LCP
solution to an absorption-path jump or a chronological block.

Let `M` be the normalized singleton matrix. Let `P` be a proper principal
face and let `q : P -> R` have no standard-LCP solution on `M_P`. Extend `q`
by zero off `P`. If full `M` is standard Q, every full standard solution `z`
has some `z_o>0` with `o` outside `P`: otherwise restriction solves the
prohibited face problem. With `Z=sum_i z_i`, division gives

```text
(M(z/Z))_o >= 0,
z_o>0  -> (M(z/Z))_o=0                         for o outside P,
(M(z/Z))_i >= -q_i/Z,
z_i>0  -> (M(z/Z))_i=-q_i/Z                    for i in P.       (N1)
```

This is Gauss's cross-face calculation. The proof is valid elementary LCP
algebra. The following proposition identifies exactly what it does not yet
provide.

**Proposition 56 (proved ordinary mathematics; cross-face semantic-gap
lemma).** A solution of `(N1)` has none of the following consequences without
additional data.

1. It need not be a continuous zero-perfect direction at the actually reached
   payoff boundary.
2. Its projective normalization need not be the law of one product-row jump.
3. Even if its cemetery and singleton masses are realized exactly by a finite
   one-owner-at-a-time schedule, projective complementarity does not make all
   scheduled rows sequentially zero-perfect at their reached tails.

All three failures occur in the checked paired-singleton matrix

```text
M = [[ 0, 3,-1,-1],
     [ 3, 0,-1,-1],
     [-1,-1, 0, 3],
     [-1,-1, 3, 0]],
P={0,2}, q=(-1,-1), qbar=(-1,0,-1,0),
z=(1/3,2/3,1/3,2/3).                              (N2)
```

**Proof of 1.** The continuous projective-Q construction works at a
nonnegative payoff-gap boundary `g`, whose zero coordinates are precisely the
owners currently indifferent between continuing and quitting alone. Its
control satisfies

```text
weight_i != 0 -> g_i=0,                            (N3)
```

which is the field `NonnegativeBoundaryDirection.supported_on_zero` and is
also forced semantically by the active half of continuous SP.2. By contrast,
the LCP in `(N1)` uses the artificial right-hand side which equals `q` on `P`
and **zero** off `P`; it does not contain the actual positive gaps off the
current zero face. Since `(N1)` forces positive weight off `P`, extending that
weight to a current boundary whose zero set is exactly `P` violates `(N3)`.
Thus the cross-face solution is not a support-compatible continuous germ.

**Proof of 2.** More generally, suppose a projective packet has cemetery mass
`c>0`, singleton masses `a_i`, and zero mass on every coalition of size at
least two. If a product row `p` had exactly this law, then

```text
c=product_i(1-p_i),
a_i=p_i product_{j!=i}(1-p_j).
```

If `a_i,a_j>0` for distinct `i,j`, then `p_i,p_j>0` and, because `c>0`, every
`p_k<1`. Hence the exact coalition `{i,j}` has strictly positive mass

```text
p_i p_j product_{k notin {i,j}}(1-p_k)>0,
```

contradicting the packet law. In `(N2)`, `Z=2`, so
`StandardLCPSolution.toProjective` has

```text
c=1/3,  a=(1/9,2/9,1/9,2/9).                     (N4)
```

Even matching only the singleton-to-cemetery odds forces

```text
p=(1/4,2/5,1/4,2/5),
product_i(1-p_i)=81/400,
sum_i Pr(exactly {i} quits)=162/400,
Pr(at least two quit)=157/400.                     (N5)
```

Thus `(N4)` is very far from a one-row jump; the missing collision rewards
are semantic input, not a second-order normalization detail.

**Proof of 3.** There is a canonical collision-free realization of any
packet `(c,a)`: order its active owners `i_1,...,i_m` and, at phase `k`, let
only `i_k` quit with conditional probability

```text
h_k = a_{i_k}/(c+sum_{ell>=k} a_{i_ell}).           (N6)
```

This realizes each singleton mass `a_i` and final survival `c` exactly. Let
`x` be the payoff after final survival, let `s_i=r({i})_i`, and write
`R^j_i=r({j})_i`. Projective complementarity for every active `i` is exactly

```text
c x_i + sum_j a_j R^j_i = s_i.                     (N7)
```

It pins the payoff of the **whole** packet. At phase `k`, however, mixing by
`i_k` requires its conditional tail after that phase to equal `s_{i_k}`.
For the first scheduled owner, `(N7)` supplies that equality. For the second
owner `j=i_2`, removing the first singleton term gives

```text
tail_after_i2(j)-s_j
  = a_{i_1}(s_j-R^{i_1}_j)/(1-a_{i_1}).             (N8)
```

Therefore the second row is exact only if
`R^{i_1}_j=s_j`. In `(N2)` every off-diagonal entry is nonzero, so `(N8)`
fails for every possible first/second pair. No ordering of the four positive
masses in `(N4)` is sequentially zero-perfect. This proves all three claims.
∎

**Positive boundary and exact quantifiers.** Proposition 56 refutes only the
implications

```text
cross-face LCP -> continuous zero-perfect germ,
cross-face LCP -> one product jump,
cross-face LCP -> one-pass singleton-perfect block.                 (N9)
```

It does not refute an existential recurrent multi-owner block. Indeed the
same matrix `(N2)`, completed by the Solan--Vieille boundary reward, has the
checked two-phase certificate `periodTwo_isQuittingBlockCertificate`: active
pairs `{0,2}` and `{1,3}` alternate, collision rows are used, and the two
distinct continuation values return exactly after the block. Thus the
smallest surviving producer statement is:

```text
For every minimal all-normal nonprojective face of a full standard-Q
normalized singleton matrix, the actual reward table supplies, at every
accuracy, a finite source-matched product block which
  (i) begins at the reached continuation payoff,
 (ii) is rowwise approximately perfect against each reached tail,
(iii) exits the bad face through at least one outside owner, and
 (iv) returns to its initial payoff within an error tending to zero. (SCB)
```

The quantifier over the **actual reward table**, including every
nonsingleton row, is essential. `SCB` is a producer from arbitrary residual
data, not a verifier for a supplied block. It would feed the checked
`IsQuittingBlockCertificate` consumer after compactification; Proposition 56
does not supply it.

**Next exact test.** Hold the singleton matrix `(N2)` fixed and replace the
Solan--Vieille completion by `stationaryCompletionReward`, whose every
collision row is the constant vector `-2`. Determine whether a two-phase
cross-pair block can satisfy the exact Bellman and endpoint-Nash equations
with a returned payoff, or prove an explicit positive lower bound on one of
those defects over the compact two-phase parameter box. This paired-completion
comparison changes only the semantic data absent from `(N1)`, so either answer
directly tests `SCB` rather than another matrix class.

#### 58.1 The same singleton packet can lose its alternating-pair block

The proposed test has an exact negative answer for interior hazards. This is
not a game counterexample: `stationaryCompletionReward` has a pure terminal
equilibrium. It is a controlled comparison showing that the successful
period-two repair is not determined by the standard-Q singleton matrix.

At the odd phase let only players `0,2` have Quit hazards `u,v`; at the even
phase let only `1,3` have hazards `a,b`. Assume

```text
0<u,v,a,b<1.                                          (N10)
```

Let `x` and `y` be the odd and even phase values. Every collision pays every
player `-2`. If all four active owners are indifferent, their coordinates and
the relevant tails are forced to be

```text
x_0=-2v,       y_0=-v/(1-v),
x_2=-2u,       y_2=-u/(1-u),
y_1=-2b,       x_1=-b/(1-b),
y_3=-2a,       x_3=-a/(1-a).                         (N11)
```

Writing `c=(1-u)(1-v)` and `d=(1-a)(1-b)`, the remaining Bellman equations
are therefore exactly

```text
b/(1-b)=2bc-3u+v+4uv,
a/(1-a)=2ac+u-3v+4uv,
v/(1-v)=2vd-3a+b+4ab,
u/(1-u)=2ud+a-3b+4ab.                                (N12)
```

The inactive players' endpoint-Nash inequalities are

```text
a/(1-a), b/(1-b) <= 2(1-c),
u/(1-u), v/(1-v) <= 2(1-d).                          (N13)
```

Indeed an inactive player who deviates to Quit gets `-2` exactly when at
least one member of the active pair quits, and gets its solo payoff zero
otherwise.

**Proposition 57 (proved ordinary mathematics; exact two-phase completion
separation).** Under `(N10)`, equations `(N12)` and inequalities `(N13)` have
no solution. Consequently `stationaryCompletionReward` has no exact returned
two-phase block whose active sets are `{0,2}` and `{1,3}` and in which every
listed owner mixes in the interior. The Solan--Vieille completion of the same
singleton matrix does have such a block, by
`periodTwo_isQuittingBlockCertificate`.

**Proof.** Put `phi(t)=t/(1-t)` and let
`T=max{u,v,a,b}`. If, for example, `T=u`, then `(N13)` gives

```text
phi(T) <= 2(1-d) <= 2(2T-T^2).
```

The same argument applies whichever hazard is maximal. Hence

```text
1/(1-T) <= 4-2T,
T <= (3-sqrt(3))/2 < 3/4.                            (N14)
```

Use the equation in `(N12)` belonging to the maximal hazard. Its form is

```text
phi(T)=2TD+x-y(3-4x),                                (N15)
```

where `D` is the other phase's joint survival and `0<x,y<=T`. By `(N14)`,
`3-4x>0`, so `(N15)` implies

```text
T/(1-T) < 2TD+T,
T/(1-T) < 2D.                                        (N16)
```

The corresponding inactive inequality gives `T/(1-T)<=2(1-D)`. Thus
`T/(1-T)<2 min(D,1-D)<=1`, and therefore

```text
T<1/2.                                               (N17)
```

Now set

```text
g_c(t)=phi(t)-2ct,  g_d(t)=phi(t)-2dt.
```

Subtracting the first two and the last two equations in `(N12)` gives

```text
g_c(b)-g_c(a)=4(v-u),
g_d(v)-g_d(u)=4(b-a).                                (N18)
```

On `(0,1/2)`, for either `e=c,d`,

```text
-1 < g_e'(t)=1/(1-t)^2-2e < 4.                      (N19)
```

If `b-a` and `v-u` have the same nonzero sign, the upper bound in `(N19)`
and the mean-value theorem applied to both equations in `(N18)` give
simultaneously

```text
|v-u|<|b-a|  and  |b-a|<|v-u|,
```

a contradiction. If their signs are opposite, the lower bound in `(N19)`
gives

```text
4|v-u|<|b-a|  and  4|b-a|<|v-u|,
```

again a contradiction. If one difference is zero, `(N18)` makes the other
zero. Hence

```text
a=b=r,  u=v=s.                                       (N20)
```

After `(N20)`, the first and third equations in `(N12)` become

```text
r/(1-r)=2r(1-s)^2-2s+4s^2,
s/(1-s)=2s(1-r)^2-2r+4r^2.                          (N21)
```

Let `P(r,s)` be the first equation after multiplying by `1-r` and moving all
terms to the left. Direct expansion gives

```text
P(r,s)-P(s,r)
  =3(r-s)(2r+2s-2rs-1).                             (N22)
```

Thus either `r=s` or `r+s-rs=1/2`. In the first case `(N21)` reduces to

```text
1=2r^2(1-r),
```

impossible because `r^2(1-r)<=4/27`. In the second case

```text
s=(1-2r)/(2(1-r)).
```

Substitution in the first equation, with denominators cleared, gives

```text
[2(1-r)]^2 P(r,s)=6r(1-r)(1-2r)>0
```

by `(N10)` and `(N17)`, contradicting `P(r,s)=0`. This proves the claim. ∎

**Exact scope and surviving obligation.** Proposition 57 excludes one
specific two-phase support pattern with four interior hazards. It excludes no
boundary block, no longer block, and no approximate block whose hazards tend
to zero. In fact the stationary completion's pure terminal equilibrium makes
it unsuitable as a counterexample candidate. What it proves is the sharp
data-dependence statement

```text
same normalized singleton matrix and same cross-face LCP packet
does not imply the same recurrent product-block solvability.              (N23)
```

Therefore any universal `SCB` theorem must use the actual nonsingleton rows
or dispatch to another solved branch; standard-Q cross-support alone is only
a diagnostic screen.

**Next concrete question.** Add the hard-branch exclusions to `SCB` and ask
for a quantitative alternative: either the actual table has an instant or
stationarily generated equilibrium, or some finite cross-face block has
endpoint/Bellman defect `o(A)`, where `A` is its total absorption. The relative
error is essential—absolute defect can vanish trivially with all hazards.
Test this normalized alternative first on the two paired completions and on
the reviewed full-core deadlock joint-phase completion in Proposition 20.

#### 58.2 A raw-gap no-go survives vanishing hazards

For the stationary completion, Proposition 57 can be sharpened near the
all-Continue boundary. This directly tests the normalized formulation rather
than only exact roots.

For interior hazards `(u,v,a,b)`, put

```text
A=max{u,v,a,b}.
```

Let `beta` be the maximum absolute Bellman-recursion residual over both phase
values and all four coordinates. Let `eta` be the maximum absolute **raw**
Quit-minus-Continue difference of the four active owners. This is stronger
than the checked standard endpoint-Nash error: strict interiority alone gives
no hazard-independent comparison between them.

Define the four reduced residuals

```text
F_1 = b/(1-b)-2bc+3u-v-4uv,
F_2 = a/(1-a)-2ac-u+3v-4uv,
F_3 = v/(1-v)-2vd+3a-b-4ab,
F_4 = u/(1-u)-2ud-a+3b-4ab,                         (N24)
```

where again `c=(1-u)(1-v)` and `d=(1-a)(1-b)`.

**Proposition 58 (proved ordinary mathematics; local raw-gap two-phase
coercivity).** For `stationaryCompletionReward`, if

```text
0<u,v,a,b and A<=1/40,
```

then every two-phase alternating-pair candidate satisfies

```text
2 beta + 3 eta >= A/4,
max(beta,eta) >= A/20.                               (N25)
```

In particular there is no sequence of such blocks with positive absorption,
hazards tending to zero, and both Bellman defect and raw active endpoint gap
`o(A)`.

**Proof.** Active endpoint defect at the odd row gives, for example,

```text
|[-2v]-[-v+(1-v)y_0]|<=eta,
```

and hence

```text
|y_0+v/(1-v)|<=eta/(1-v)<=2eta.                      (N26)
```

The odd current successor in coordinate `0` is a mixture of those two pure
endpoint values, so Bellman defect gives

```text
|x_0+2v|<=beta+eta.                                  (N27)
```

The same calculation applies to all four active owners. Insert these eight
estimates into the four nonactive-coordinate Bellman equations. For example,

```text
x_1 = c y_1 + 3u-v-4uv + error,
```

whereas even-row activity gives

```text
|x_1+b/(1-b)|<=2eta,  |y_1+2b|<=beta+eta.
```

Therefore

```text
|F_k|<=2beta+3eta                                    (N28)
```

for every `k`.

The linearization of `(N24)` at zero is

```text
L(u,v,a,b)=
 (3u-v-b,
  -u+3v-a,
  -v+3a-b,
  -u-a+3b).                                          (N29)
```

The symmetric matrix of `L` is `3I` minus the adjacency matrix of the
four-cycle. Its eigenvalues are exactly `1,3,3,5`; hence

```text
||Lh||_2 >= ||h||_2 >= A.                            (N30)
```

For every coordinate, the nonlinear remainder is a permutation of

```text
[t/(1-t)-t] + 2t(1-c) - 4uv.
```

When `A<=1/2`, its absolute value is at most

```text
2A^2+4A^2+4A^2=10A^2.
```

Thus

```text
||F||_2 >= A-20A^2 >= A/2                           (N31)
```

when `A<=1/40`. Since a vector in `R^4` has
`max_k |F_k|>=||F||_2/2`, `(N28)`--`(N31)` give the first inequality in
`(N25)`. The second follows from
`2beta+3eta<=5 max(beta,eta)`. ∎

**Corrected interpretation.** The two completions separate at the raw support-
indifference scale. The Solan--Vieille collision rows support an exact positive
alternating block. The all-`-2` completion has a fixed relative raw defect on
that support even as all hazards vanish. But this is not yet a semantic
endpoint-Nash lower bound: if the large raw gap is negative, the checked
predicate charges it only after multiplication by the owner's Quit hazard,
and the regret may be `O(A^2)`. Since the latter game also has a pure generated
solution, Proposition 58 remains a local diagnostic, not a hard-branch no-go.

**Next concrete question.** Formulate the finite-support alternative with a
uniform integer horizon:

```text
generated/instant branch, or
there exist m<=m(I), absorption A>0, and an m-phase source-matched
cross-face block with standard endpoint-Nash and Bellman defects
<=epsilon A.                                          (N32)
```

Determine whether compactness plus full standard Q can bound `m(I)`, or
whether the checked paired/deadlock examples force support-dependent horizons.
Without a uniform horizon or a separate recurrence compactness theorem,
`(N32)` is not yet a producer. Proposition 58 does not refute `(N32)` because
its `eta` is the unweighted raw gap.

#### 58.3 Raw-perfect vanishing product phases require collision complementarity

Proposition 58 is an instance of a general asymmetry: collision coalitions
have probability `O(alpha^2)` on path when every hazard is `O(alpha)`, but
they have probability `O(alpha)` after one player is forced to Quit. Thus a
singleton LCP controls prescribed payoff motion but not the first-order active
deviation equations.

Let `lambda` be a probability vector on the players and, for
`0<alpha<=1`, let the product root have hazards

```text
p_i=alpha lambda_i.                                   (N33)
```

Write `s_i=r({i})_i`, and suppose `M` bounds the absolute value of every
terminal reward coordinate. Define the collision tangent

```text
C_i(lambda)=sum_{j!=i} lambda_j [r({i,j})_i-s_i].     (N34)
```

**Proposition 59 (proved ordinary mathematics; universal raw-gap
collision-tangent necessity).** Let `x` be a proposed current payoff and `t`
a tail payoff for
the root `(N33)`. Assume every active coordinate is pinned,

```text
lambda_i>0 -> x_i=s_i.                                (N35)
```

Let

```text
beta=max_i |x_i-T_i(t,p)|,
eta=max_{i:lambda_i>0} |Quit_i(t,p)-Continue_i(t,p)|. (N36)
```

Then, for every `i` with `lambda_i>0`,

```text
alpha |C_i(lambda)| <= beta+eta+3M alpha^2.           (N37)
```

Consequently any family with `beta+eta=o(alpha)` must satisfy

```text
lambda_i>0 -> C_i(lambda)=0.                          (N38)
```

**Proof.** Conditional on player `i` being forced to Quit, no opponent quits
with the zeroth-order payoff `s_i`. Exactly opponent `j` quits with probability

```text
alpha lambda_j product_{k notin {i,j}}(1-alpha lambda_k).
```

Its difference from `alpha lambda_j` is at most
`alpha^2 lambda_j`. Since both the joint reward and `s_i` have absolute value
at most `M`, replacing all exact-one-opponent masses by their first-order
values costs at most `2M alpha^2`. The probability that at least two opponents
Quit is at most the expected number of opponent pairs,

```text
sum_{j<k} p_j p_k <= alpha^2/2,
```

so all higher coalitions cost at most another `M alpha^2`. Therefore

```text
|Quit_i(t,p)-s_i-alpha C_i(lambda)|<=3M alpha^2.       (N39)
```

The root successor is the own-action mixture of `Quit_i` and `Continue_i`.
Hence

```text
|Quit_i-T_i(t,p)|
  =(1-p_i)|Quit_i-Continue_i|<=eta.
```

By `(N35)` and the Bellman defect,

```text
|Quit_i-s_i|<=eta+beta.
```

Combine this with `(N39)` to get `(N37)`, then divide by `alpha` and let
`alpha` vanish to obtain `(N38)`. ∎

**Sharp small tests.** With two active owners, `(N38)` says exactly

```text
r({i,j})_i=s_i and r({i,j})_j=s_j.                   (N40)
```

A single active owner satisfies `(N38)` vacuously. For
`stationaryCompletionReward`, every collision payoff is `-2` and every solo
baseline is `0`, so no direction with two positive coordinates is
collision-complementary. For the Solan--Vieille completion, every member of a
two-player quitting coalition receives its solo baseline `1`; hence every
rate vector satisfies `(N38)`. This is precisely the first-order information
which the common paired singleton matrix cannot distinguish.

**Exact source-overlap correction.** The quantity in `(N34)` is already the
named
`quittingActiveMixingPairJoinEffect` summed against the packet mass. The
checked theorem
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.activePairCompatibilityResidual_eq_sum_pairJoinEffect`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Analytic/ChargeTangent/MixingCompatibility.lean`)
identifies the same active row, and
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.activeMixingCompatibility_or_signedPivot` gives
the finite algebraic alternative: compatible active rows, a positive supported
pair-join pivot, or a negative supported pivot. In the two-owner zero-pivot
case, `isQuittingNashBellmanEdge_twoOwner_of_pairJoin_zero`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Analytic/ChargeTangent/TwoOwner/ExactRoot.lean`)
already integrates the tangent to an exact Nash--Bellman edge under the
explicit inactive, floor, and box gates.

Thus Proposition 59 is not a new tangent condition or a new exact-edge class.
Its incremental content is the direct quantitative raw product-row decoder
`(N37)`: an actual small product root with pinned active current values cannot
have Bellman and **raw endpoint-gap** error small relative to absorption unless
the checked tangent residual vanishes. It is not a decoder from the checked
probability-weighted endpoint-Nash predicate. No Lean seal is claimed.

**Raw-perfect producer boundary.** A one-phase vanishing implementation whose
raw active gaps are `o(alpha)` requires simultaneous feasibility of two
systems:

```text
singleton LCP complementarity for lambda,
lambda_i C_i(lambda)=0 for every i.                  (N41)
```

The second system is quadratic complementarity in the actual pair-collision
rows. Its algebra and two-owner exact-edge consumer are checked as just
listed, but source-matched tail motion, inactive join inequalities, floor
admissibility in higher support, and recurrence remain. For standard
approximate endpoint Nash, Proposition 60 below shows that `(N41)` weakens to
one-sided nonpositivity. If that one-sided condition fails, the checked
positive-pivot theorem supplies a sign witness but no strategy producer; a
conjecture-closing theorem must turn it into an instant/generated mechanism or
use a genuinely nonvanishing multi-phase block.

**Superseded exact alternative.** For exact or raw-perfect rows, the natural
alternatives remain

```text
(a) a collision-complementary direction satisfying (N41);
(b) an actual-table instant/generated certificate forced by the sign of C;
(c) a finite nonvanishing product block with a returned payoff.     (N42)
```

For semantic approximate rows, this is too strong: a negative tangent is
first-order unfavorable to Quit and costs only second order when the prescribed
hazard is small. The next section gives the corrected alternative.

#### 58.4 Probability-weighted endpoint Nash gives a one-sided tangent law

The exact predicate is asymmetric. Put

```text
D_i=Quit_i(t,p)-Continue_i(t,p),
p_i=alpha lambda_i,
T_i=p_i Quit_i+(1-p_i)Continue_i.
```

Then `IsεQuittingRootEndpointNash` is precisely

```text
(1-p_i)D_i<=epsilon,       -epsilon<=p_iD_i.          (N43)
```

The first inequality sees a profitable Quit deviation at full first order;
the second sees a profitable Continue deviation only through the probability
with which the prescribed row mistakenly Quits.

**Proposition 60 (proved ordinary mathematics; one-sided semantic collision
tangent).** In the setting `(N33)`--`(N35)`, suppose

```text
|x_i-T_i(t,p)|<=beta
```

and the root satisfies `IsεQuittingRootEndpointNash` with `epsilon>=0`.
Then every `i` with `lambda_i>0` satisfies

```text
alpha C_i(lambda) <= epsilon+beta+3M alpha^2.          (N44)
```

The opposite side has the necessarily weaker bound

```text
p_i alpha C_i(lambda)
  >= -(1-p_i)epsilon-p_i beta-3M p_i alpha^2.          (N45)
```

Consequently, for a fixed direction `lambda`, any family with
`epsilon+beta=o(alpha)` satisfies

```text
lambda_i>0 -> C_i(lambda)<=0.                          (N46)
```

If in addition `epsilon=o(alpha^2)` and `beta=o(alpha)`, then `(N45)` gives
the reverse inequality on every fixed positive coordinate of `lambda`, and
hence equality `(N38)`.

**Proof.** The forced-Quit expansion `(N39)` is independent of the endpoint
notion. Also

```text
Quit_i-T_i=(1-p_i)D_i.
```

By the first half of `(N43)` and the pinning `x_i=s_i`,

```text
Quit_i-s_i=(Quit_i-T_i)+(T_i-x_i)<=epsilon+beta.
```

Combining this with the lower side of `(N39)` proves `(N44)`. For the other
side, multiply the same identity by `p_i`. The second half of `(N43)` gives

```text
p_i(Quit_i-T_i)=p_i(1-p_i)D_i>=-(1-p_i)epsilon.
```

Since `p_i(T_i-x_i)>=-p_i beta`, the upper side of the absolute error in
`(N39)` yields `(N45)`. Divide `(N44)` by `alpha` for `(N46)`. When
`lambda_i>0` is fixed, divide `(N45)` by
`p_i alpha=alpha^2 lambda_i`; the additional rates make its right side tend
to zero, proving the reverse inequality. ∎

**Sharpness and exact correction to Propositions 58--59.** Algebraically, take
`D_i=-kappa alpha` and `p_i=alpha lambda_i`. The raw active gap is first order,
but the only profitable endpoint deviation is Continue and its regret is

```text
-p_iD_i=kappa lambda_i alpha^2.
```

Thus `epsilon=o(alpha)` cannot force `D_i/alpha` to vanish. This is exactly
the mechanism missed by the earlier sentence `eta<=epsilon`. Proposition 58
therefore remains a raw-gap obstruction, and Proposition 59 remains the
stronger raw-perfect tangent theorem. `CODEX_GAUSS` independently validated
their displayed algebra and identified this scope correction in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_22.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_22.md).
`CODEX_CEDAR` then independently rechecked the corrected Section 58 and found
Propositions 56--60 valid ordinary mathematics in
[`../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_CEDAR__ROUND_16.md`](../feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_CEDAR__ROUND_16.md).

**Source audit.** A narrow search of
`MixingCompatibility.lean`, the charge-tangent subtree, and
`SuccessorCertificate.lean` found the checked exact residual identity, its
positive/negative signed-pivot alternative, and the probability-weighted
endpoint definition, but no one-sided approximate tangent adapter `(N44)`.
This is ordinary mathematics and has no Lean seal.

**Corrected conjecture-facing fork.** A vanishing semantic product phase now
has the first-order alternatives

```text
(a) C_i(lambda)<=0 for every supported i;
(b) some supported C_i(lambda)>0, which must be dispatched or repaired;
(c) hazards do not vanish, so compactness seeks an exact positive block.  (N47)
```

The checked `activeMixingCompatibility_or_signedPivot` refines (a) into exact
compatibility versus a negative pivot, but only the positive pivot contradicts
`o(alpha)` endpoint Nash directly. The next universal obligation is therefore
not to force `(N41)`: it is to compile a collision-**subcompatible** direction
with `C<=0` into a source-matched recurrent block, or prove that accumulated
negative pivots force a different solved mechanism. A new bounded-horizon
compactness argument must use the probability-weighted signs; raw coercivity
cannot substitute for it.

#### 58.5 A returned-loop telescope removes the vanishing-total-hazard regime

The preceding one-row asymmetry is real, but it is not the final returned-block
boundary. Gauss Section 35, Propositions 46--47 prove the following universal
ordinary-mathematics theorem. For a finite returned sequence of product rows,
let

```text
S=sum_(phases k, players i) p_(k,i)
```

be the total hazard, `B` the sum of absolute Bellman residuals, and `E` the
sum of the two positive probability-weighted endpoint regrets from `(N43)`.
For a uniformly bounded family of returned blocks,

```text
S_n->0,       B_n/S_n->0,       E_n/S_n->0           (N48)
```

imply that the normalized singleton matrix has a homogeneous simplex LCP
solution.

I independently checked the theorem in
[`../feedback/CODEX_GAUSS__CURL_FREE_TOGGLE_POTENTIAL__BY_CODEX_NOETHER__ROUND_11.md`](../feedback/CODEX_GAUSS__CURL_FREE_TOGGLE_POTENTIAL__BY_CODEX_NOETHER__ROUND_11.md).
The decisive points are:

1. total phase-value variation is `O(S_n)+B_n`, so every moving tail converges
   uniformly to one payoff `w` even when the finite horizon varies;
2. the first endpoint sign gives `w_i>=s_i`, while the cumulative cheap
   Continue regrets force `w_i=s_i` on the limiting cumulative owner support;
3. the product-row remainder is `O(q_k^2)` at phase hazard `q_k`, and
   `sum_k q_k^2<=S_n^2`; and
4. summing Bellman motion around the returned block yields
   `w=sum_i mu_i r({i})`, hence the homogeneous LCP conditions.

The closest checked one-row result is
`singletonLCPFeasible_of_stationaryEndpointNash_tangent`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Stationary/ApproximabilityCompactification.lean`).
No returned-block version was found in the narrow source search. The theorem
is not Lean-checked and supplies no path producer.

**Gate qualification.** `ResidualHardClass.no_homogeneous`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/Gate.lean`) excludes a
homogeneous solution for `normalizedNormalPlayerMatrix reward`, not for the
full normalized singleton matrix produced by `(N48)`. The direct contradiction
therefore applies when all Quit support remains in the recursive normal core,
after restricting the reward table and payoff coordinates to that subtype.
The restriction has normalized singleton matrix equal to the corresponding
principal matrix by literal singleton expansion; ambient product laws with
zero outside hazards agree on the retained coordinates. No named recursive-
normal reward adapter was found (the checked
`normalizedSoloMatrix_quittingPunishmentNormalReward` concerns the distinct
production-punishment-normal subtype).

For ambient blocks, Proposition 62 below shows that the only homogeneous
escape from the normal-core gate is a pure vertex outside the first normal
layer. Thus a fixed-horizon `SCB` family cannot shrink to the all-Continue
boundary unless its cumulative hazard concentrates on that exceptional owner.
After dispatching the vertex escape, positive-regime compactness must seek an
exact returned block; if the horizon diverges, total hazard or payoff motion
must remain nonvanishing. The exact paired period-two certificate realizes the
first survivor, while the stationary completion dispatches to its pure
generated solution.

**Next concrete universal obligation.** Starting from a minimal
nonprojective all-normal face and its ambient standard-Q cross-support packet,
prove one of

```text
(a) an exact positive-absorption, source-matched returned block satisfying
    endpoint Nash, Bellman recursion, floors, and the fixed-target consumer;
(b) an unbounded finite chronology with nonvanishing accumulated hazard whose
    payoff projections enter a checked charged-return/atom-chronology consumer;
(c) a stationarily generated or instant dispatch forced by a signed pivot;
(d) a dispatch for the pure first-layer-abnormal homogeneous vertex.       (N49)
```

The kill criterion is now exact: if the actual nonsingleton rows admit a hard
table on which (a)--(d) all fail with a uniform positive semantic gap, abandon
`SCB`. Further fixed-support algebra which does not address `(N49)` is no
longer conjecture-facing.

#### 58.6 Nonpositive tangent residuals compile to quadratic-error product rows

There is a positive local statement behind the one-sided law. Let `packet` be
a `QuittingChargeTangentPacket` with boundary `b`, mass `mu`, and tangent `z`.
Fix `scale>0` and put

```text
a_i=scale*mu_i,
u_i=-scale*z_i,
h_i(t)=t*a_i,
y_i(t)=b_i+t*u_i.                                    (N50)
```

Thus `h(t)` is the literal product hazard row and `y(t)` is its proposed tail;
the current payoff remains `b`. Write

```text
R_i=quittingActivePairCompatibilityResidual packet i.
```

Assume the **tight-coordinate subcompatibility condition**

```text
b_i=r({i})_i -> R_i<=0.                              (N51)
```

For active owners this is exactly `C_i(mu)<=0`. It also covers an inactive
owner whose solo constraint is tight, which is necessary because such an
owner has full-weight Quit deviation even when its prescribed hazard is zero.

**Proposition 61 (proved ordinary mathematics; quadratic-error tangent-row
compiler).** Under `(N51)`, there exist `t_0>0` and `K>=0` such that for every
`0<t<=t_0`:

1. `0<=h_i(t)<1` for every player;
2. the product row has Bellman error at most `K t^2`,
   ```text
   |T_i(y(t),h(t))-b_i|<=K t^2;
   ```
3. it satisfies the standard probability-weighted endpoint inequalities with
   error `K t^2`,
   ```text
   (1-h_i(t))D_i(y(t),h(t))<=K t^2,
   -K t^2<=h_i(t)D_i(y(t),h(t));                     (N52)
   ```
4. if `q(t)=1-product_i(1-h_i(t))` is row absorption, then
   ```text
   |q(t)-scale*t|<=K t^2.                            (N53)
   ```

Hence both semantic errors are `O(q(t)^2)=o(q(t))`, while the reached tail
moves by the first-order amount

```text
y(t)-b=-scale*t*z.                                   (N54)
```

**Proof.** Use the exact first-blow-up coordinates in
`RegularArcLift.lean`. The checked identity
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.bellmanFirstOrderResidual_eq_zero` says that the
blown-up Bellman residual `B_i(t)` at `(a,u)` has `B_i(0)=0`. Since it is a
finite polynomial in `t`, after decreasing `t_0` there is a common finite
constant `L` with

```text
|B_i(t)|<=L*t
```

for every coordinate. The exact factorization
`t_mul_quittingBellmanBlowupResidual`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Analytic/ChargeTangent/RegularArcLift.lean`)
identifies `t B_i(t)` with `T_i(y(t),h(t))-b_i`; this gives item 2.

Fix a singleton-tight coordinate. The exact mixing factorization
`t_mul_quittingMixingBlowupResidual` identifies its endpoint difference with

```text
D_i(y(t),h(t))=t*M_i(t),
```

where the checked identities
`quittingMixingBlowupResidual_zero` and
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.mixingFirstOrderResidual_eq_scale_mul_activePairCompatibilityResidual`
give

```text
M_i(0)=scale*R_i<=0.                                 (N55)
```

Polynomial local boundedness therefore gives, uniformly over the finite
player set,

```text
M_i(t)<=L*t,          |M_i(t)|<=L.
```

The profitable-Quit side of `(N52)` is then at most `L t^2`. For the
profitable-Continue side, `h_i(t)<=t*max_j a_j`, so

```text
-h_i(t)D_i(t)<=h_i(t)|D_i(t)|<=constant*t^2.
```

If a coordinate is not singleton-tight, then `b_i>r({i})_i`. It cannot have
positive packet mass by `positive_mass_pins_boundary`; nonnegativity therefore
gives `mu_i=0`, hence `h_i(t)=0`. Its endpoint difference is strictly negative
at `t=0` and remains nonpositive after decreasing `t_0`, so both endpoint
inequalities hold with zero error. This proves item 3.

Finally, `q(t)` is a finite polynomial with `q(0)=0` and

```text
q'(0)=sum_i a_i=scale*sum_i mu_i=scale.
```

A common quadratic Taylor bound proves `(N53)`. Hazard positivity and the
strict upper bound follow from `mu_i>=0`, finiteness, and one final reduction
of `t_0`. Absorb all finite constants into `K`. ∎

**Exact scope and source overlap.** This is a local approximate row, not an
exact Nash--Bellman edge, floor-admissible relation, or returned block. The
regular-arc theorems in `RegularArcLift.lean` construct exact roots only from
full residual zero plus analytic transversality. Proposition 61 instead uses
the standard endpoint asymmetry and requires only the one-sided sign `(N51)`.
A narrow search of the charge-tangent subtree found the exact factorizations,
the exact zero-residual arc interface, and two-owner exact gates, but no
quadratic-error subcompatible-row compiler.

**Why this changes the universal obligation.** Repeating `O(1/t)` such rows
would accumulate order-one hazard and payoff motion but only `O(t)` total
Bellman/endpoint error. That scaling is precisely the survivor left by the
returned-loop telescope `(N48)`. The missing step is no longer local
Nashification: it is **moving-source reprojection**. After taking one step to
`y(t)`, one must produce a new charge-tangent packet based at that actually
reached payoff, preserve `(N51)` and punishment floors, and eventually enter a
checked charged-return or atom-chronology consumer. A frozen packet cannot be
iterated because `(N54)` changes its boundary.

**Next concrete check.** Determine whether the tail-derived packet extractor
in `QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness.exists_chargeTangentData_of_windows`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/ChargeTangentPacket.lean`)
forces `(N51)` after the positive-pivot/generated branches are excluded, and
whether its next window begins at the displaced payoff `(N54)`. A positive
answer supplies the local-to-global reprojection needed by `(N49b)`; a
singleton-tight positive residual is the exact obstruction.

#### 58.7 Full homogeneous limits reduce to the normal core or one abnormal vertex

The gate mismatch in Section 58.5 admits a sharp algebraic reduction. Let `M`
be a finite zero-diagonal matrix and let `mu` be a homogeneous simplex witness:

```text
mu_i>=0,     sum_i mu_i=1,
(M mu)_i>=0,     mu_i(M mu)_i=0.                    (N56)
```

**Proposition 62 (proved ordinary mathematics; full-to-normal homogeneous
support reduction).** If at least two coordinates of `mu` are positive, then
every positive coordinate belongs to `normalCore M`, and restriction of `mu`
to that subtype is a homogeneous simplex solution of `normalPlayerMatrix M`.
Consequently, if

```text
not HasHomogeneousSimplexSolution (normalPlayerMatrix M),               (N57)
```

then every full homogeneous witness is a vertex `mu=e_j`, with
`j` outside `normalCore M`.

For `M=normalizedSoloMatrix reward`, add the hypothesis that the game has no
uniform-equilibrium payoff. Then the exceptional vertex satisfies the exact
two-sided first-layer sign pattern

```text
M_i,j>=0 for every i,
M_j,k>0 for every k!=j.                              (N58)
```

In particular `j` is absent already from `normalLayer M 1`.

**Proof.** Let `S={i:mu_i>0}` and suppose `S` has at least two elements. Fix
`i in S`. Complementarity gives `(M mu)_i=0`, while the diagonal term is zero.
If `M_i,j>0` for every `j in S\{i}`, then

```text
(M mu)_i=sum_(j in S\{i}) mu_j M_i,j>0,
```

a contradiction. Hence there is a distinct `j in S` with `M_i,j<=0`.

Now induct on the normal layers. Every member of `S` lies in layer zero. If
`S` lies in layer `n`, the preceding distinct `j` witnesses that each
`i in S` lies in layer `n+1`. Thus `S` lies in every layer, hence in
`normalCore M`. Restricting `mu` preserves total mass because it vanishes off
`S`; on a core row, the restricted principal residual is the same full sum.
Residual nonnegativity and complementarity therefore restrict verbatim. This
proves the first claim.

Under `(N57)`, a full witness cannot have two positive coordinates, so the
simplex equation makes it a vertex. If its owner `j` lay in the core, the same
restriction would contradict `(N57)`, hence `j` lies outside. For a vertex,
`(M mu)_i=M_i,j`, proving the nonnegative column in `(N58)`. If some distinct
`k` also satisfied `M_j,k<=0`, the checked theorem
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_nonnegative_column`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/LaterLayerAbnormal.lean`)
would produce a uniform-equilibrium payoff with owner `j` and blocker `k`.
Under the stated counterexample hypothesis this is impossible, so every such
entry is strictly positive. ∎

**Corrected corollary of the returned telescope.** On a residual-hard game,
`(N48)` has only two possibilities:

```text
(i) the limiting cumulative hazard has at least two owners, contradicting
    ResidualHardClass.no_homogeneous by Proposition 62; or
(ii) it converges to e_j for one owner outside the recursive normal core.
```

Under a terminal counterexample hypothesis, case (ii) has the full sign
pattern `(N58)`. This confirms the normal-core restriction qualification in
the corrected Round 11 review and improves it: ambient abnormal owners do not
create a diffuse escape; only one pure first-layer-abnormal column can survive.

Gauss's uniform-modulus Corollary 48 is valid with the same scope. If the
**full** matrix has no homogeneous solution and phase values lie in a fixed
box, compactness by contradiction gives `delta,c>0` such that every returned
block with `0<S<=delta` has `B+E>=cS`. Under only `(N57)`, the same modulus
holds for normal-core-supported blocks after restriction. It does not yet hold
for arbitrary ambient blocks. Proposition 63 dispatches the pure vertex for
the narrower but producer-relevant class whose Quit hazards are supported on
positive masses of charge-tangent packets.

and found both valid ordinary mathematics. The review checked the exact

Either show that hard-branch floor/instant exclusions force a second owner at

into a stationary/generated uniform-payoff mechanism. Proposition 63 performs

vertex remains possible for arbitrary ambient product rows.

#### 58.8 Charge-tangent support excludes the exceptional homogeneous vertex

There are two different notions of normal player in the preceding discussion:

```text
recursive normality: membership in normalCore(normalizedSoloMatrix reward),
production normality: punishmentValue_i <= r_i({i}).                (N59)
```

They must not be conflated. Nevertheless, the fields of a charge-tangent
packet force the second notion on every active owner.

**Proposition 63 (proved ordinary mathematics; active-packet normality and
homogeneous-vertex dispatch).** Let `packet` be a
`QuittingChargeTangentData reward`. Then

```text
0<packet.mass i  ->  IsQuittingNormalPlayer reward i.               (N60)
```

Now let `M=normalizedSoloMatrix reward`, assume

```text
not HasHomogeneousSimplexSolution(normalPlayerMatrix M),             (N61)
not exists v, (quittingGame reward).IsUniformEquilibriumPayoff none v,
```

and let `mu` be a full homogeneous simplex witness for `M`. If every positive
coordinate of `mu` is production-normal, a contradiction follows. Therefore
no full homogeneous witness can arise as the normalized cumulative hazard of
a chronology in which every positive row hazard is supported on positive
masses of charge-tangent data.

**Proof.** If `packet.mass i>0`, positive-mass pinning gives

```text
packet.boundary i=r_i({i}),
```

while the punishment-floor field gives

```text
quittingPunishmentValue reward i <= packet.boundary i.
```

Combining the two is exactly `IsQuittingNormalPlayer reward i`, proving
`(N60)`. This implication is not a compactness argument and has no limiting
qualification.

For the homogeneous witness, if at least two coordinates are positive,
Proposition 62 restricts it to a homogeneous solution of `normalPlayerMatrix
M`, contradicting `(N61)`. Hence it is a vertex `mu=e_j`. Its full homogeneous
residual is column `j`, so

```text
0<=M_i,j=r_i({j})-r_i({i})   for every i.                            (N62)
```

The support hypothesis says that `j` is production-normal. Thus `j` is a
`QuittingNormalNoHarmSingletonOwner`: `(N62)` says that quitting alone by `j`
weakly improves every player's payoff over that player's own singleton row,
and production normality supplies the owner's punishment-floor inequality.
The checked theorem
`exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_normalNoHarmSingletonOwner`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/ProjectiveQBarBehavioralDecoder.lean`)
then produces a uniform-equilibrium payoff against arbitrary behavioral
deviations, contradicting the second hypothesis in `(N61)`. This eliminates
the vertex. Finally, in a packet row `h_i=t*scale*packet.mass i` with positive
`t,scale`, every positive hazard has positive packet mass. Finite sums,
normalization, and limits preserve zero mass outside the fixed finite set of
production-normal players, so the static result applies to the normalized
cumulative-hazard limit. ∎

**Exact source scope.** `IsQuittingNormalPlayer` is defined in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/AbnormalPlayers.lean`.
`QuittingNormalNoHarmSingletonOwner` and its unrestricted-behavior consumer
are checked in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/LCP/ProjectiveQBarBehavioralDecoder.lean`.
A narrow search of the charge-tangent subtree found the packet pinning and
punishment-floor fields but no named theorem `(N60)`. Proposition 63 is an
ordinary adapter between already checked interfaces; it is not a new game
producer.

**What changes.** Gauss Propositions 46--47 and Corollary 48 now have a full
residual-hard contradiction for returned blocks assembled from
charge-tangent-supported rows, even when their hazards are ambient rather than
recursively normal-core supported. The two compactness cases are exhaustive:
a nonvertex homogeneous limit contradicts the recursive normal-core gate, and
a vertex limit triggers the checked production-normal no-harm branch. This
does **not** extend the modulus to arbitrary product rows, whose active owners
need not satisfy a packet punishment floor.

**Remaining producer obligation.** Proposition 61 supplies one cheap local
packet row, and Proposition 63 prevents any returned loop of such rows from
collapsing homogeneously at zero total scale. What remains is the chronological
construction: re-extract a subcompatible packet at the actually reached tail,
keep all punishment floors, and either return exactly or accumulate enough
hazard/charge to enter an existing global consumer. The next check is the
moving-source behavior of
`QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness.exists_chargeTangentData_of_windows`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/ChargeTangentPacket.lean`).

`feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_24.md`
and found it valid ordinary mathematics, including the finite support-limit

#### 58.9 The actual tail residual is moving-source drift, not a fixed-base sign

The proposed next check has a negative answer in its fixed-boundary form.
`exists_chargeTangentData_of_windows` always sets

```text
packet.boundary = seam.limit.value,                                  (N63)
```

not the source payoff of each selected edge. The extracted tangent is the
limit of `(current-successor)/q`, but the declaration supplies no estimate
that either endpoint differs from the limiting boundary by `o(q)`. Exact
Nash mixing determines the missing first-order source displacement instead.

**Proposition 64 (proved ordinary mathematics; active moving-source readout).**
Consider a sequence of exact Nash--Bellman product edges with current payoff
`x^n`, successor payoff `y^n`, root hazards `p^n`, and absorption `q_n>0`.
Assume

```text
max_i p_i^n -> 0,
x^n,y^n -> b,
normalized singleton occupations m^n -> mu,
normalized collision mass -> 0,
(x^n-y^n)/q_n -> z,                                                   (N64)
```

and that `(mu,b,z)` is the resulting charge-tangent datum. For every owner
`i` with `mu_i>0`,

```text
(x_i^n-b_i)/q_n -> R_i,
(y_i^n-b_i)/q_n -> R_i-z_i,                                          (N65)
R_i = quittingActivePairCompatibilityResidual(packet,i)
    = sum_(j!=i) mu_j [r_i({i,j})-r_i({i})].                          (N66)
```

Thus the extractor does **not** force `R_i<=0`. Proposition 61 freezes the
current payoff at `b`, which sets the first radial displacement in `(N65)` to
zero and consequently needs the one-sided endpoint sign. An actual exact
tail instead transports the source payoff with velocity `R` on its active
coordinates.

**Proof.** Since `mu_i>0`, eventually the normalized singleton occupation of
`i` is positive. Hence `0<p_i^n<1` eventually. Exact endpoint Nash then makes
Quit and Continue indifferent, and Bellman equality identifies both with
`x_i^n`. Positive-mass packet pinning gives `b_i=r_i({i})`.

Condition on player `i` choosing Quit. The probability that exactly opponent
`j` also quits is

```text
p_j^n product_(k!=i,j)(1-p_k^n)
  = q_n m_j^n/(1-p_i^n).                                             (N67)
```

The conditional probability of at least two opponent quitters, divided by
`q_n`, tends to zero: after multiplying by `1-p_i^n` it is a subevent of the
root's collision mass, while `p_i^n->0`. Bounded rewards therefore make the
multi-opponent remainder `o(q_n)`. Consequently

```text
(x_i^n-b_i)/q_n
 = (QuitPayoff_i^n-r_i({i}))/q_n
 -> sum_(j!=i) mu_j [r_i({i,j})-r_i({i})].                           (N68)
```

The active-row collapse
`QuittingChargeTangentPacket.activePairCompatibilityResidual_eq_sum_pairJoinEffect`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Analytic/ChargeTangent/MixingCompatibility.lean`)
identifies this limit with `R_i`. Subtracting the tangent limit in `(N64)`
gives the successor formula in `(N65)`. ∎

**Exact positive-residual test.** Take two players, parameters `0<d<c`, and
the symmetric reward table

```text
r({0})=(0,d),   r({1})=(d,0),   r({0,1})=(c,c).                      (N69)
```

At every `0<t<1`, let both hazards equal `t`, put

```text
y^t_i=t(c-d)/(1-t),      x^t_i=t c.                                 (N70)
```

For either player the forced-Quit endpoint is `tc`, while the forced-Continue
endpoint is

```text
t d+(1-t)y^t_i=tc.
```

Hence this is an exact Nash--Bellman edge. Its absorption is
`q_t=t(2-t)`, each normalized singleton occupation is
`(1-t)/(2-t)->1/2`, and

```text
(x^t_i-y^t_i)/q_t=(d-ct)/((1-t)(2-t))->d/2.
```

The limiting datum has `b=0`, `mu=(1/2,1/2)`, `z=(d/2,d/2)`, and

```text
R_0=R_1=d/2+(c-d)/2=c/2>0.                                          (N71)
```

This family can be made into one literal chronological tail, rather than a
disconnected collection of edges. Choose `0<t_0<d/c` and recursively set

```text
t_(n+1)=t_n(c-d)/(c(1-t_n)).
```

Then `0<t_(n+1)<t_n`, hence `t_n->0`, and `(N70)` gives
`y^{t_n}=x^{t_(n+1)}` exactly.

Its punishment floor is exactly zero: all-Continue opponents cap the best
response at zero, while Continue forever guarantees a nonnegative payoff
against every opponent root. Thus it is a genuine floor-admissible tangent
packet, not merely an affine Bellman example. It is not a counterexample or a
returned block; it is the minimal exact chronological falsifier of the implication
“tail-derived exact mixing forces `(N51)`.”

`CODEX_GAUSS` independently reviewed Proposition 64 in
`feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_25.md`
and found it valid ordinary mathematics. The review checked the conditional
pair probability `(N67)`, the collision remainder, active indifference, the
exact chronological recursion, and the punishment-floor calculation. It
retains the correct scope: `(N69)` is a solved zero-solo boundary family, not
a game counterexample; its role is to falsify frozen-base reprojection.

**Corrected moving-source obligation.** The local object sought in `(N49b)`
must allow a current radial displacement `v` with active equations

```text
v_i=R_i,                                                             (N72)
```

rather than demand `R_i<=0` at a frozen boundary. The plausible global
mechanism is therefore a differential inclusion on the compact floor carrier:
extract a packet at a limit boundary, move the source and successor according
to `(R,R-z)`, and control the mismatch when the next packet is selected. The
kill criterion becomes a failure of closed source matching for this moving
graph, not the existence of a positive compatibility residual.

#### 58.10 Consecutive packet charts obey one projective clock ratio

Write `q_n` for one-stage absorption, `x_n` for the current payoff, and
`b` for the limiting payoff. Whenever `q_n>0`, introduce the exact radial
source coordinate

```text
v_n=(x_n-b)/q_n.                                                      (N73)
```

Because the reached successor is `x_(n+1)`, the definition of the normalized
endpoint tangent `z_n=(x_n-x_(n+1))/q_n` gives the algebraic identity

```text
v_n=z_n+(q_(n+1)/q_n)v_(n+1).                                       (N74)
```

This simple equality is the source-matching law absent from a frozen packet.

**Proposition 65 (proved ordinary mathematics; projective moving-chart
transition).** Consider a subsequence of consecutive positive-absorption
edges along which

```text
mu_n -> mu,       z_n -> z,
mu_(n+1) -> mu',  z_(n+1) -> z',
q_(n+1)/q_n -> theta in [0,infinity),                                (N75)
```

with normalized collision vanishing in both charts, so `(mu,b,z)` and
`(mu',b,z')` are the two limiting charge-tangent data. Let `R(mu)` and
`R(mu')` denote their active pair-join residuals (on positive mass these
depend only on the displayed occupation). For every player `i` with
`mu_i>0` and `mu'_i>0`,

```text
R_i(mu)=z_i+theta R_i(mu').                                         (N76)
```

In particular, if the same packet chart recurs on the next edge, then

```text
z_i=(1-theta)R_i(mu)                                                 (N77)
```

for every active owner. Hence an active coordinate with `z_i>0` gives the
exact dichotomy

```text
(R_i(mu)>0 and theta<1)  or  (R_i(mu)<0 and theta>1).                 (N77a)
```

All active nonzero ratios `z_i/R_i(mu)` must be the same number
`1-theta`. If `theta=1`, the tangent vanishes on the entire recurring positive
support. If this same chart occurs at **every sufficiently late edge** and
`q_(n+1)/q_n->theta`, then `q_n->0` forces `theta<=1`; only in that stronger
eventual regime does `z_i>0` force the contracting branch.

**Proof.** Proposition 64 applied to the first chart gives
`v_(n),i -> R_i(mu)`. Applied to the shifted consecutive chart it gives
`v_(n+1),i -> R_i(mu')`. Passing to the limit in the exact identity `(N74)`
proves `(N76)`. The recurrent formula `(N77)` is immediate. Since
`theta>=0`, the sign of the product in `(N77)` gives `(N77a)`; division gives
the common-ratio statement. For the final eventual-regime claim, a limit
`theta>1` makes `q_(n+1)>q_n` by a fixed factor at every sufficiently late
date, contradicting `q_n->0`. ∎

The two-player tail `(N69)--(N71)` is sharp:

```text
theta=(c-d)/c,      z_i/R_i=d/c=1-theta.
```

Thus clock contraction is not itself a contradiction. It is exactly how a
floor-admissible moving source accommodates a positive compatibility
residual. Nor may sparse clock expansion be silently excluded: a summable
positive sequence can rise on infinitely many selected adjacent pairs and
fall more sharply between them. The conjecture-facing restriction is instead
on a fully recurrent chart or support cycle. Since finitely many supports
exist, any proposed infinite nonperiodic producer must specify the resulting
support-transition graph and verify `(N76)` on every shared active owner. A
cycle retaining one owner throughout has the iterated law

```text
(1-product_a theta_a)R_i^0
  = sum_a (product_(b<a) theta_b) z_i^a.                              (N78)
```

If the cycle is genuinely repeated at all sufficiently late visits, its
full-cycle clock product is at most one because the absorption scales tend to
zero. Then a nonnegative right side in `(N78)`, with one strict term, forces
the contracting product and a positive residual. Without genuine recurrence,
even this conclusion is unavailable.

This is a necessary screen, not a producer. The next substantive question is
whether the counterexample tail's positive-debt owner or an active-funded
packet owner must persist through an eventually recurrent support cycle. If
so, `(N78)` can be combined with floor viability and the period-one refusal
readout; if owners can switch indefinitely or charts recur only sparsely, the
producer must control the intervening expansions and deleted clocks.

`feedback/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION__BY_CODEX_GAUSS__ROUND_26.md`.
The first review found the omitted expanding branch, with the exact algebraic
falsifier `theta=2,R=-1,z=1`. After the correction above, Gauss rechecked the
local dichotomy, eventual-chart qualification, full-cycle product condition,
and shifted use of Proposition 64, and found the revised proposition valid
ordinary mathematics with no remaining objection.

#### 58.11 Compressing zero rows exposes an unbounded logarithmic clock ledger

The sparse-expansion caveat does not prevent a canonical representation of
the whole moving graph. Suppose the counterexample tail is not eventually
all-Continue. Enumerate its positive-absorption dates as

```text
d_0<d_1<...,      Q_k=q_(d_k)>0,      X_k=x_(d_k).                   (N79)
```

Every date strictly between `d_k` and `d_(k+1)` has zero absorption. The
checked theorem `zeroAbsorption_dynamicDebtEdge_plateau`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/ZeroAbsorptionPlateau.lean`)
says that such an edge preserves its payoff exactly. Hence the immediate
successor of the root at `d_k` has payoff `X_(k+1)` even when zero rows occur
between them.

**Proposition 66 (proved ordinary mathematics; compressed positive-edge
moving graph).** Define

```text
Z_k=(X_k-X_(k+1))/Q_k,       V_k=(X_k-b)/Q_k,
Theta_k=Q_(k+1)/Q_k.                                             (N80)
```

Then

```text
V_k=Z_k+Theta_k V_(k+1)                                         (N81)
```

exactly. Moreover `sum_k Q_k<infinity`, `Q_k->0`, and

```text
sum_(k<N) log Theta_k = log(Q_N/Q_0) -> -infinity,                (N82)
sum_(k<N) log(1/Theta_k) -> +infinity.                            (N83)
```

Thus local expanding transitions may occur infinitely often, but their
logarithmic expansion is overpaid by contraction along the full compressed
path. Every consecutive-chart limit of this compressed graph obeys
Proposition 65.

**Proof.** Non-eventual all-Continue gives infinitely many positive dates;
otherwise the last positive date would be followed by a zero-absorption
plateau. The zero-edge theorem gives
`x_(d_k+1)=...=x_(d_(k+1))`, so the normalized one-stage endpoint drift at
`d_k` is precisely `Z_k`. Substituting `(N80)` proves `(N81)`. The positive
subsequence `Q_k` of the canonical tail's summable absorption charges is
summable and therefore tends to zero. Finally `(N82)` is the finite
telescoping identity for logarithms of positive ratios; its limit and `(N83)`
follow from `Q_N->0`. ∎

This ledger is not the absorption charge consumed by the punishment-floor
return theorem: total absorption is finite while the net log contraction is
infinite. Its role is diagnostic. Any source-matched producer that alternates
the expanding branch in `(N77a)` with later contractions must say where the
unbounded log account is stored in payoff/floor coordinates. Conversely, a
future falsifier cannot cite sparse expansions alone; it must realize an
entire compressed graph satisfying both the local transition law `(N76)` and
the global ledger `(N82)` while retaining the positive-debt semantic gap.

#### 58.12 Negative radial residual requires a strict normality margin

The canonical tail has more structure than an arbitrary moving graph: every
source payoff is already above the behavioral punishment floor by
`QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness.punishmentValue_le_tailValue`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/DynamicTailCapCarrier.lean`).
This rules out the expanding branch at a punishment-tight active owner.

**Proposition 67 (proved ordinary mathematics; floor-tight expansion
exclusion).** In the setting of Proposition 64, assume the exact edges belong
to the canonical counterexample tail. For every active limiting owner `i`,

```text
R_i<0  ->  quittingPunishmentValue reward i < r_i({i}).              (N84)
```

Equivalently, if `quittingPunishmentValue reward i=r_i({i})`, then `R_i>=0`.
Consequently, in a recurrent chart with `z_i>0`, the expanding alternative
`R_i<0,theta>1` from `(N77a)` is available only to a **strictly** production-
normal owner. A punishment-tight owner necessarily lies in the contracting
branch `R_i>0,theta<1` whenever the recurrent-ratio identity applies.

**Proof.** Active packet mass pins `b_i=r_i({i})`. Every selected source
satisfies

```text
quittingPunishmentValue reward i <= x_i^n.
```

If punishment equals the solo payoff, then `0<=x_i^n-b_i`. Dividing by the
positive absorption `q_n` and using Proposition 64 gives `0<=R_i`. The
contrapositive proves `(N84)`. In the recurrent positive-tangent case,
`R_i=0` is incompatible with `z_i=(1-theta)R_i>0`; the corrected dichotomy
from Proposition 65 then leaves strict contraction. ∎

The statement is sharp at the level of available hypotheses. A fixed strict
margin `r_i({i})-punishment_i>0` allows `x_i^n-b_i=q_nR_i<0` for all small
`q_n` without breaching the floor, so no sign conclusion follows for strictly
normal owners. The next route split is therefore exact:

```text
punishment-tight active owners: no expanding positive-tangent chart;
strictly normal active owners: expansion survives and must be handled by
  a no-harm/generated argument, a collision punishment, or the log ledger.
```

#### 58.13 A collapsed record clock is negligible-tail or diffuse-block

Gauss Proposition 50 removes local expansion by selecting suffix-maximal
positive rows, but its immediate-next ratio may converge to zero. The correct
object for that boundary is the **next suffix record**, not merely the next
row. Choose the records recursively so that

```text
Q_(r_l)=max_(k>=r_l)Q_k,
Q_(r_(l+1))=max_(k>r_l)Q_k.                                         (N85)
```

Put

```text
Phi_l=Q_(r_(l+1))/Q_(r_l),
H_l=sum_(k>r_l) Q_k.                                                 (N86)
```

**Proposition 68 (proved ordinary mathematics; record-collapse/diffuse
trichotomy).** After passing to a subsequence, one of the following holds:

```text
(a) Phi_l>=rho>0;
(b) Phi_l->0 and H_l/Q_(r_l)->0;
(c) Phi_l->0 and there are finite blocks strictly after r_l whose total
    one-stage charge is comparable to Q_(r_l), while their largest row
    charge divided by block total tends to zero.                    (N87)
```

In case (b), for every owner active in the limiting record chart,

```text
P_i(mu)=z_i.                                                        (N88)
```

Thus the old-support radial law survives the clock collapse. Case (c) is a
literal source-matched **diffuse exact Nash--Bellman block**, not an arbitrary
uncontrolled reset.

**Proof.** Either `limsup Phi_l>0`, yielding (a), or pass to a subsequence with
`Phi_l->0`. In the second branch, either `H_l/Q_(r_l)` has a subsequence
tending to zero, giving (b), or after another subsequence there is `eta>0`
with

```text
H_l>=eta Q_(r_l).                                                    (N89)
```

By `(N85)`, every future row has charge at most
`Q_(r_(l+1))=Phi_l Q_(r_l)=o(Q_(r_l))`. Starting immediately after `r_l`,
stop at the first finite index for which accumulated one-stage charge reaches
`eta Q_(r_l)/2`. Such an index exists by `(N89)`, and the overshoot is at most
`Phi_l Q_(r_l)`. Hence the finite block sum `S_l` satisfies

```text
eta Q_(r_l)/2 <= S_l <= (eta/2+Phi_l)Q_(r_l),
maxRow_l/S_l <= 2 Phi_l/eta ->0.                                   (N90)
```

Because `S_l->0`, the literal product absorption of the block is
`S_l+O(S_l^2)`; replacing `S_l` by product absorption preserves both
comparability and diffuseness. The compressed tail is an exact
source-matched Nash--Bellman chronology and zero rows preserve payoff, so the
selected block is semantically literal. This proves (c).

For (b), the one-stage value-motion bound
`QuittingDynamicDebtTail.abs_value_succ_sub_le_two_mul_absorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/PositiveDebtSelfLoopLimit.lean`)
and telescoping give

```text
|X_(r_l+1),i-b_i| <= 2M H_l=o(Q_(r_l)).                             (N91)
```

On an active record owner, Proposition 64 gives
`(X_(r_l),i-b_i)/Q_(r_l)->P_i(mu)`. Subtract `(N91)` in the exact definition
of the record tangent to obtain `(N88)`. ∎

**Exact scope.** This does not solve the diffuse branch. The block's
one-stage roots and all intermediate payoffs are actual and floor-admissible,
but no theorem here supplies every-suffix deleted-clock contraction or a
return. It does remove a false degree of freedom: a zero next-record ratio
cannot hide a finite radial correction in one uncontrolled atom. Either the
whole remainder is negligible, or the correction is carried by many
source-matched small rows. On the refreshed board this is the precise
interface with `CONDITIONED_PACKET_REPROJECTION.md` and
`PERSISTENT_DELETED_CLOCKS.md` in the read-only question pool; the earlier
diffuse-stationary question file is no longer present and is not cited as a
current obligation.

#### 58.14 The diffuse arm has a finite/infinite normalized-horizon split

Proposition 68 used only whether `H_l/Q_(r_l)` vanishes or stays positive.
The full extended limit of this ratio retains substantially more chronological
information.  Put

```text
L_l=H_l/Q_(r_l).                                                     (N92)
```

**Proposition 69 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed
normalized-horizon
trichotomy).**  In the record-collapse branch `Phi_l->0`, pass to a
subsequence on which

```text
L_l -> L in [0,+infinity].                                          (N93)
```

Then exactly one of the following regimes holds.

```text
(i)   L=0.  The future tail is negligible and every record-active owner
      satisfies P_i(mu)=z_i.
(ii)  0<L<infinity.  For every fixed 0<T<L, the initial future prefix whose
      charge first reaches T Q_(r_l) is an exact source-matched diffuse block
      B_l(T) with

        chargeSum(B_l(T))/Q_(r_l) -> T,
        absorption(B_l(T))/Q_(r_l) -> T,
        maxRowCharge(B_l(T))/chargeSum(B_l(T)) -> 0.                 (N94)

(iii) L=+infinity.  The same conclusion holds for every fixed T>0.
      Every fixed finite target family has nested first-crossing prefixes on
      one sufficiently late common tail.  For a countable increasing list,
      a triangular sequence of tails carries the first m targets at stage m,
      and each fixed target has the limits in (N94).                          (N95)
```

If `M` bounds the absolute values of the reward and tail-payoff coordinates,
the total payoff variation inside `B_l(T)` also satisfies

```text
sum_(edge in B_l(T)) ||X_edge-X_successor||_infinity
  <= 2 M (T+o(1)) Q_(r_l).                                         (N96)
```

Thus (iii) supplies source-matched diffuse prefixes of every prescribed
**normalized absorption-charge horizon**.  It does not assert that their
Euclidean Simon variation is bounded below.

**Proof.**  Compactness of the extended half-line gives `(N93)` after a
subsequence.  Regime (i) is Proposition 68(b), including `(N88)`.  Suppose
`T<L`, where every finite `T` is allowed if `L=+infinity`.  For all large
`l`, the future charge `H_l` exceeds `T Q_(r_l)`.  Beginning immediately
after `r_l`, stop at the first positive compressed row for which the partial
charge sum `S_l(T)` reaches `T Q_(r_l)`.  The overshoot is at most the
largest future row, and `(N85)--(N86)` give

```text
T Q_(r_l) <= S_l(T)
  <= (T+Phi_l) Q_(r_l),
maxRow_l(T)/S_l(T) <= Phi_l/T ->0.                                  (N97)
```

Hence `S_l(T)/Q_(r_l)->T`.  The elementary product estimate

```text
0 <= S_l(T)-[1-product_(edge in B_l(T))(1-Q_edge)]
   <= S_l(T)^2/2                                                     (N98)
```

and `Q_(r_l)->0` show that literal block absorption divided by
`Q_(r_l)` has the same limit `T`.  Every block is a contiguous prefix of the
actual positive-row compression; the intervening zero rows preserve payoff
by `zeroAbsorption_dynamicDebtEdge_plateau`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/ZeroAbsorptionPlateau.lean`).
Therefore the roots, intermediate sources, endpoint Nash equations, Bellman
equations, and punishment floors are literal rather than reprojected.

For two targets `T_1<T_2`, first crossing at `T_1 Q_(r_l)` occurs no later
than first crossing at `T_2 Q_(r_l)`, proving finite nestedness.  Given an
increasing countable list, choose `l_m` so late that the future ratio exceeds
its first `m` targets and the error bounds for those targets are below
`1/m`.  The first `m` prefixes on tail `l_m` are nested, and every fixed
target converges along this triangular sequence.  No one finite tail is
claimed to carry an unbounded list of horizons, and no simultaneous
uncountable selection is claimed.  Finally the canonical
one-edge motion bound
`QuittingDynamicDebtTail.abs_value_succ_sub_le_two_mul_absorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/PositiveDebtSelfLoopLimit.lean`)
summed over the block gives `(N96)`. ∎

**Boundary tests and exact consequence.**  For
`Q_k=2^(-2^k)`, one has `L=0`.  If a record `q` is followed by approximately
`T/sqrt(q)` rows of size `q^(3/2)` and then a negligible remainder, then
`L=T` and `(ii)` recovers every strict subhorizon.  If it is followed by
approximately `1/q` rows of size `q^(3/2)`, then `L->infinity` and `(iii)`
gives every fixed horizon even though the unscaled total tail still tends to
zero.

The infinite regime is the first scalar conclusion here that matches the
quantifier “every prescribed finite budget” in
`SIMON_NORMALIZED_MOTION_PREFIX.md`, but only for normalized absorption
charge.  That question explicitly requires Euclidean Simon variation and a
common near-feasible carrier, neither of which follows from `(N96)`; indeed
`(N96)` is an upper, not lower, variation estimate.  Likewise one finite
prefix does not imply the every-suffix deleted clocks in
`PERSISTENT_DELETED_CLOCKS.md`.  The next concrete check is therefore a
continuum compactification of the nested prefixes which retains the exact
endpoint-Nash support inequalities: either its moving support forces positive
payoff variation on a hard normal face, or the zero-variation case must be
identified with a checked generated/sure-exit branch.
