#### 58.15 Infinite normalized horizon yields a complementarity trajectory

The proposed compactification can be carried out without assuming a returned
payoff.  Write the normalized singleton matrix as

```text
M_(i,j)=r_i({j})-r_i({i}).                                          (N99)
```

**Proposition 70 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed diffuse radial
trajectory).**  Assume Proposition 69(iii).  After passing to a subsequence,
there are

- a nonempty set `A` of players;
- a locally absolutely continuous path `v:[0,+infinity)->R^A`; and
- a measurable control `mu(t)` in the probability simplex on `A` for almost
  every `t`;

such that every `i in A` is production-normal,

```text
b_i=r_i({i}),
v_i(t)>=0,
mu_i(t)>0 -> v_i(t)=0                    for almost every t,        (N100)
v_i'(t)=-sum_(j in A) M_(i,j) mu_j(t)    for almost every t.        (N101)
```

Moreover exactly one of the following holds:

```text
(a) Var(v;[0,T]) is unbounded as T->infinity.  Then the original nested
    exact blocks have arbitrarily large normalized payoff variation
    sum ||X_k-X_(k+1)||/Q_(r_l).
(b) Var(v;[0,+infinity))<infinity.  Then the principal matrix M_A has a
    simplex vector mu_* with M_A mu_*=0.                             (N102)
```

The vector in `(N102)` is only a homogeneous kernel on the active principal
face.  No sign is obtained on rows outside `A`, so it is not silently promoted
to a full homogeneous LCP solution.

**Proof.**  Fix the record scale `q_l=Q_(r_l)`.  On the common future tail,
use normalized charge time

```text
t_(l,0)=0,
t_(l,m+1)-t_(l,m)=Q_(l,m)/q_l.                                    (N103)
```

Proposition 69(iii) says that these grids cover every fixed compact time
interval for all large `l`, with mesh at most `Phi_l->0`.  On the interval
corresponding to row `m`, let

```text
mu_(l,m),i = Pr(row m absorbs at exactly {i})/Q_(l,m).               (N104)
```

This is a subprobability vector.  If `h_(l,m)=max_i p_(l,m),i`, then
`h_(l,m)<=Q_(l,m)`.  A union bound over pairs gives

```text
Pr(at least two quitters in row m)
  <= choose(card I,2) h_(l,m) Q_(l,m),                              (N105)
```

after the harmless weaker estimate obtained by summing `p_i p_j`.
Consequently the missing mass in `(N104)` is uniformly `o(1)` on every
compact normalized-time interval.  Regard `mu_l` as piecewise constant on
the grid intervals.  Weak-star compactness in the finite-dimensional
`L^infinity` unit box, followed by a diagonal subsequence, gives a measurable
limit `mu`; `(N105)` makes its coordinates sum to one almost everywhere.
Let `A` be the union of its essential supports.  Then `A` is nonempty and
`mu` is simplex-valued on `A`.

Let `X_(l,m)` be the actual current payoff at row `m`, and interpolate

```text
v_l(t_(l,m))=(X_(l,m)-b)/q_l                                      (N106)
```

affinely between grid points.  Initially this need not be bounded in every
ambient coordinate.  It is locally bounded in every coordinate of `A`.
Indeed, if `i in A`, some fixed compact interval carries positive limiting
`i`-mass.  Hence there are active `i`-rows in that interval.  Exact endpoint
Nash and `0<p_i<1` make Quit and Continue indifferent there.  Forced Quit
differs from `r_i({i})` by at most a reward-bound constant times
`Q_(l,m)`, since an opponent Quits with probability at most
`(card I-1)Q_(l,m)`.  Therefore

```text
X_(l,m),i=r_i({i})+O(Q_(l,m)).                                     (N107)
```

Taking the ordinary limit pins `b_i=r_i({i})`.  The one-edge value-motion
bound and the finite normalized distance to the active row then bound
`v_(l,i)` on every compact interval.  The same motion bound makes these
interpolants equi-Lipschitz.  Arzela--Ascoli and a second diagonal extraction
give a locally absolutely continuous limit `v` on `A`.

At any row, exact endpoint Nash makes forced Quit no better than the current
payoff.  For `i in A`, use `b_i=r_i({i})` and the same opponent-Quit estimate
as in `(N107)` to obtain

```text
v_(l,i)>=-C Q_(l,m)/q_l.
```

The grid mesh tends to zero, so `v_i>=0`.  On an active `i`-row, equality
in `(N107)` instead gives `v_(l,i)=o(1)`.  Thus
`integral mu_(l,i)|v_(l,i)|->0`; weak-star convergence of `mu_l` and uniform
convergence of `v_l` on compact intervals yield the complementarity clause
in `(N100)`.

Bellman equality at row `m` is

```text
X_(l,m)=(1-Q_(l,m))X_(l,m+1)+Q_(l,m)D_(l,m),                       (N108)
```

where `D_(l,m)` is the row's conditional absorbing delivery.  On its grid
interval the derivative of `(N106)` is therefore

```text
v_l'=X_(l,m+1)-D_(l,m).                                            (N109)
```

All source values converge to `b`.  By `(N105)`, the collision part of
`D_(l,m)` vanishes uniformly and its singleton part converges weakly to
`sum_j mu_j r({j})`.  Passing to the distributional, hence almost-everywhere,
derivative identity gives

```text
v_i'=b_i-sum_j mu_j r_i({j})=-sum_j M_(i,j)mu_j,
```

which is `(N101)`.  Finally the canonical punishment floor passes to the
limit and, together with `b_i=r_i({i})`, makes every `i in A`
production-normal.

For the last dichotomy, local absolute continuity gives

```text
Var(v;[0,T])=integral_0^T ||M_A mu(t)|| dt.                         (N110)
```

If this is unbounded, lower semicontinuity of variation under uniform
convergence makes the normalized variations of the exact approximating
prefixes arbitrarily large, proving (a).  If the total integral is finite,
choose Lebesgue points `t_n->infinity` with
`||M_A mu(t_n)||->0`.  Compactness of the simplex gives a subsequence
`mu(t_n)->mu_*`; continuity of matrix multiplication yields
`M_A mu_*=0`, proving `(N102)`. ∎

**Exact source and scope.**  The only game-semantic inputs beyond the
canonical exact tail are `IsεQuittingRootEndpointNash` at `epsilon=0`,
Bellman equality, the punishment-floor field, and
`QuittingDynamicDebtTail.abs_value_succ_sub_le_two_mul_absorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/PositiveDebtSelfLoopLimit.lean`).
A narrow search of the charge-tangent, conditioned-diffuse, and Simon-orbit
subtrees found no declaration that turns this record-rescaled infinite
chronology into `(N100)--(N101)`.  This proposition is not Lean-checked.

The output is now a genuine moving-support differential complementarity
object.  Arm (a) still lacks the common near-feasible carrier and unscaled
edge correspondence required by `QuittingSimonFiniteNearOrbitConditionAt`.
Arm (b) is weaker than the full homogeneous witness used in Proposition 63,
because outside-row inequalities are absent.  These are the two precise next
checks; neither absorption charge nor a principal kernel alone closes the
conjecture.

**Exact nonvacuity test.**  In the one-player zero-reward game, every hazard
row over the constant payoff zero is an exact Nash--Bellman row and respects
the punishment floor.  Choose cluster-start charges
`q_l=2^(-2^l)`, follow `q_l` by about `1/q_l` rows of charge
`q_l^(3/2)`, and then begin the next cluster.  The full sequence is summable;
along the cluster starts the next-record ratio is `sqrt(q_l)->0` while the
future-charge ratio is asymptotic to `1/sqrt(q_l)->infinity`.  Proposition 70
gives `A={i}`, `v=0`, and `M_A=[0]`, hence arm (b).  This confirms both that
the infinite normalized horizon is compatible with exact game semantics and
that a principal kernel can be a harmless generated branch rather than a
counterexample.  No exact hard-branch example realizing arm (a) is claimed.

#### 58.16 Full centered motion removes the principal-kernel escape

The principal kernel in Proposition 70(b) is an artifact of discarding
coordinates which never become active at the record scale.  Their **radial
offsets** may diverge, but their payoff motion across a normalized prefix is
still uniformly bounded after centering at the prefix source.

**Proposition 71 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed full-motion
homogeneous dispatch).**  In the setting of Proposition 70 there is a locally
absolutely continuous full-payoff path

```text
w:[0,+infinity)->R^I,       w(0)=0,                                 (N111)
w_i'(t)=b_i-sum_(j in A) mu_j(t) r_i({j})     almost everywhere.    (N112)
```

Exactly one of the following holds.

```text
(a) Var(w;[0,T])->infinity.  The original exact nested blocks have
    arbitrarily large full normalized payoff variation
      sum_k ||X_k-X_(k+1)||/Q_(r_l).
(b) Var(w;[0,+infinity))<infinity.  Then the full normalized singleton
    matrix M has a homogeneous simplex solution mu_* supported on A.         (N113)
```

Consequently, assume the residual-hard/no-uniform hypotheses `(N61)` used in
Proposition 63.  Since every owner in `A` is production-normal, arm (b) is
impossible.  Hence Proposition 69(iii) forces arm (a).

**Proof.**  On the triangular exact prefixes from Proposition 69 define

```text
w_l(t_(l,m))=[X_(l,m)-X_(l,0)]/q_l                                (N114)
```

and interpolate affinely on the normalized charge grid `(N103)`.  Unlike
`v_l`, this path is anchored at zero in every ambient coordinate.  The
one-edge value-motion bound makes the family equi-Lipschitz and locally
bounded without any active-coordinate hypothesis.  A diagonal
Arzela--Ascoli extraction gives the full path `w`.  The Bellman calculation
`(N108)--(N109)`, the collision estimate `(N105)`, and weak-star convergence
of `mu_l` give `(N112)` in every coordinate.  Its exact-prefix variations are
the left side of arm (a), so lower semicontinuity proves that arm when the
limit variation is unbounded.

Suppose instead that the total variation is finite.  Then

```text
integral_0^infinity
  ||b-sum_(j in A)mu_j(t)r({j})|| dt < infinity.                    (N115)
```

Choose Lebesgue points `t_n->infinity` at which the integrand tends to zero,
and pass to a simplex limit `mu(t_n)->mu_*`.  Extend `mu_*` by zero outside
`A`.  Continuity gives the full coordinate identity

```text
b_i=sum_j mu_*_j r_i({j})  for every i.                             (N116)
```

The canonical limiting endpoint-Nash inequality is

```text
r_i({i})<=b_i  for every i.                                        (N117)
```

Therefore `(N99)` and `(N116)` imply

```text
(M mu_*)_i=b_i-r_i({i})>=0.                                        (N118)
```

If `mu_*_i>0`, then `i in A`; Proposition 70 gives
`b_i=r_i({i})`, so `(M mu_*)_i=0`.  Thus `mu_*` is a probability vector,
`M mu_*>=0`, and `mu_*_i(M mu_*)_i=0` for every `i`: exactly a full
homogeneous simplex solution.  Its support lies in the production-normal set
by Proposition 70.  Proposition 63 now dispatches it under `(N61)`, proving
the final assertion. ∎

**What changes and what does not.**  This closes the outside-row gap from
Proposition 70(b): finite **full** motion produces the missing ambient signs
automatically from the boundary slack `(N117)`.  It is a genuine universal
obligation change: in a residual-hard counterexample, the infinite
future-charge/record-charge arm cannot be a zero-motion or finite-motion
phantom; it carries arbitrary normalized Euclidean payoff variation along
literal exact Nash--Bellman edges.

The normalization remains essential.  The unscaled variation of each block
is `q_l Var(w_l)` and may tend to zero, and the radial points `(N114)` need not
belong to `QuittingSimonFiniteOrbitCarrier` even though the unscaled sources
do.  Proposition 71 therefore does not prove
`QuittingSimonFiniteNearOrbitConditionAt`: the next exact adapter must either
show that Simon's correspondence is stable under this blow-up at the common
boundary, or convert repeated radial excursions into an unscaled returned
orbit before the record scale vanishes.

#### 58.17 Bounded radial oscillation would already return

The reviewed returned-block telescope rules out one more apparent escape.
First note an exact support fact for the canonical tail.

```text
Every production-abnormal player is eventually assigned Quit hazard zero.  (N119)
```

Indeed, if player `i` has positive hazards at cofinally many dates, select
such dates.  Vanishing joint absorption and exact mixing give
`b_i=r_i({i})`; the canonical punishment floor gives
`punishment_i<=b_i`, so `i` is production-normal.  The contrapositive and
finiteness of the player set yield one common cutoff after which every active
owner is production-normal.

**Proposition 72 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed radial-escape
necessity).**  Assume Proposition 69(iii) and the residual-hard/no-uniform
hypotheses `(N61)`.  The full centered trajectory `w` from Proposition 71 is
unbounded:

```text
sup_(t>=0) ||w(t)||=+infinity.                                      (N120)
```

**Proof.**  Proposition 71 says `Var(w;[0,T])->infinity`.  Suppose nevertheless
that the range of `w` is bounded.  The sequence `w(n)`, `n in N`, lies in a
compact finite-dimensional box, so choose integers

```text
n_1<n_2<...,
||w(n_(k+1))-w(n_k)|| ->0.                                         (N121)
```

Every interval has normalized charge length
`T_k=n_(k+1)-n_k>=1`.  For each fixed `k`, the triangular convergence in
Propositions 69--71 lets us choose a sufficiently late record tail `l_k`
whose exact normalized path covers `[0,n_(k+1)]`, is uniformly close to `w`
there, and has grid mesh `mesh_k` tending to zero.  Because `T_k` varies with
`k`, choose it still later so that both

```text
q_(l_k) T_k ->0,       mesh_k T_k ->0.                              (N122)
```

Cut the actual chronology at the grid crossings nearest `n_k` and
`n_(k+1)`.  Its total one-stage hazard `S_k` and endpoint mismatch satisfy

```text
S_k/q_(l_k) -> T_k,
||X_start-X_end||/q_(l_k)
  <= ||w(n_k)-w(n_(k+1))||+o(1),
||X_start-X_end||/S_k ->0.                                         (N123)
```

All rows inside the cut block are literal exact Nash--Bellman rows.  By
`(N119)` and the late choice, their positive hazards are supported on
production-normal owners.  Close the block by replacing its final tail
payoff by its initial source payoff.  Multilinearity of the one-row successor
and endpoint values changes the last Bellman and probability-weighted
endpoint-Nash residuals by at most a reward-independent constant times
`||X_start-X_end||`; every other row remains exact.  Thus the aggregate
Bellman-plus-semantic endpoint residual satisfies

```text
(B_k+E_k)/S_k ->0,       S_k->0.                                   (N124)
```

This is precisely the vanishing-relative-error returned-block family ruled
out by the independently reviewed returned-block telescope (Gauss
Propositions 46--47, audited in this notebook's Round 11 feedback) together
with Proposition 63's production-normal homogeneous dispatch.  The
contradiction proves `(N120)`. ∎

not an exact payoff return; the returned-block theorem is invoked in its

argument would be invalid with only raw endpoint gaps, but the closed rows use

For active-face coordinates, `w_i=v_i-v_i(0)` and `v_i>=0`, so every such

#### 58.18 The radial escape is uniformly ballistic

The differential law answers the size part of that question without a return
argument.  Put

```text
s_i=r_i({i}),       c=b-s>=0.                                      (N126)
```

For `T>0`, let the time-averaged control be

```text
barMu_T=(1/T) integral_0^T mu(t)dt in Simplex(A).                   (N127)
```

**Proposition 73 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed ballistic radial
fork).**  Under `(N61)`, there is a constant `delta>0`, depending only on
`b`, `M`, and `A`, such that

```text
||w(T)||>=delta T             for every T>0.                        (N128)
```

More precisely,

```text
delta=min_(nu in Simplex(A)) ||c-M nu|| >0,                         (N129)
w(T)/T=c-M barMu_T.                                                (N130)
```

Every subsequential limit `barMu_(T_k)->nu` with `T_k->infinity` therefore
has a nonzero drift

```text
d=c-M nu,       ||d||>=delta,       d_i>=0 for i in A.              (N131)
```

Consequently exactly one of the following algebraic escape modes occurs
along that subsequence:

```text
(a) some i in A has d_i>0, equivalently (M_A nu)_i<0;
(b) d_i=0 for every i in A, so M_A nu=0, while some coordinate outside A
    has d_i!=0.                                                      (N132)
```

**Proof.**  Integrating `(N112)` and using `w(0)=0` gives

```text
w(T)/T=b-sum_j barMu_(T,j)r({j})=c-M barMu_T,
```

which is `(N130)`.  The simplex on `A` is compact, so the minimum in `(N129)`
is attained.  If it were zero, some `nu` supported on `A` would satisfy

```text
M nu=c>=0.
```

Every positive coordinate of `nu` lies in `A`, where Proposition 70 gives
`c_i=0`; hence `nu_i(Mnu)_i=0`.  Thus `nu` would be a full homogeneous
simplex witness supported on production-normal owners, contradicting
Proposition 63 under `(N61)`.  Therefore `delta>0`, and `(N128)` follows from
`(N130)`.

Now pass to any convergent subsequence of the compact averages.  Equation
`(N130)` gives the first two clauses of `(N131)`.  On `A`, Proposition 70 has
`v_i>=0` and the centered paths satisfy

```text
w_i(T)=v_i(T)-v_i(0).
```

Division by `T` and passage to the limit give `d_i>=0`.  Since `c_i=0` on
`A`, this is `(M_A nu)_i<=0`.  Either one inequality is strict, giving (a),
or all are equalities.  In the latter case `M_A nu=0`; because `d` is
nonzero, some ambient coordinate outside `A` carries the drift, giving (b).
∎

**Exact consequence.**  Proposition 72 is now a qualitative corollary of
`(N128)`; its returned-block proof remains useful because it explains why
bounded oscillation is incompatible with the semantic telescope.  The new
quantitative fork identifies the only two surviving directions:

- a strict nonpositive principal residual on the production-normal active
  face; or
- a principal kernel whose entire ballistic displacement is seen by inactive
  ambient players.

Neither is a full homogeneous solution: in (a) the principal sign is the
opposite one, while in (b) an outside row is necessarily mismatched.  This is
the precise face-descent/cross-face datum to compare next with

(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/PrincipalQBoundaryDirection.lean`)

#### 58.19 First-exit normalization gives face descent or cross-face escape

Let

```text
K=max_(nu in Simplex(A)) ||c-M nu||.                                (N133)
```

Under `(N61)`, Proposition 73 gives `0<delta<=K`.  Since `w'=c-Mmu`, the
path is `K`-Lipschitz and satisfies `||w(t)||>=delta t`.

**Proposition 74 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed unit escape arc).**
There exist a locally absolutely continuous path `u:[0,1]->R^I`, a constant

```text
lambda in [1/K,1/delta],                                           (N134)
```

and a measurable simplex control `nu(s)` supported on `A` such that

```text
u(0)=0,       ||u(s)||<=1,       ||u(1)||=1,                        (N135)
u'(s)=lambda[c-M nu(s)]                    almost everywhere,       (N136)
u_i(s)>=0,   nu_i(s)>0 -> u_i(s)=0         for i in A, a.e.         (N137)
```

Put

```text
A'={i in A | u_i(1)=0}.                                            (N138)
```

Then `A'` is nonempty, and exactly one of the following occurs.

```text
(a) A' is a proper subset of A.  On some final interval (sigma,1), the
    control nu(s) is supported on A' for almost every s; thus the escape arc
    strictly descends to a smaller production-normal face.
(b) A'=A.  Then u_i(1)=0 for every active-face coordinate, while
    ||u(1)||=1 is carried entirely by coordinates outside A.        (N139)
```

**Proof.**  For `R>0`, continuity, `w(0)=0`, and `(N128)` give a first hitting
time

```text
tau_R=inf{t>=0 | ||w(t)||=R},       tau_R<=R/delta.                  (N140)
```

The `K`-Lipschitz bound also gives `R<=K tau_R`, hence

```text
1/K<=tau_R/R<=1/delta.                                             (N141)
```

Choose `R_n->infinity`, pass to a subsequence with
`tau_(R_n)/R_n->lambda`, and define

```text
u_n(s)=w(tau_(R_n)s)/R_n,
nu_n(s)=mu(tau_(R_n)s).                                            (N142)
```

First hitting gives `||u_n(s)||<=1` and `||u_n(1)||=1`.  Equations
`(N133)--(N141)` make the paths equi-Lipschitz.  Arzela--Ascoli and weak-star
compactness of the simplex-valued controls produce limits `u,nu` satisfying
`(N134)--(N136)`.

On `A`, the paths from Proposition 70 obey

```text
u_(n,i)(s)=[v_i(tau_(R_n)s)-v_i(0)]/R_n.
```

Since `v_i>=0`, the limit has `u_i>=0`.  Moreover
`mu_i(t)v_i(t)=0` almost everywhere, so

```text
nu_(n,i)(s)[u_(n,i)(s)+v_i(0)/R_n]=0
```

almost everywhere.  Weak-star/uniform passage gives the complementarity
clause `(N137)`.

If `A'` is proper, every `i in A\A'` has `u_i(1)>0`.  Continuity and finiteness
of `A` give one `sigma<1` such that all these coordinates stay positive on
`(sigma,1)`.  Complementarity forces their control coordinates to vanish
almost everywhere there, so the final control is supported on `A'`.  Every
member of `A'` remains production-normal because `A' subset A`.  This proves
(a); it also proves `A'` nonempty because a simplex-valued control cannot be
supported on the empty set.  If `A'=A`, nonemptiness follows from that of
`A`, all active coordinates of `u(1)` vanish, and `(N135)` forces a
nonzero outside coordinate, proving (b). ∎

**Exact scope.**  In case (a), the **control** descends on a final interval;
the ambient path still records every payoff coordinate.  Iterating this
finite-player descent can happen only finitely often before reaching a
one-face or case (b), but no restart theorem is claimed: each new arc arises

#### 58.20 Endpoint Nash and floors do not sign the cross-face drift

The proposed sign implication is false even for an exact two-player
floor-admissible chronology.

**Proposition 75 (proved ordinary mathematics; exact two-sign boundary
test).**  Let the players be `0,1`.  For a parameter `R in {-1,1}`, define

```text
r_0({0})=r_0({1})=r_0({0,1})=0,
r_1({0})=R,
r_1({1})=r_1({0,1})=-2.                                           (N143)
```

Let `p_n in (0,1)` be any summable positive hazard sequence and prescribe
only player `0` to Quit, with hazard `p_n`, at date `n`.  Put

```text
A_n=1-product_(k>=n)(1-p_k),
x_n=(0,R A_n),       b=(0,0).                                     (N144)
```

Then every row is an exact Nash--Bellman row over successor `x_(n+1)`, every
`x_n` lies above the production punishment floor, and `x_n->b`.  If the
hazards are chosen in the cluster form from the nonvacuity test after
Proposition 70, the record-collapse/infinite normalized-horizon construction
has active face `A={0}` and outside derivative

```text
w_1'=b_1-r_1({0})=-R.                                              (N145)
```

Thus `R=-1` gives positive cross-face drift and `R=1` gives negative
cross-face drift under the same exact clock and support.

**Proof.**  The identity

```text
A_n=p_n+(1-p_n)A_(n+1)                                             (N146)
```

gives Bellman equality in player `1`'s coordinate; player `0`'s coordinate is
identically zero.  Player `0` mixes between Quit payoff zero and Continue
payoff zero, so its endpoint condition is exact.  Player `1` is inactive.
If player `1` deviates to Quit at the current row, it receives `-2` whether
player `0` Quits simultaneously or Continues, while its prescribed current
payoff `R A_n` lies in `[-1,1]`.  Hence Quit is never profitable and the root
is exact endpoint Nash.

For the floors, player `0` can be held to value zero, so its punishment value
is at most `0=x_(n,0)`.  If `R=1`, opponents
continuing forever cap player `1` at `max(0,-2)=0<=A_n`.  If `R=-1`, player
`0` quitting surely caps player `1` at `max(-1,-2)=-1<=-A_n`.  Thus the
production punishment floor is respected in both tables.  Summability gives
`A_n->0`, so `(N144)` converges to `b`.  Finally a solo-player-`0` row has
conditional absorbing delivery `r({0})`, and `(N112)` gives `(N145)`. ∎

**Minimal failed implication.**  Exact endpoint Nash, exact Bellman
source-matching, vanishing mesh, and production punishment floors do not imply
either sign for an inactive coordinate in Proposition 74(b).  The examples
are not hard-branch counterexamples: their one-owner structure enters an
easy stationary/generated branch.  Therefore the surviving cross-face sign
must use the global residual-hard matrix structure or a compatible restart,
not the local chronology axioms alone.

#### 58.21 The hard radial motion has a fixed strict linear Lyapunov coordinate

Proposition 73 used only the distance of the compact affine drift set from
zero.  Convexity gives a stronger statement: the direction cannot rotate
around zero while the owner support stays on `A`.

**Proposition 76 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed strict-halfspace
return obstruction).**  Under the residual-hard/no-uniform hypotheses
`(N61)`, let

```text
D={c-M mu | mu in Simplex(A)} subset R^I.                           (N147)
```

There are a unit Euclidean covector `ell` and `kappa>0`, depending only on
`b,M,A`, such that

```text
ell dot (c-M mu) >= kappa       for every mu in Simplex(A).         (N148)
```

Consequently the full centered path of Proposition 71 satisfies

```text
ell dot [w(T)-w(S)] >= kappa(T-S)       for 0<=S<T.                 (N149)
```

In particular it has no nonconstant returned subarc while its control stays
in `Simplex(A)`.  The unit first-exit arc in Proposition 74 also satisfies

```text
ell dot u(1) >= lambda kappa>0.                                    (N150)
```

At the level of the original exact chronology, fix `T>0` and take the
first-crossing prefixes of normalized charge `T` used in Proposition 69.
After the triangular subsequence defining `w`, their endpoint displacement
obeys

```text
ell dot [X_end-X_start]/q_l >= kappa T/2                           (N151)
```

for all sufficiently large `l`.  Thus a diffuse fixed-normalized-horizon
prefix supported asymptotically on the old face cannot be closed with an
`o(q_l)` endpoint seam.  Any such returned-block construction must use an
owner outside `A`, a nonnegligible collision/atom, or a horizon outside this
fixed-`T` compactification.

**Proof.**  The set `D` is the affine image of a finite simplex, hence compact
and convex.  Proposition 73 proves `0 notin D`.  Choose `d_* in D` of least
Euclidean norm and put

```text
ell=d_*/||d_*||_2,        kappa=||d_*||_2>0.                        (N152)
```

For any `d in D` and `t in [0,1]`, the point
`d_*+t(d-d_*)` lies in `D`.  Minimality at `t=0` in the squared Euclidean
norm gives

```text
d_* dot (d-d_*)>=0,
```

so `ell dot d>=||d_*||_2=kappa`.  This proves `(N148)`.  Since
`w'=c-M mu(t)` almost everywhere, integration gives `(N149)`.  Applying the
same calculation to `u'=lambda(c-M nu)` and `u(0)=0` gives `(N150)`.

For `(N151)`, the centered interpolants `w_l` converge uniformly to `w` on
every fixed normalized-charge interval.  The first-crossing overshoot is at
most the normalized mesh `Phi_l->0`; equi-Lipschitz continuity therefore
identifies the exact endpoint with `w_l(T)+o(1)`.  Equation `(N149)` at
`S=0` gives `ell dot w(T)>=kappa T`, so the weaker half-margin in `(N151)`
holds eventually. ∎

**Boundary tests and scope.**  If `0 in D`, for example on a homogeneous
zero-matrix face, no positive `kappa` is possible; this is exactly the branch
excluded by Proposition 73.  Proposition 75 is not a counterexample: each of
its two easy tables has its own strict covector, with opposite ambient
orientation.  Proposition 76 asserts no coordinatewise sign and no
finite-scale excursion.  Its genuine gain is to remove old-face oscillatory
recurrence at every fixed normalized horizon.  The remaining universal
producer must therefore realize the positive production-normal cross-face
exit isolated by independently checked `CODEX_GAUSS` Proposition 53 as a

in `(N151)`.

#### 58.22 Every diffuse near-return needs a positive cross-face mass fraction

A support change of vanishing weight cannot cancel the strict old-face
separator.  The following elementary estimate is the quantitative form
needed by any returned-block producer.

For each singleton owner `j`, put

```text
g_j=c-M e_j=b-r({j}).                                               (N153)
```

Assume `I\A` is nonempty and define

```text
K_out=max_(j outside A)|ell dot g_j|,
theta=kappa/(kappa+K_out)>0.                                       (N154)
```

**Proposition 77 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed cross-face mass
pinning).**  For every probability vector `mu` on all singleton owners, let

```text
e(mu)=sum_(j outside A)mu_j.
```

Then

```text
ell dot (c-M mu) >= kappa-(kappa+K_out)e(mu).                       (N155)
```

Consequently, if `ell dot(c-M mu)<=eta<kappa`, then

```text
e(mu)>=(kappa-eta)/(kappa+K_out).                                  (N156)
```

In particular every singleton mixture equal to `b` assigns at least `theta`
mass to owners outside `A`.

The same conclusion survives literal diffuse returned blocks.  Precisely,
consider any sequence of finite exact Bellman blocks with positive total
absorption probabilities `a_l`, start and successor payoffs `x_l^0,x_l^1`,
and conditional absorbing deliveries `D_l`, so

```text
x_l^0=(1-a_l)x_l^1+a_l D_l.                                       (N157)
```

Assume

```text
x_l^1 -> b,
||x_l^0-x_l^1||/a_l ->0,
zeta_l:=P(first coalition is nonsingleton | absorption)->0.         (N158)
```

Let `mu_l` be the owner law conditional on the first coalition being a
singleton.  Then

```text
liminf_l sum_(j outside A)mu_(l,j) >= theta.                        (N159)
```

Thus neither an `o(a_l)` returned seam nor vanishing collision mass can hide
the required cross-face participation.

**Proof.**  For `j in A`, the vertex `e_j` belongs to `Simplex(A)`, so
Proposition 76 gives `ell dot g_j>=kappa`.  For `j outside A`, definition of
`K_out` gives `ell dot g_j>=-K_out`.  Splitting the convex sum over `A` and
its complement yields

```text
ell dot(c-M mu)=sum_j mu_j ell dot g_j
 >=(1-e(mu))kappa-e(mu)K_out,
```

which is `(N155)`; rearrangement gives `(N156)`.

For the block statement, `(N157)` gives

```text
D_l-x_l^1=(x_l^0-x_l^1)/a_l ->0,
```

so `D_l->b`.  Conditional on absorption, decompose

```text
D_l=(1-zeta_l)sum_j mu_(l,j)r({j})+zeta_l R_l^collision.            (N160)
```

The finite reward table bounds `R_l^collision`; hence `(N158)--(N160)` imply

```text
sum_j mu_(l,j)r({j}) -> b,
c-M mu_l=b-sum_j mu_(l,j)r({j}) ->0.                               (N161)
```

Apply `(N156)` with
`eta_l=ell dot(c-M mu_l)->0` (or simply take a vanishing upper bound on its
absolute value) to obtain `(N159)`. ∎

**Boundary tests and exact scope.**  If no owner lies outside `A`, the strict
separator already forbids a returned singleton mixture, so `(N154)` is not
needed.  If outside columns all have nonnegative `ell` drift, even full
outside mass cannot return; `(N155)` remains valid but is not sharp.  A
positive collision fraction can cancel the separator and is deliberately
excluded only asymptotically in `(N158)`.  Proposition 77 is a necessary
first-absorption law, not a construction and not an all-behavior equilibrium
claim.  It upgrades the hard route's next obligation from “some support
entry” to a **uniformly mass-bearing cross-face phase** compatible with
endpoint Nash, punishment floors, and the source-matched continuation.

#### 58.23 Exact semantic price of the positive Never atom

The global strict-covector theorem forces finite tail charge, hence positive
joint survival.  This does not merely violate the `joint_survival` field of
`QuittingChronologicalDebtShadowingCertificate`; it has an exact behavioral
price determined by the phantom boundary.

Let `roots n` and `X_n` be any exact Nash--Bellman tail with `X_n->b` and
`Q_n->0`.  Choose `N` so that every individual Quit probability is strictly
less than one for `n>=N`.  For a player `i` and `n>=N`, write

```text
C_n     = joint survival probability from n forever,
rho_i,n = opponent-only survival probability from n forever.       (N162)
```

Let `U_i(n)` be player `i`'s actual terminal payoff under the prescribed root
sequence and `R_i(n)` its payoff after replacing its entire suffix strategy
by pure Never.  At date `t`, let `Q_i(t)` be the forced-Quit value against the
opponents' row and put the exact endpoint slack

```text
Delta_i(t)=X_(t,i)-Q_i(t)>=0.                                      (N163)
```

Let `rho_i(n,t)` be opponent-only survival from `n` through `t-1`, and let
`T_i(n,t)` be the actual payoff from continuing through those dates and
quitting surely at `t`.

**Proposition 78 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed nonperiodic phantom
attachment identities).**  For every `i`, `n>=N`, and `t>=n`,

```text
U_i(n)=X_(n,i)-C_n b_i,                                            (N164)
R_i(n)=X_(n,i)-rho_(i,n)b_i,                                      (N165)
T_i(n,t)=X_(n,i)-rho_i(n,t)Delta_i(t).                             (N166)
```

Consequently the exact deviation gains over the prescribed terminal payoff
are

```text
R_i(n)-U_i(n)=(rho_(i,n)-C_n)(-b_i),                               (N167)
T_i(n,t)-U_i(n)=C_n b_i-rho_i(n,t)Delta_i(t).                       (N168)
```

If player `i` has positive prescribed Quit probability at `t`, then
`Delta_i(t)=0`.  Hence a future-active player with `b_i>0` has a pure-time
gain `C_n b_i`; a future-active player with `b_i<0` has a strict Never gain
whenever its future own survival is below one.

**Proof.**  Equation `(N164)` is exactly
`quittingValuePath_eq_terminalValue_add_survivalLimit_mul`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/PhantomBoundaryRestart.lean`).

For every late row, zero-error endpoint Nash and `p_(n,i)<1` imply that
forced Continue has value `X_(n,i)`.  Indeed policy evaluation writes `X` as
the convex combination of forced Quit and forced Continue; forced Quit is no
better than `X`, and positive Continue weight forces the Continue endpoint to
equal `X`.  Thus the scalar path `X_(n,i)` is also a live prescribed-value
path for the root sequence in which player `i` is replaced by sure Continue.
Its limiting annotation is still `b_i`, its joint survival is exactly
`rho_(i,n)`, and its terminal payoff is the literal pure-Never payoff.
Applying the same phantom-boundary theorem proves `(N165)`.

For `(N166)`, stop the sure-Continue recursion at date `t`.  Its displayed
terminal value is the forced-Quit value
`Q_i(t)=X_(t,i)-Delta_i(t)`.  Affinity of the finite recursion propagates
this one endpoint difference back with exactly the opponent-only survival
factor `rho_i(n,t)`.  No boundary term remains because quitting surely at
`t` absorbs the game.  Subtracting `(N164)` gives `(N167)--(N168)`.
Finally, if `p_(t,i)>0`, both actions have positive or available endpoint
weight; exact Nash plus policy evaluation pins forced Quit to the same value
`X_(t,i)`, so `Delta_i(t)=0`. ∎

**Boundary tests and exact scope.**  In a one-player constant singleton table
with summable positive hazards, `rho=1`; `(N167)` becomes the obvious Never
gain `(1-C)(-b)`, while `(N168)` at any active date becomes the immediate-Quit
gain `Cb`.  At `b_i=0` both phantom prices vanish exactly.  Under the global
late-tail theorem, summable charge gives `C_n,rho_(i,n)>0`, and any future own
hazard gives `rho_(i,n)>C_n`; thus the stated strictness has no hidden
zero-product case.

Proposition 78 explains why the tail cannot simply be executed or fed to the
vanishing-survival chronological consumer.  It does not solve the atom: a
finite restart must replace the phantom boundary while controlling the
normalized endpoint slack in `(N168)`, and a player with `b_i=0` is invisible
to these two scalar deviations.  The next producer obligation is therefore
an exact paid return/attachment or a different all-behavior treatment of the
zero-boundary coordinates, not another compactification of the same summable
tail.

### 59. Bounded transient charge has a state-local bias, but not a continuous one

There is a complementary global route through the compact punishment-floor
Nash--Bellman relation developed in
`notes/CODEX_CEDAR__ERGODIC_NASH_BELLMAN_RECURRENCE.md`.  Its independently
checked Proposition 1C shows that any invariant law with positive mean joint
absorption already supplies a uniform-equilibrium payoff through the checked
punishment-completed solo-cycle compiler.  Thus an unresolved game must have
zero mean absorption for every invariant law.  The remaining bounded-charge
subcase admits a stronger state-local conclusion than the path-dependent bias
recorded there.

Let `K` be a compact metrizable state space, `R subset K x K` a closed
relation, and

```text
Omega={omega in K^N : R(omega_t,omega_(t+1)) for every t}
```

its nonempty compact one-sided path space.  Let `a:K->[0,infinity)` be
continuous and suppose

```text
sup_(omega in Omega) sum_(t>=0) a(omega_t) <= B < infinity.       (N169)
```

Write `Y={omega_0:omega in Omega}` for the viable state set.

**Proposition 79 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed state-local bias
and sharp continuity obstruction).**  The function

```text
V(x)=sup { sum_(t>=0)a(omega_t) : omega in Omega, omega_0=x }
                                                                  (N170)
```

is well-defined on the compact set `Y`, satisfies `0<=V<=B`, and for every
viable edge `R(x,y)` with `y in Y`,

```text
a(x)+V(y) <= V(x).                                                (N171)
```

Indeed it obeys the exact dynamic-programming identity

```text
V(x)=a(x)+sup {V(y): y in Y and R(x,y)}.                          (N172)
```

However compactness, closedness, predecessor seriality, and continuity of
`a` do **not** imply that any continuous real function satisfies `(N171)`.

**Proof.**  The projection `Y` of compact `Omega` is compact, and every fiber
in `(N170)` is nonempty.  Bound `(N169)` makes its supremum finite.  Every
path from `x` has a first tail `y in Y`; conversely, if `R(x,y)` and `y` is
viable, prepending `x` to any path from `y` produces a path from `x`.
Splitting off the first charge and taking suprema proves `(N172)`, hence
`(N171)`.

For the sharp boundary, put

```text
o=(0,0),
x_(n,k)=(1/n,k/n^2),  n>=1, 0<=k<=n,
K={o} union {x_(n,k)}.
```

The only edges are

```text
o->o,
x_(n,0)->x_(n,0),       x_(n,0)->x_(n,1),
x_(n,k)->x_(n,k+1)      (1<=k<n),
x_(n,n)->o.                                                    (N173)
```

This is a compact closed relation; every state has both a predecessor and a
viable infinite future.  Define

```text
a(o)=a(x_(n,0))=0,
a(x_(n,k))=1/n  for 1<=k<=n.                                   (N174)
```

The charge is continuous because every branch converges uniformly to `o` as
`n->infinity`.  Every path either stays forever at a zero-charge loop or
traverses one finite branch, whose total charge is exactly one.  Thus
`(N169)` holds with `B=1`, while

```text
V(o)=0,   V(x_(n,0))=1,   x_(n,0) ->[topology] o.                 (N175)
```

So the canonical state envelope is discontinuous.  More strongly, if a
continuous `W` satisfied `a(x)+W(y)<=W(x)` on every edge, telescoping the
branch from `x_(n,1)` to `o` would give

```text
W(x_(n,1))-W(o)>=1
```

for every `n`, contradicting `x_(n,1)->o`.  Hence no continuous exact
state-local Lyapunov function exists for this compact closed
predecessor-serial example. ∎

**Exact change in the universal obligation.**  In the bounded-charge branch,
state locality itself is free: `(N170)--(N172)` always provide an exact
bounded decreasing bias on the viable floor carrier.  Continuity is the sharp
generic failure, not the Bellman identity.  A conjecture-closing use must
therefore exploit additional quitting Nash--Bellman geometry to regularize
`V`, work with lower-semicontinuous/state-augmented bias, or prove that the
branch-chain discontinuity `(N173)--(N175)` cannot be embedded in the actual
floor relation.  Merely invoking compact ergodic optimization cannot supply
the continuous certificate requested in `questions/SIMON_LYAPUNOV_CERTIFICATE.md`.

and found it valid ordinary mathematics.  The review checked the viable-fiber

### 60. Sublinear unbounded prefix charge need not produce one divergent path

The other zero-mean subcase has `A_N->infinity` but `A_N/N->0`.  Compactness
alone cannot diagonalize the maximizing finite prefixes into one path with
infinite charge.  The smallest exact obstruction is a scaled version of the
branch-chain above.

**Proposition 80 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed sublinear-prefix
no-go).**  There is a compact metrizable state space, a closed
predecessor-serial relation with a viable infinite future from every state,
and a continuous nonnegative charge such that

```text
floor(sqrt(N)) <= A_N <= sqrt(N)      for every N>=1,             (N176)
```

but every individual infinite path has finite total charge.

Here, explicitly,

```text
A_N=sup {sum_(t=0)^(N-1)a(omega_t): omega in Omega}.              (N176a)
```

**Construction and proof.**  Put

```text
o=(0,0),
y_(n,k)=(1/n,k/n^3),  n>=1, 0<=k<=n^2,
K={o} union {y_(n,k)}.                                           (N177)
```

Use the edges

```text
o->o,
y_(n,0)->y_(n,0),       y_(n,0)->y_(n,1),
y_(n,k)->y_(n,k+1)      (1<=k<n^2),
y_(n,n^2)->o.                                                  (N178)
```

Set

```text
a(o)=a(y_(n,0))=0,
a(y_(n,k))=1/n  for 1<=k<=n^2.                                  (N179)
```

Because `0<=k/n^3<=1/n`, every sequence with `n->infinity` converges to
`o`.  Hence `K` is compact, `(N178)` is closed, and `(N179)` is continuous.
Self-loops provide predecessors for `o` and `y_(n,0)`; every later branch
state has its preceding state as predecessor.  Each branch reaches `o`, so
every state is viable.

A path can wait on one zero loop and then traverse at most one fixed branch.
Branch `n` has `n^2` positive states of charge `1/n`, so its total charge is
exactly `n`, finite for that path.  For a horizon of `N` rows, the largest
prefix charge available on branch `n` is

```text
min(N,n^2)/n.                                                     (N180)
```

If `n<=sqrt(N)`, this is at most `n<=sqrt(N)`; if `n>=sqrt(N)`, it is at most
`N/n<=sqrt(N)`.  Thus `A_N<=sqrt(N)`.  Taking
`n=floor(sqrt(N))` gives a complete branch within the horizon and charge `n`,
which proves the lower bound in `(N176)`.  Therefore `A_N` is unbounded and
sublinear, yet no single path has divergent charge. ∎

**Exact scope and surviving producer obligation.**  This is again a generic
closed-relation obstruction, not an embedding in the quitting floor carrier.
It shows that the slogan “unbounded finite-prefix charge gives a divergent
clock path” is false without source matching or nesting.  Together with
Proposition 79, the zero-invariant-mean branch has an exact generic trichotomy:

1. bounded `A_N` gives a bounded state-local bias, possibly discontinuous;
2. unbounded sublinear `A_N` may escape through longer transient branches
   with no common infinite path; and
3. a genuinely nested divergent path is the only case immediately accepted
   by the checked chronological clock compiler, subject also to every-player-
   deleted divergence.

A universal proof must use Nash--Bellman/quitting geometry to rule out or
semantically consume the collapsing long-branch pattern `(N177)--(N180)`.
Compactness and Fekete's lemma alone cannot do so.

### 61. Semialgebraicity does not remove the collapsing-branch obstruction

Proposition 80 used a countable compact carrier.  The actual quitting
Nash--Bellman correspondence is finite-dimensional and semialgebraic, so the
next natural hope is that tame geometry forbids those branches.  It does not.

**Proposition 81 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed semialgebraic
strengthening).**  There is a compact semialgebraic state space `K subset
R^3`, a compact semialgebraic closed predecessor-serial relation `R`, and a
continuous polynomial nonnegative charge `a` on `K` such that

```text
sqrt(N) <= A_N <= sqrt(N)+2       for every N>=1,                 (N181)
```

while every individual infinite path has finite total charge.

**Construction.**  Use three disjoint labeled components

```text
Z={z_e=(0,e,0): 0<=e<=1},
P={u_(e,s)=(1,e,s): 0<=e<=1, 0<=s<=1},
{o}={(2,0,0)},
K=Z union P union {o}.                                           (N182)
```

Let `R` be the union of the following closed semialgebraic edge sets:

```text
z_e -> z_e,
z_e -> u_(e,s)              when 0<=s<=e^2,
u_(e,s) -> u_(e,s+e^2)      when s+e^2<=1,
u_(e,s) -> o                when s+e^2>=1,
o -> o.                                                          (N183)
```

At equality both displayed outgoing edges are allowed.  Define the restriction
to `K` of the polynomial

```text
a(label,e,s)=e*label*(2-label).                                  (N184)
```

Thus `a=0` on `Z` and at `o`, while `a(u_(e,s))=e`.

**Proof.**  The carrier and each graph piece in `(N183)` are compact
semialgebraic, so their finite union is a compact semialgebraic closed
relation.  Every `z_e` has its self-loop as predecessor.  A phase state
`u_(e,s)` has predecessor `z_e` if `s<=e^2`, and predecessor
`u_(e,s-e^2)` if `s>=e^2`; for `e=0` this includes its own self-loop.  The
point `o` has its self-loop.  Thus `R` is predecessor-serial, and every state
also has an infinite future.

Fix a path.  If it never leaves `Z`, or lies in the `e=0` phase, its total
charge is zero.  Otherwise it chooses one fixed `e>0`, enters `P` once, and
its `s` coordinate then increases by `e^2` until it moves to `o`.  It has at
most `1/e^2+2` positive-charge rows, hence total charge at most

```text
1/e+2e < infinity.                                                (N185)
```

For the prefix maximum `(N176a)`, choose `e=1/sqrt(N)` and start at
`u_(e,0)`.  The first `N` phase states have
`s=0,e^2,...,(N-1)e^2`, so their total charge is exactly `N e=sqrt(N)`.
This proves the lower bound in `(N181)`.

For the upper bound, a path with parameter `e` contributes at most

```text
min(N e, 1/e+2e).                                                 (N186)
```

If `e<=1/sqrt(N)`, the first term is at most `sqrt(N)`.  If
`e>=1/sqrt(N)`, the second is at most `sqrt(N)+2`, since `e<=1`.
This proves `(N181)`.  In particular `A_N/N->0`, but `A_N->infinity`, and no
one path has divergent charge. ∎

**Boundary and exact scope.**  The relation is deliberately an abstract tame
model, not a supplied quitting reward table.  It rules out a purely
semialgebraic rescue of the zero-mean recurrence argument: compactness,
predecessor seriality, finite dimension, polynomial charge, and
semialgebraicity still allow arbitrarily long transient clocks to escape
through a parameter `e->0`.  A successful game proof must use the actual
multilinear Nash--Bellman equations, punishment-floor inequalities, or a
uniform source-matching property beyond tame topology.

and found it valid ordinary mathematics.  The review checked the compact

### 62. One-edge normalized coercivity still permits sublinear transient escape

The hard quitting branch has more than semialgebraicity.  Reviewed normalized-
motion coercivity gives a game-dependent `rho>0` with payoff displacement at
least `rho` times one-row absorption.  Even that local estimate does not by
itself turn unbounded sublinear prefix charge into a divergent path: a slow
counter can coexist with a fast compact rotation.

For `0<=e<=1`, let `Rot_e` be the rational rotation of the unit circle

```text
Rot_e(x,y)=(((1-e^2)x-2e y)/(1+e^2),
            (2e x+(1-e^2)y)/(1+e^2)).                            (N187)
```

It satisfies

```text
||Rot_e(v)-v||=2e/sqrt(1+e^2)>=e.                                (N188)
```

**Proposition 82 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed coercive
semialgebraic no-go).**  There is a compact semialgebraic closed
predecessor-serial relation with continuous polynomial charge such that

```text
||x-y||>=a(x)                    for every relation edge x->y,    (N189)
sqrt(N)<=A_N<=sqrt(N)+2,                                          (N190)
```

yet every individual path has finite total charge.

**Construction and proof.**  In `R^5`, use launcher states, phase states, and
one terminal point

```text
L={(0,e,0,v): 0<=e<=1, ||v||=1},
P={(1,e,t,v): 0<=e,t<=1, ||v||=1},
o=(2,0,0,1,0),
K=L union P union {o}.                                           (N191)
```

The relation is the finite union

```text
(0,e,0,v) -> (0,e,0,v),
(0,e,0,v) -> (1,e,t,v)                 when 0<=t<=e^2,
(1,e,t,v) -> (1,e,t+e^2,Rot_e(v))      when t+e^2<=1,
(1,e,t,v) -> o                         when t+e^2>=1,
o -> o.                                                          (N192)
```

The equations for `Rot_e` become polynomial after multiplication by the
strictly positive denominator `1+e^2`, so `(N191)--(N192)` are compact
semialgebraic sets and the graph is closed.  Define the polynomial charge

```text
a(label,e,t,v)=e*label*(2-label),                                 (N193)
```

which is zero on `L` and at `o`, and equals `e` on `P`.

Launcher and terminal self-loops provide their own predecessors.  A phase
state `(1,e,t,v)` with `t<=e^2` has launcher predecessor `(0,e,0,v)`; if
`t>=e^2`, it has phase predecessor
`(1,e,t-e^2,Rot_e^(-1)(v))`.  These cover `e=0` as well.  Every state has an
infinite future.

Every phase-to-phase edge satisfies `(N189)` by `(N188)` (the counter motion
only increases its Euclidean length), and every phase-to-terminal edge has a
unit label displacement, at least `e`.  All remaining edges have zero source
charge.  Thus the normalized one-edge coercivity is literal with constant
one.

For fixed `e>0`, the counter `t` increases by `e^2` until the path reaches
`o`; hence there are at most `1/e^2+2` positive rows and total charge at most
`1/e+2e`.  The prefix calculation is unchanged from Proposition 81: taking
`e=1/sqrt(N)` gives `N` positive rows and charge `sqrt(N)`, while every path
contributes at most `min(Ne,1/e+2e)<=sqrt(N)+2`.  This proves `(N190)` and
the every-path finite-total claim. ∎

**Exact boundary.**  Proposition 82 is not a quitting-game embedding; in
particular its fast circle coordinate is an abstract recurrence variable.
It does show that the already reviewed one-edge estimate
`||T(x,b)-x||>=rho q(b)` cannot by itself close the sublinear-prefix branch.
The missing input must control accumulated direction—such as a common
Lyapunov covector, bounded turning, source-matched return, or a specifically
game-semantic account—not merely the norm of each step.

### 63. The checked finite-prefix compiler eliminates every unbounded-charge branch

The preceding generic relations deliberately lacked quitting semantics.  On
the actual punishment-floor Nash--Bellman carrier, the project already has a
stronger consumer than diagonal extraction: arbitrarily charged **finite**
forward prefixes produce a single-seam projective lasso and hence a uniform
payoff.  Because uniform-equilibrium profiles may depend on accuracy, no one
divergent infinite path is needed.

Use `Omega`, `R`, `a`, and `A_N` from
`notes/CODEX_CEDAR__ERGODIC_NASH_BELLMAN_RECURRENCE.md`: `R(x,y)` is the exact
chronological edge from current state `x` to tail state `y`, and `a(x)` is the
absorption mass of the root stored at `x`.

**Proposition 83 (independently reviewed ordinary adapter between named
checked declarations).**  If

```text
sup_N A_N=infinity,                                               (N194)
```

then the quitting game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted
behavioral deviations.  Equivalently, if the game has no uniform-equilibrium
payoff, there is one `B>=0` such that for every `omega in Omega` and every
`N`,

```text
sum_(t=0)^(N-1)a(omega_t)<=B.                                    (N195)
```

Thus the positive-invariant-mean and unbounded-sublinear branches are both
already closed.  Only the uniformly bounded transient-charge branch survives.

**Proof and exact orientation.**  Fix a path prefix

```text
omega_0 R omega_1 R ... R omega_N.                               (N196)
```

`quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`)
orients the same exact edge in reverse:

```text
src=omega_(t+1),  tgt=omega_t,
charge=a(omega_t).                                               (N197)
```

Therefore reversing `(N196)` gives a literal finite path of that charged
relation from `omega_N` to `omega_0`, with total charge
`sum_(t<N)a(omega_t)`.  Every state is boxed and floor-admissible by the
definition of `Omega`.  The named adapter
`QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.pathToFinitePrefix` in the
same file converts this reversed path to a
`QuittingPunishmentFloorFinitePrefix`, and
`pathToFinitePrefix_charge` preserves the charge exactly.

Under `(N194)`, for every nonnegative target charge choose `N` and `omega`
whose prefix charge exceeds it, reverse the prefix as above, and apply the
checked
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_unbounded_floorPrefixCharge`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorFinitePrefix.lean`).
Its downstream finite-packet/single-seam compiler covers unrestricted
behavioral deviations.

For the contrapositive formulation, apply the checked dichotomy
`quittingGame_uniformPayoff_or_bounded_floorPrefixCharge` in the same file.
Under no uniform payoff it supplies `B>=0` bounding every exact floor-prefix
certificate.  Apply that bound to the reversed certificate of every
`(N196)` and use exact charge preservation to obtain `(N195)`. ∎

**Potential orientation correction.**  There are two different state-local
accounts.  Proposition 79's supremal **future** charge decreases in the

```text
a(x)+V(y)<=V(x)       when R(x,y).                                (N198)
```

By contrast, the project's charged relation reverses `R`; its canonical

```text
a(x)+Phi(x)<=Phi(y)   when R(x,y),                                (N199)
```

via `quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissiblePotential_predecessor_decrement`

Thus the increasing chronological sign in the Cedar note's equation (5.2)
