### 64. The exact bounded-capacity mismatch/positive-solo dichotomy

The checked capacity account and exact dynamic debt have stronger opposite
monotonicities than the one-step asymptotic statement alone reveals.  This
pins down exactly why a constant boundary reanchor cannot close the remaining
branch.

Fix the canonical `QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness` from a terminal
exploitability witness and a player `i`.  Write

```text
D_t = killedDebtReference(i,t),
A_t = killedCapacityDebtAccount(i,t),
m_t = D_t-A_t.                                                (N200)
```

Let `d_infty=limit.debt(i)`.  Since `A_t` is nonnegative and antitone, let
`A_infty=inf_t A_t`, and put `m_infty=d_infty-A_infty`.

**Proposition 84 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; capacity/solo
dichotomy).**  The following hold.

1. `m_t` is monotone nondecreasing and converges to `m_infty`.
2. If `m_infty<=0`, then `D_t<=A_t` for every `t`: the canonical remaining-
   capacity account already dominates exact dynamic debt at every finite
   date.
3. If `m_infty>0`, then `d_infty>0` and

   ```text
   0<r_i({i})<=limit.value(i).                               (N201)
   ```

   Thus failure of asymptotic capacity domination is confined to a player
   with a fixed positive own-solo terminal gap.
4. Let `c_t=killedDebtSurvival(t)` and let `delta_t` be the checked killed
   dissipation of `A`.  Then exactly

   ```text
   c_t m_(t+1)-m_t=delta_t>=0,                               (N202)
   ```

   and for every finite window

   ```text
   W(t,L)m_(t+L)
     =m_t+sum_(j<L) W(t,j)delta_(t+j),                       (N203)
   ```

   where `W` is joint prefix survival.
5. Suppose `m_t>=0` and reanchor by the only constant that matches exact debt
   initially,

   ```text
   A^t_u=A_u+m_t.                                            (N204)
   ```

   Then `A^t` remains killed-excessive and `A^t_t=D_t`.  But at the far end
   of a window,

   ```text
   boundary_D-boundary_(A^t)
     =W(t,L)(m_(t+L)-m_t)>=0.                                (N205)
   ```

   If `W(t,L)>0`, the boundary domination needed by
   `killedDissipationSum_eq_zero_of_debtBoundary_le` holds if and only if
   `m_(t+L)=m_t`.  Thus a positive constant reanchor is safe for excessivity
   but cannot reverse any genuine mismatch growth.

**Proof.**  Exact dynamic debt is monotone in chronological time by
`QuittingDynamicDebtTail.monotone_debt`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/PositiveDebtSelfLoopLimit.lean`).
The capacity account is antitone by
`antitone_killedCapacityDebtAccount`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/BoundaryMismatchAlternative.lean`).
Therefore

```text
m_(t+1)-m_t=(D_(t+1)-D_t)+(A_t-A_(t+1))>=0.                  (N206)
```

The checked convergence `debt_tendsto` gives `D_t->d_infty`; monotone bounded
convergence gives `A_t->A_infty`, proving clause 1.  In particular
`m_t<=m_infty`.  Clause 2 follows immediately.  If `m_infty>0`, then
`d_infty>A_infty>=0`.  The named declarations
`debt_le_soloReward_of_debt_pos` and `soloReward_le_value` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Dynamic/PositiveDebtSelfLoopLimit.lean`
give `(N201)`, proving clause 3.

For clause 4, exact debt satisfies

```text
D_t=source_t+c_t D_(t+1),
```

while by definition

```text
delta_t=A_t-source_t-c_t A_(t+1).
```

Subtracting gives `(N202)`.  Equivalently, combine
`killedCapacityBoundaryMismatch_one` with
`killedCapacityBoundaryMismatch_eq_initial_add_dissipation` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/BoundaryMismatchAlternative.lean`
and
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Debt/Source/DynamicAlternative.lean`.
Iterating the identity gives `(N203)`.

For clause 5, `m_t>=0`, `c_u<=1`, and excessivity of `A` give

```text
source_u+c_u(A_(u+1)+m_t)
 <= A_u+c_u m_t
 <= A_u+m_t.
```

Thus the shifted account is excessive, and `(N200)` gives the initial
equality.  The exact definition of `killedBoundaryRemainder` gives the first
equality in `(N205)`; monotonicity `(N206)` gives its sign.  When the prefix
weight is positive, the difference is nonpositive exactly when its final
factor is zero, which by monotonicity is exactly
`m_(t+L)=m_t`. ∎

**Small exact boundary tests.**  These tests concern the scalar killed laws,
not arbitrary game realization.  Take one row with `c_0=1/2` and zero source,
followed by `c_t=1` for `t>=1`.  Let `D_t=d` and `A_t=a` for `t>=1`, and set
`D_0=d/2`, `A_0=a`.  Thus debt is exact, the capacity account satisfies its
stronger additive inequality with equality `A_0=A_1`, and its killed
dissipation at the first row is `a/2`.  Now

```text
m_0=d/2-a,          m_infty=d-a.
```

Both limiting signs occur: `d=1,a=2` is the capacity-domination arm, while
`d=3,a=1` is the positive-mismatch arm.  In the latter, shifting by
`m_0=1/2` makes the initial values equal, but the far boundary difference is
`(1/2)(m_infty-m_0)=3/4>0`, exactly `(N205)`.  Hence neither sign nor the
constant-reanchor failure can be removed by scalar killed accounting alone.

**Narrow source/novelty audit.**  A search for `initialMismatch`,
`CapacityBoundaryMismatch`, and reference/account comparisons found the
one-step identity, summable dissipation, and conditional boundary consumers
in `KilledCapacityPotential.lean`, `KilledTailPotential.lean`,
`BoundaryMismatchAlternative.lean`, and `Source/DynamicAlternative.lean`.
I found no named declaration stating monotonicity of `m_t`, the limiting-sign
dichotomy, or the exact constant-reanchor obstruction `(N205)`.  Proposition
84 is nevertheless an ordinary adapter among those declarations, not a new
consumer or a uniform-payoff theorem.

**Exact surviving obligation.**  The bounded-capacity seam now has two
noninterchangeable arms.  On `m_infty<=0`, one must turn pointwise capacity
domination into an actually co-realized finite debt-paying prefix; the scalar
inequality does not produce that path.  On `m_infty>0`, the same player has a
positive own-solo deviation, and Proposition 55 of the Gauss note computes
the full late-suffix behavioral gap.  A successful proof must use that gap to
construct a source-matched paid return or persistent punishment chronology.
Constantly shifting the capacity account cannot do either job.

Next concrete check: independently falsify `(N206)--(N205)`, especially the
positive-shift excessivity and the strict equivalence when the joint prefix
weight is positive; then test whether `D_t<=A_t` is consumed anywhere beyond
the currently conditional reachable-carry telescope.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 44 independently reconstructed the
opposite monotonicities, the limiting-sign dichotomy, `(N202)--(N205)` with
the chronological indexing shown here, and the positive-shift excessivity.
It found Proposition 84 valid ordinary mathematics and emphasized the same
scope: the result diagnoses the seam but does not co-realize a capacity
account with an executable path or attach the positive-solo cost.

### 65. Any physical all-positive-solo frozen root funds its own terminal cap

The exact incoming-path fork in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Capacity/TerminalIncomingPathAlternative.lean`
currently chooses a root whose absorption is prescribed to pay the aggregate
terminal debt.  That magnitude choice is unnecessary once the support is
chosen correctly.

Let

```text
M = quittingRewardBound(reward),
P = {i | 0<r_i({i})},
Q = quittingRootAbsorptionMass(root).                         (N207)
```

Recall that the zero-boundary aggregate terminal debt is

```text
T=sum_i max(0,r_i({i}))=sum_(i in P) r_i({i}).                (N208)
```

**Proposition 85 (independently reviewed ordinary mathematics; automatic
funding law).**  Suppose `root` is interior on a support `S`, `P subset S`, and there
is a physical continuation satisfying

```text
IsQuittingFrozenRootContinuationLift
  reward 0 floor M root S continuation.                       (N209)
```

Then for every `i in P`,

```text
r_i({i}) <= M * opponentAbsorption(root,i) <= M*Q,             (N210)
```

and consequently

```text
T <= |P|*M*Q <= card(I)*M*Q.                                  (N211)
```

Thus `(N209)` automatically supplies the `hpays` premise of
`debt_zero_le_aggregateCapacityAccount_zero_of_frozenRootLift` for every
finite zero-boundary chain in the nonpositive-punishment lane.  The remaining
branch is pure feasibility (or its explicit Farkas certificate), not a
separate charge calibration.

**Proof.**  Fix `i in P`.  Because `P subset S`, interior support gives
`0<p_i<1`.  The lift's active derivative equality is precisely equality of
player `i`'s forced-Quit and forced-Continue endpoints, by
`quittingRootEndpointDifference_eq_mobiusCoordinateDerivative`.  Their
`p_i`-mixture is the Bellman successor payoff, which is the zero target.
Hence both endpoints, and in particular the forced-Quit endpoint, equal zero.

Let `mu_i` be the product law of the opponents' Quit coalition, let
`s_i=r_i({i})>0`, and let `h_i=mu_i(T nonempty)`.  Forced Quit gives the exact
identity

```text
0=sum_T mu_i(T) r_i(T union {i}).                              (N212)
```

The empty term is `mu_i(empty)s_i`.  Therefore

```text
s_i
 = |sum_(T nonempty) mu_i(T)(r_i(T union {i})-s_i)|
 <= sum_(T nonempty) mu_i(T)
      (|r_i(T union {i})|+|s_i|).                             (N213)
```

For nonempty `T`, the terminal entries `(T union {i},i)` and `({i},i)` are
distinct.  Since `M` is the sum of the absolute values of **all** terminal
reward entries, not merely a coordinatewise maximum,

```text
|r_i(T union {i})|+|s_i| <= M.                                (N214)
```

Equations `(N213)--(N214)` give `s_i<=M h_i`.  Opponent absorption is at most
joint root absorption, so `(N210)` follows.  Sum `(N210)` over `P`, use
`(N208)`, and then `|P|<=card(I)` to get `(N211)`. ∎

**Exact positive and negative tests.**

- With one player and positive solo reward, a root supported on that player
  cannot satisfy `(N209)`: forced Quit is the positive singleton reward and
  cannot equal the zero target.  This matches
  `positiveSingletonDebtCap_oneOwnerFarkas`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/TerminalFunding/OneOwnerFarkas.lean`).
- The coefficient `M` in `(N210)` is sharp at the endpoint level.  Take two
  players `i,j`, let `r_i({i})=s>0`, `r_i({i,j})=-a<0`, and make all other
  entries zero.  Give `j` hazard `s/(s+a)` and give `i` any interior positive
  hazard.  At continuation zero, player `i`'s forced-Quit endpoint is zero;
  player `j` has two zero endpoints; hence zero is a physical Bellman/Nash
  target (with floor at most zero).  Here `M=s+a` and
  `M*opponentAbsorption_i=s` exactly.  The strict positive hazard of `i`
  makes joint absorption larger, as it should.

**Narrow source audit and exact scope.**  The active endpoint-zero step is
already checked exactly as
`quittingRootQuitPayoff_eq_zero_of_zeroTargetLift`, and the qualitative
negative-collision consequence is checked as
`exists_negativeCollision_of_positiveSingleton_zeroTargetLift`, both in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/TerminalFunding/SupportNecessity.lean`.
The canonical definition in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/RewardBound.lean` is literally
`sum_terminal sum_player |reward terminal player|`, so `(N214)` uses two
distinct summands rather than a generic coordinate bound.  The search around
`HasQuittingFrozenRootLiftFarkasCertificate`,
`strict_terminalDebt_fundingEdge_or_farkas`, and
`positiveSingletonDebtCap_oneOwnerFarkas` found the supplied-root affine
alternative and the complete one-owner obstruction, but no aggregate
automatic-funding lemma for a support containing `P`.  Thus the new content
is the sharp quantitative law `(N210)` and its aggregate `hpays` adapter, not
the endpoint-zero or negative-collision necessity.  The commonly cited `2*M`
endpoint perturbation estimate treats `M` as a generic uniform bound; the
improvement to `M` here uses the special global-sum definition and the fact
that the singleton and collision entries are distinct.

Proposition 85 does **not** produce a feasible root, rule out the Farkas
branch, construct a return, or cover a positive punishment floor without the
existing floor-admissible lift.  It changes the exact universal obligation to:

```text
find one zero-target physical product root with P subset support,
or combine the Farkas certificates excluding every such root.              (N215)
```

No hazard magnitude or additional `hpays` proof is needed after that.

Next concrete check: independently verify the use of the global absolute-sum
reward bound in `(N214)` and the active-endpoint-zero implication; then test
whether the Farkas family on supports containing `P` has a common dual
normalization or a minimal exact two-player obstruction outside the already
dispatched solo branch.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 44 checked the named endpoint-zero
theorem, the two-distinct-coordinate use of the global absolute-sum bound,
the aggregate `hpays` quantifiers, and both exact tests.  It found no missing
factor and judged Proposition 85 valid ordinary mathematics.  The surviving
obligation remains simultaneous physical-root/Farkas feasibility and a
source-matched return; magnitude calibration alone is now removed.

### 66. The Solan--Vieille table kills simultaneous zero-target funding but not recurrence

Proposition 85 reduces the incoming-edge magnitude problem to support and
physical feasibility.  The tempting universal completion of that result is
false even on the central four-player residual-hard calibration, outside
every stationary exact terminal branch.

Let `r=SolanVieilleBoundary.boundaryReward` on `I=Fin 4`, and let

```text
P={i | 0<r_i({i})}.
```

The checked table has `r_i({i})=1` for every `i`, hence `P=I`.  Write
`chi_i=quittingPunishmentValue(r,i)`.

**Proposition 86 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed exact floor
obstruction and raw-root witness).**  The following statements hold.

1. `chi_i=0` for every player.
2. There do not exist a root, support, and continuation satisfying

   ```text
   P subset support,
   IsQuittingRootInteriorOnSupport root support,
   IsQuittingFrozenRootContinuationLift
     r 0 chi quittingRewardBound(r) root support continuation.      (N216)
   ```

3. The obstruction in clause 2 is exactly the punishment floor.  If the
   lower continuation bound is dropped, put

   ```text
   p=1/sqrt(2),
   root_i(Quit)=p       for every i,
   z_i=-10-8sqrt(2)     for every i.                              (N217)
   ```

   Then the full-support root has Bellman target zero and every active
   endpoint is indifferent.  Moreover `-48<z_i<0` and
   `quittingRewardBound(r)=48`, so it satisfies the ordinary payoff box but
   violates precisely the lower floor `0<=z_i`.

4. This failure is not a sure-exit or stationary escape.  The table is in
   `ResidualHardClass`, no stationary product profile is exact terminal Nash,
   and the checked two-row period-two block nevertheless supplies a uniform-
   equilibrium payoff.

**Proof of clause 1.**  The continue floor of each player is zero.  Every
terminal reward to `i` at a coalition excluding `i` is nonnegative, and the
coalition of all three opponents pays `i` exactly zero.  Thus the finite
minimum defining `quittingContinueFloor r i` is zero.  Against the pure row
where all three opponents Quit, player `i` receives zero by Continue and
`-1` by joining, so the stationary unilateral cap is zero.  Therefore
`quittingPunishmentValue_eq_continueFloor_of_pureRow`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/ContinueFloor.lean`) gives
`chi_i=0`.  This is also the direct behavioral minmax calculation: Never
guarantees a nonnegative excluded-player payoff, while the all-opponents-Quit
row caps every reply by zero.

**Proof of clause 2.**  Since `P=I`, the support in `(N216)` is all of `I`,
and every hazard is strictly between zero and one.  Fix player `i` and its
same-pair partner `j` (`0<->1`, `2<->3`).  The named checked theorem
`quittingRootQuitPayoff_eq_zero_of_zeroTargetLift`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/TerminalFunding/SupportNecessity.lean`)
makes player `i`'s forced-Quit endpoint zero.  Active endpoint equality, or
equivalently the lift's zero Mobius derivative, then makes its forced-
Continue endpoint zero as well.

But the forced-Continue expectation is strictly positive.  The event on
which only partner `j` Quits has positive product probability, and it pays
player `i` exactly `4`.  Every other nonempty opponent coalition pays `i`
nonnegatively, while the all-opponents-Continue term is the continuation
`z_i>=chi_i=0`.  Hence the expectation is positive, a contradiction.

**Proof and exact check for clause 3.**  For one owner, conditional on that
owner being forced to Quit and with the other three hazards all equal to
`p`, the terminal rewards by opponent-coalition size are:

```text
size 0: 1;
size 1: 1,1,1;
size 2: 1,0,0 in some order;
size 3: -1.
```

Consequently its forced-Quit payoff is

```text
(1-p)^3+3p(1-p)^2+p^2(1-p)-p^3=1-2p^2=0.             (N218)
```

Conditional on forced Continue, the three singleton-opponent rewards sum to
`4`, the three pair-opponent rewards sum to `2`, and the all-three-opponent
reward is zero.  Thus the endpoint is

```text
(1-p)^3 z_i+4p(1-p)^2+2p^2(1-p).                     (N219)
```

Since `p/(1-p)=sqrt(2)+1`, the value in `(N217)` makes `(N219)` zero.
Both endpoints are therefore zero for every player, so their product mixture
has Bellman target zero and every full-support derivative vanishes.  Direct
row summation in the displayed table gives

```text
quittingRewardBound(r)=4*5+(4+3+3+3+3+4)+4*1+4=48.   (N220)
```

The inequalities `-48<-10-8sqrt(2)<0` are immediate from
`1<sqrt(2)<3/2`.  Hence the upper and symmetric payoff-box bounds hold; only
the production lower floor fails.

Clause 4 is exactly
`periodTwo_residualHard_fullCore_nonstationary_but_uniform`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonResidualHard.lean`),
together with the checked block construction in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonPeriodTwo.lean`.
In particular, absence of a sure-exit set follows already because every pure
sure-exit root would be a stationary exact terminal Nash profile.

**Exact conjecture-facing consequence.**  Proposition 85 does remove charge
calibration, but `(N215)` is not a viable universal one-row producer even
after imposing residual-hard data, full positive-solo support, and excluding
stationary exact equilibria.  The Solan--Vieille table lands in the Farkas
branch for every root whose support contains `P`, while its checked period-two
block closes the game.  The weakest surviving target is therefore not
"one physical root or a contradiction" but:

```text
one physical positive-solo funding root,
or a finite moving-payoff block whose phases collectively fund the cap,
or an all-behavior obstruction to every such block.                         (N221)
```

This is not a game counterexample or a new existence class.  It is an exact
minimal obstruction to collapsing the universal moving-source problem into
one frozen zero-target lift.  The raw witness `(N217)` also pinpoints the
missing resource: the periodic construction **does not** transport the raw
negative continuation.  It moves the Bellman target, and every actually
reached continuation coordinate is at least one and hence floor-admissible.
The raw `z<0` witnesses only why the target cannot remain frozen at zero.

Next concrete check: recast the checked Solan--Vieille period-two block as a
two-phase analogue of Proposition 85 and identify an aggregate funding law
that charges the negative raw continuation at one phase against the positive
floor slack at the other.  Kill this route if the exact two-phase identities
still require a separately supplied returned payoff rather than deriving it
from the Farkas data.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 45 independently computed the continue
floor and pure-row punishment cap, all forced-Quit and forced-Continue rows,
the symmetric raw root `(N217)`, and the exact reward-bound sum.  It also
rechecked the named residual-hard, stationary-nonexistence, and period-two
consumer.  Verdict: Proposition 86 is valid ordinary mathematics and sharply
closes the universal one-row zero-target funding step at its stated scope.
The review supplied the interpretive correction incorporated above: the
period-two repair raises and moves the target while keeping all reached
continuations floor-admissible; it does not carry the negative raw `z`.

### 67. Clean-negative hyperedges are necessary for zero-target funding

The Solan--Vieille contradiction is an instance of a general support law,
not an accident of the quartic period-two parameters.  It combines the two
endpoint equations which a frozen Farkas certificate keeps separate.

Fix a zero-target physical lift with root `root`, interior support `S`,
continuation `z`, and lower floor `floor`.  Fix an active owner `i in S` and
assume

```text
0<=floor_i,
r_i(U)>=0 for every nonempty opponent coalition U with i notin U.  (N222)
```

Call a nonempty `T subset S\{i}` a **clean negative funding hyperedge** for
`i` if

```text
r_i(T union {i})<0,
r_i(U)=0 for every nonempty U subset T.                       (N223)
```

**Proposition 87 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed clean-negative
support necessity).**  Under `(N222)`, every zero-target physical lift which
actively mixes a player with `r_i({i})>0` satisfies:

1. `z_i=0`;
2. `r_i(U)=0` for every nonempty `U subset S\{i}`; and
3. `S\{i}` contains a clean negative funding hyperedge `T`.

Equivalently, if every negative owner-containing coalition
`T union {i}` has some nonempty subcoalition `U subset T` with
`r_i(U)>0`, then no zero-target floor-admissible root can actively mix `i`.

**Proof.**  The checked endpoint-zero theorem makes the forced-Quit endpoint
zero, and the active Mobius equality makes the forced-Continue endpoint zero.
Under forced Continue, every possible absorbing coalition excludes `i`, so
all its terminal rewards are nonnegative by `(N222)`; the no-absorption term
is `z_i>=floor_i>=0`.

Interior support gives positive probability to every exact coalition
`U subset S\{i}`: all owners in `U` Quit, every other supported opponent
Continues, and every owner outside `S` surely Continues.  In particular the
empty coalition has positive probability.  A finite sum of nonnegative
terms equals the zero forced-Continue endpoint.  Hence the empty term gives
`z_i=0`, and each nonempty term gives clause 2.

Now force `i` to Quit.  The empty-opponent event again has positive
probability and pays `r_i({i})>0`.  The total forced-Quit expectation is zero,
so some nonempty opponent coalition `T subset S\{i}` with positive product
mass must satisfy `r_i(T union {i})<0`.  Clause 2 applied to every nonempty
`U subset T` makes this `T` clean, proving clause 3.  This is also a direct
quantifier-sharpening of
`exists_negativeCollision_of_positiveSingleton_zeroTargetLift`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Boundary/Repair/TerminalFunding/SupportNecessity.lean`):
the new information is that every opponent-only subcoalition of the negative
collision must be exactly floor-neutral.

**Sharp positive test.**  In a two-player table let

```text
r_i({i})=s_i>0,       r_i({j})=0,
r_i({i,j})=-a_i<0
```

for each owner, take floor and continuation zero, and give opponent `j`
hazard `s_i/(s_i+a_i)`.  The forced-Quit endpoint is zero and the forced-
Continue endpoint is identically zero.  Thus `T={j}` is clean and all three
conclusions are attained; the theorem does not overexclude physical
collision funding.

**Solan--Vieille falsifier of the one-row producer.**  For every player `i`
in `boundaryReward`, all excluded-player rewards are nonnegative and the
floor is zero.  The only negative reward at a coalition containing `i` is the
grand coalition, so any candidate `T` in clause 3 is all three opponents.
But the same-pair partner `j` is a singleton subcoalition with
`r_i({j})=4>0`, contradicting cleanliness.  This recovers Proposition 86
without solving the raw product equations.

**Conjecture-facing boundary.**  A Farkas family excluding all one-row lifts
need not be combined at the level of abstract multipliers.  Under the common
nonnegative-floor/nonnegative-excluded-payoff regime it first decodes to a
finite signed support hypergraph: active positive-solo owners require a
negative joined hyperedge whose entire opponent-only down-set is zero.  The
Solan--Vieille table has no such hyperedge but does have a checked two-phase
cycle.  Therefore a universal producer must either find a clean hyperedge or
move the payoff/floor before activating the blocked owner.  Merely using
several zero-target roots cannot repair the obstruction.

Next concrete check: formulate the target-shifted version of Proposition 87.
For current value `x_i` and continuation `z_i`, determine the exact slack
budget which permits a positive opponent-only subcoalition in one phase and
show how the next phase's Bellman motion repays it.  Test the formula on the
odd/even Solan--Vieille block before proposing any universal cycle theorem.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 46 independently checked the finite
product-atom positivity, both endpoint-mixture implications, the clean
negative hyperedge decoding, and the sharp two-player and Solan--Vieille
tests.  It found Proposition 87 valid ordinary mathematics with no objection.

### 68. Exact target-shift funding ledger on a returned block

The target-shifted replacement for Proposition 87 is an equality, not a sign
condition.  It makes precise how a later phase can repay the opponent-only
deficit which forced a zero-target continuation below the floor.

Consider a finite cyclic exact Nash--Bellman block of length `L>0`, with
current payoffs `X_t`, successor `X_(t+1)` (indices modulo `L`), and product
root `p_t`.  Assume every player has positive prescribed Continue probability
at every phase.  For player `i`, let

```text
mu_(t,i)(U) = probability that exactly the opponents U Quit at phase t,
c_(t,i)     = mu_(t,i)(empty)>0.                              (N224)
```

Define the opponent-only deficit and excess relative to the **current**
target by

```text
D_(t,i)=sum_(U nonempty) mu_(t,i)(U)[X_(t,i)-r_i(U)]^+,
E_(t,i)=sum_(U nonempty) mu_(t,i)(U)[r_i(U)-X_(t,i)]^+.
                                                                    (N225)
```

**Proposition 88 (proved ordinary mathematics; unreviewed exact moving-target
ledger).**  Every phase and player satisfy

```text
c_(t,i)(X_(t+1,i)-X_(t,i))=D_(t,i)-E_(t,i).          (N226)
```

Consequently every returned block satisfies the exact aggregate balance

```text
sum_(t<L) D_(t,i)/c_(t,i)
  =sum_(t<L) E_(t,i)/c_(t,i)                          (N227)
```

for every player `i`.  If player `i` also has positive Quit probability at
phase `t`, its joined-coalition ledger is independently

```text
sum_U mu_(t,i)(U)[r_i(U union {i})-X_(t,i)]=0.        (N228)
```

In particular, when `X_(t,i)=r_i({i})`, the empty-coalition term in `(N228)`
vanishes: collision join effects must balance, while `(N226)` accounts for
the distinct opponent-only/preemption cost of moving the continuation.

**Proof.**  Exact endpoint Nash makes forced Quit and forced Continue no
larger than the Bellman current value.  The Bellman value is their convex
mixture.  Since the prescribed Continue probability is positive, forced
Continue must equal `X_(t,i)`.  Expanding it over the opponent coalition law
gives

```text
X_(t,i)=c_(t,i)X_(t+1,i)
          +sum_(U nonempty)mu_(t,i)(U)r_i(U).         (N229)
```

Subtract `X_(t,i)` times the total opponent absorption probability.  Splitting
each difference into positive and negative parts yields `(N226)`.  Divide by
the positive `c_(t,i)` and sum around the returned block; the payoff
increments telescope to zero, proving `(N227)`.  If the prescribed Quit
probability is also positive, the same convex-mixture argument makes forced
Quit equal the current value.  Expanding that endpoint gives `(N228)`.

**Exact Solan--Vieille calculation.**  Put

```text
a=periodTwoParameter,
b=periodTwoSecondary.
```

For player `0` at the odd phase, the only active opponent is player `2`, its
Continue probability is `b`, its singleton reward to player `0` is zero, and

```text
X_odd,0=1,              X_even,0=1/b.
```

Therefore

```text
c_odd,0=b,
D_odd,0-E_odd,0=1-b,
(D_odd,0-E_odd,0)/c_odd,0=1/b-1.                    (N230)
```

At the even phase, player `0` is inactive while players `1,3` are active;
their joint Continue probability is `ab`, and the successor returns to
`X_odd,0=1`.  Hence `(N226)` gives

```text
c_even,0=ab,
D_even,0-E_even,0=ab(1-1/b)=a(b-1),
(D_even,0-E_even,0)/c_even,0=1-1/b.                 (N231)
```

The normalized quantities in `(N230)--(N231)` cancel exactly.  The sign is
structural: the odd cross-pair singleton reward `0` creates the upward
deficit, while the even same-pair singleton reward `4` makes the later excess
large enough to pull the coordinate back down.  All actual phase values are
`1`, `1/a`, or `1/b`, hence at least the punishment floor zero.  No negative
continuation is transported.

The active joined ledger is even simpler.  At the odd phase player `0` can
only collide with player `2`, and both its singleton and `{0,2}` rewards are
one, equal to `X_odd,0`; thus `(N228)` vanishes term by term.  The same holds
for player `2`, and symmetrically at the even phase for players `1,3`.

**Source/novelty audit and exact scope.**  The row identity is implicit in
the checked endpoint/Bellman definitions and the repository's `gainValue`
coordinate formula.  A narrow search for `forcedContinue`, `target shift`,
`cycle deficit`, and `excludedValue` found no named declaration stating the
cycle balance `(N227)` or its deficit/excess interpretation.  The exact
Solan--Vieille ingredients are the checked `oddRoot_successor`,
`evenRoot_successor`, endpoint-difference theorems, and phase definitions in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Examples/BlockPair/FourPlayerPairedSingletonPeriodTwo.lean`.

Proposition 88 is a necessary ledger for a supplied returned block, not a
producer of one and not a semantic consumer beyond the already checked block
compiler.  Its conjecture-facing value is to replace the false one-row
funding objective with an exact finite-cycle obligation: every upward
opponent-only deficit must be matched by a later survival-normalized excess
for the same player, while the separate active joined ledgers and endpoint
Nash inequalities remain valid at the reached sources.

Next concrete check: attempt to derive `(N227)` from the family of one-row
Farkas certificates on all supports, or produce a residual-hard table where
every clean-negative support fails and the signed target-shift ledgers have a
strict separating covector.  The latter would meet the route's kill criterion
only if the separation survives arbitrary finite lengths and simultaneous
quitters, not merely the two-phase ansatz.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 46 independently reconstructed the
forced-Continue and forced-Quit mixture arguments, the positive `c_(t,i)`
division, the cyclic telescope, and the exact `a,b` cancellation in the
