### 69. A charged payoff near-return must leave the diffuse tight face

The moving-target ledger does not by itself produce a returned block.  On the
residual-hard boundary there is, however, one exact universal consequence:
the strict singleton separator survives every product row whose conditional
collision mass is small.  This turns the open paid payoff-near-return producer
into a three-way escape obligation.

Use the data of Gauss Proposition 54.  Thus `b` is the limiting payoff,

```text
s_i=r_i({i}),       E={i | b_i=s_i},
```

and there are a covector `ell` and `kappa>0` such that, for every probability
vector `mu` supported on `E`,

```text
ell dot (b-sum_(j in E) mu_j r({j})) >= kappa.       (N232)
```

This is exactly `(GC5)` after expanding the normalized singleton matrix.  Put

```text
L=sum_i |ell_i|,
M=quittingRewardBound(r),
B=max(1,M),
epsilon_0=kappa/(4L),
zeta_0=min(1/2,kappa/(8BL)).                          (N233)
```

The separating covector is nonzero, hence `L>0`; also `B>=1`, so both displayed
scales are positive.

For a product root `q` with positive absorption `Q`, let

```text
zeta(q)=quittingRootCollisionMass(q)/Q,              (N234)
```

the conditional probability that at least two players Quit, given that the
row absorbs.  When `Q=0`, put `zeta(q)=0`.

**Proposition 89 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed local escape
trichotomy).**  Let `path` be any finite exact punishment-floor admissible
path, oriented as the charged relation from each Bellman tail to its current
state.  Suppose every state payoff of `path` lies in the sup-norm
`epsilon_0`-ball about `b`, and every positive Quit coordinate of every edge
root belongs to `E`.  Write

```text
Collision(path)=sum_edges quittingRootCollisionMass(root).   (N235)
```

Then the stronger aggregate inequality is

```text
ell dot (payoff(source)-payoff(target))
 >= (3*kappa/4)*path.chargeSum-2BL*Collision(path).           (N236)
```

If in addition `zeta(q)<=zeta_0` at every edge, then

```text
ell dot (payoff(source)-payoff(target))
  >= (kappa/2)*path.chargeSum.                       (N237)
```

Consequently, if `path` contains an edge of absorption at least `a>0`, then

```text
||payoff(source)-payoff(target)||_infinity
  >= kappa*a/(2L)                                    (N238)
```

under the conditional-collision bound.  Without that rowwise bound, endpoint
closeness at scale

```text
||payoff(source)-payoff(target)||_infinity <= kappa*a/(4L)
```

forces the fixed cumulative collision budget

```text
Collision(path) >= kappa*a/(4BL).                    (N239)
```

In particular, a
`QuittingPositiveAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily` of threshold `a` cannot,
at all endpoint tolerances, satisfy both local-face conditions above.  For
every tolerance smaller than `kappa*a/(2L)`, its selected path must do at
least one of the following:

```text
- leave the epsilon_0 payoff neighborhood of b;
- activate a Quit coordinate outside E;
- contain a row with conditional collision fraction greater than zeta_0.
                                                               (N240)
```

If the first two alternatives never occur and the endpoint error is at most
`kappa*a/(4L)`, the stronger conclusion `(N239)` says that the whole path
carries a fixed **absolute**, not merely conditional, simultaneous-quitter
mass.

There is a literal one-row consequence in the counterexample branch.  Let
`P_0` be the checked global punishment-floor prefix-charge bound and put

```text
gamma=kappa*a/(4BLP_0),
C_pair=choose(card(I),2).                              (N239a)
```

The high edge makes `0<a<=P_0`, so `gamma>0`.  If the first two escape arms
are absent and the endpoint error is at most `kappa*a/(4L)`, then some edge
`q` of the path satisfies

```text
zeta(q)>=gamma,
Q(q)>=gamma/C_pair,
quittingRootCollisionMass(q)>=gamma^2/C_pair.        (N239b)
```

Here `C_pair>0` is forced by `(N239)`; if `card(I)<2`, the local near-return
is impossible.  Thus the collision arm contains one macroscopic exact
floor-admissible Nash--Bellman row, not only many vanishing collision atoms.

**Proof.**  It is enough to prove the one-edge inequality.  Write `y` for
the tail payoff, `x` for the current payoff, `Q` for the row absorption, and
`D` for its full absorbing delivery.  The named affine identity
`IsQuittingNashBellmanEdge.eq_collisionAwareSegment`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/CollisionAwareFiniteReturn.lean`) gives

```text
x=(1-Q)y+QD,
y-x=Q(y-D).                                           (N241)
```

If `Q=0`, the desired contribution is zero.  Suppose `Q>0`.  When `zeta<1`,
normalize the exact-singleton atoms to a probability vector `mu`; the support
hypothesis makes `mu` supported on `E`.  When `zeta=1`, choose any probability
vector supported on the nonempty set `E`; its coefficient below is zero.
If `R_single` is the resulting singleton reward mixture and `R_collision` is
the conditional mixture of all multi-quitter rewards, then exactly

```text
D=(1-zeta)R_single+zeta R_collision.                 (N242)
```

Every terminal coordinate has absolute value at most the global absolute-sum
bound `M`, so

```text
||D-R_single||_infinity <= 2M zeta.                  (N243)
```

For any vector `v`,
`|ell dot v|<=L||v||_infinity`.  Equations `(N232)--(N233)` and the local
payoff hypothesis therefore give

```text
ell dot (y-D)
 >= kappa-L||y-b||_infinity-2ML zeta
 >= 3*kappa/4-2BL zeta.                              (N244)
```

Multiply by `Q` in `(N241)` and use `Q*zeta=collisionMass`.  Summing over the
relation path telescopes the intermediate payoffs and proves `(N236)`.  If
every `zeta<=zeta_0`, then

```text
Collision(path)<=zeta_0*path.chargeSum,
```

and the definition of `zeta_0` gives `(N237)`.  A high edge makes
`path.chargeSum>=a`; applying
`|ell dot v|<=L||v||_infinity` once more proves `(N238)`.  For `(N239)`, use
the same absolute-value bound on the left of `(N236)`, the endpoint-error
assumption, and `path.chargeSum>=a`:

```text
2BL*Collision(path)
 >= 3*kappa*a/4-kappa*a/4=kappa*a/2.
```

For `(N239b)`, every admissible path has
`path.chargeSum<=P_0` by the checked finite-prefix charge bound and exact path
decoder.  Since

```text
Collision(path)=sum_edges Q(q)zeta(q),
```

the weighted-average bound `(N239)` gives one edge with
`zeta(q)>=Collision(path)/path.chargeSum>=gamma`.  The checked product-law
estimate
`quittingRootCollisionMass_le_choose_card_mul_absorption_sq`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/CollisionConcentration.lean`)
gives `zeta(q)<=C_pair Q(q)`.  Hence
`Q(q)>=gamma/C_pair` and
`collisionMass(q)=Q(q)zeta(q)>=gamma^2/C_pair`, as claimed.

The endpoint-close contrapositive is `(N240)`.  Notice that exact Nash and
punishment floors are retained because the input is the literal admissible
relation; the estimate uses only its Bellman identity.

**Boundary tests.**

- On a genuinely singleton row, `zeta=0`, so `(N244)` has no collision error.
  A finite concatenation of such local tight-face rows is strictly monotone
  in the same `ell` coordinate and cannot return.
- The sharp clean-negative two-player stationary root from Proposition 87 is
  not a counterexample: its zero-payoff self-loop would force the separator
  `(N232)` to fail.  Equivalently, that singleton matrix has the corresponding
  homogeneous witness.
- The checked Solan--Vieille repair uses two simultaneously active owners in
  each phase.  Its product rows therefore possess a literal collision atom;
  Proposition 89 identifies this as one of the only mechanisms by which a
  local singleton drift argument can fail.  The theorem does not assert that
  its collision fraction exceeds the particular `zeta_0` attached to an
  unrelated boundary point.

**Exact source and consumer scope.**  The affine decomposition and its exact
cycle consumer are already checked in
`CollisionAwareFiniteReturn.lean`.  The strict separator `(N232)` is the
ordinary-mathematics core of independently reviewed Gauss Proposition 54,
derived from the absence of a production-normal homogeneous simplex witness.
The varying-edge near-return consumer is
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_admissiblePath_payoffNearReturns`
and its packaged family is `QuittingPositiveAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/PunishmentFloorNearReturn.lean`).

Proposition 89 does not construct a near-return, a collision gadget, or a
source-matched path.  Its universal content is that the paid near-return
consumer cannot be supplied by a diffuse local recurrence on the residual-
hard tight face, even when the active support changes arbitrarily inside that
face.  One of the three nonperturbative mechanisms in `(N240)` is necessary,
and the collision arm must carry the absolute budget `(N239)` if the other two
arms are absent.  In the counterexample branch, the global prefix-capacity
bound upgrades that budget to the literal macroscopic row `(N239b)`.
This is stronger than a fixed-support or fixed-horizon obstruction.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 47 independently reconstructed the
relation orientation, the `Q=0` and `zeta=1` boundary cases, the aggregate
telescope `(N236)`, both collision conclusions, the exact near-return
quantifiers, and the prefix-capacity localization `(N239b)`.  It found
Proposition 89 valid ordinary mathematics with no objection.  The review
confirms the stated scope: this is an escape obstruction, not a producer of
the nonlocal excursion, outside-face owner, or macroscopic collision row.

Next concrete check: test the simultaneous-reset/incentive-gadget interfaces
against the third arm of `(N240)`.  The route succeeds only if a literal
positive conditional-collision row can be Nashified at its actually reached
tail and then connected to a payoff near-return.  Kill this branch if the
available atom/reset data controls collision probability only at frozen
sources or only before enforcing every endpoint inequality.

### 70. A semantic collision row must leave the positive minimum-debt fiber

Proposition 89 leaves a macroscopic simultaneous-quitter row as one possible
escape from the singleton-face separator.  The terminal-semantic source
geometry gives that row a second, independent price.  This price is exact
only when the product root and its continuation payoff have literal semantic
provenance; that qualification is the point of the statement.

Let `minimum` be a member of the terminal-semantic carrier which minimizes
total debt there.  Write

```text
D_* = quittingTerminalSemanticDebtSum(minimum)>0.          (N245)
```

Call an exact Nash--Bellman edge `current -> tail` **semantically lifted** if
there is a terminal-semantic pair `pair` in the carrier with

```text
pair.payoff=tail.payoff.                                   (N246)
```

Only the payoff equality is needed: root Nash depends on the continuation
payoff, while `pair` supplies the debt coordinates and carrier provenance.

**Proposition 90 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed semantic-lift
obstruction).**  Let `current -> tail` be an exact Nash--Bellman edge and let
`pair` be a semantic lift as in `(N246)`.  If `q` is the product root stored
at `current`, then

```text
quittingRootCollisionMass(q) * D_*
  <= quittingTerminalSemanticDebtSum(pair)-D_*.            (N247)
```

Consequently an exact collision row of mass at least `c>0` can have literal
terminal-semantic root--tail provenance only at a shifted tail whose total
debt exceeds the minimum by at least `c D_*`.

In particular, combine `(N247)` with the one-row conclusion `(N239b)`.  In
the local-face collision arm of Proposition 89, any semantic lift of the
selected row obeys

```text
quittingTerminalSemanticDebtSum(pair)-D_*
  >= D_* gamma^2/C_pair.                                  (N248)
```

The right side is one fixed positive constant, independent of the endpoint
tolerance in the payoff-near-return family.  Thus a source-matched collision
producer cannot remain in an arbitrarily small neighborhood of the positive
minimum-debt fiber.

**Proof.**  Exact endpoint Nash in the edge is exact root Nash by
`isZeroQuittingRootEndpointNash_iff_isZeroQuittingRootNash`.  Transport it
across `(N246)` and apply
`terminalSemantic_literalNash_excess_support_le_card_mul`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/TerminalSemanticLiteralNashDebtSupport.lean`)
with `epsilon=0`.  If `D` is the total debt of `pair` and `C` is the root
collision mass, the result is

```text
D C + sum_i mass({i}) (D-debt_i(pair)) <= D-D_*.           (N249)
```

Every semantic debt coordinate is nonnegative, so `D-debt_i(pair)>=0` for
each `i`; every singleton mass is nonnegative.  The sum in `(N249)` is
therefore nonnegative, giving `D C<=D-D_*`.  Minimality gives `D_*<=D`, and
collision mass is nonnegative, hence `D_* C<=D C`.  This proves `(N247)`.
Equation `(N248)` follows from the lower collision bound in `(N239b)`.

If the row is an actual live row of a behavioral profile and its Bellman tail
is the payoff of the shifted all-Continue spine, the lift is automatic:
`quittingTerminalSemanticPair_mem_carrier` supplies the corresponding
semantic pair.  Conversely, changing the continuation payoff while
Nashifying the root may destroy `(N246)`; Proposition 90 deliberately does
not identify counterfactual and reached tails.

**Boundary tests.**

- If `D_*=0`, `(N247)` is vacuous.  This is the correct zero-debt boundary,
  where the minimum-fiber collision floor carries no coercive information.
- If `pair=minimum` and `D_*>0`, exact Nash forces collision mass zero.  This
  agrees with
  `minimumTerminalSemantic_debtSum_mul_collisionMass_le_literalDefect`
  in the same source file and exact zero total Nash defect.
- The positive-target rectangle row has literal profile provenance but a
  fixed positive best-endpoint gain.  The checked
  `positiveTargetReachedRowLocalization_no_exactNashBellmanEmbedding`
  (`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/StoppingLaw/OffDiagonal/PureTimeReachedRowLocalization.lean`)
  therefore places it on the other side of the same boundary: it is not an
  exact edge on its unchanged root--tail fiber.
- The Solan--Vieille period-two block is not contradicted.  Proposition 90
  does not assert that its reached continuations lie on a positive
  terminal-semantic minimum fiber.

**Exact scope and source audit.**  The checked
`causalCollision_tailEscape_or_quantitativeBestEndpoint`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/TerminalSemanticLawCarrierCausalNashDispatch.lean`)
already proves the stronger actual-profile collision-row dichotomy: a marked
collision is paid either by shifted-tail debt escape or by one legal reached-
row deviation, and it records the cap--Nash return account on the escape arm.
Proposition 90 does not duplicate that producer-facing actual-row theorem.
Its new content here is only the adapter from an arbitrary exact Nash--Bellman
edge carrying an independently supplied semantic lift, followed by the
specific quantitative composition with Proposition 89's macroscopic row.
No named declaration found in the narrow search supplies such a lift for an
arbitrary admissible path.  Proposition 90 does not produce the lift, the
high-debt excursion, or a return from that excursion.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 48 independently checked endpoint-to-
root Nash transport across the payoff equality, the exact zero-error support
budget, complementary-debt nonnegativity, `D_* C<=D C`, the actual shifted-
profile lift, and the constants in the composition with Proposition 89.  It
found Proposition 90 valid ordinary mathematics with no objection and
confirmed that the semantic lift remains a genuine hypothesis.

The universal producer obligation is now nonlocal in both accounts.  A
successful local-face payoff near-return must either activate outside the
tight singleton face or pass through a semantic tail at fixed positive debt
height and subsequently return in payoff.  The conditioned packet
reprojection problem must therefore preserve a reached root--tail law across
that excursion; a frozen minimum-fiber reset cannot close the route.

Next concrete check: test whether the executable positive-incidence
reprojection germ supplies a semantically lifted **high-debt** tail before its
vanishing-debt contact, and whether its actual profile sequence can be ordered
into one exact Nash--Bellman excursion and payoff return.  Kill the local
collision route if every available exactification either keeps the literal
tail and pays the fixed Nash defect, or changes the tail without a reached
profile whose total debt crosses the bound `(N248)`.

### 71. One source lift pays the collision mass of the whole exact path

The one-row formulation of Proposition 90 understates the amount of semantic
provenance needed for a finite exact path.  A separate lift at every edge is
unnecessary.  Prefix invariance of the terminal-semantic carrier propagates
one lift at the relation source through all later relation states.

Let a finite exact Nash--Bellman path be written in relation orientation

```text
x_0 --q_0--> x_1 --q_1--> ... --q_(m-1)--> x_m,     (N250)
```

so `x_t` is the Bellman tail, `x_(t+1)` is the current payoff, and `q_t` is
the product root stored at the current state.  Thus

```text
x_(t+1)=quittingRootSuccessorPayoff(r,x_t,q_t),
q_t is exact endpoint Nash against x_t.                         (N251)
```

Write

```text
C_t=quittingRootCollisionMass(q_t),
Collision(path)=sum_(t<m) C_t.                                 (N252)
```

Suppose only that the source payoff has a carrier lift: there is
`pair_0` in the terminal-semantic carrier with `pair_0.1=x_0`.  Define
recursively

```text
pair_(t+1)=quittingTerminalSemanticPrefix(r,q_t,pair_t),
D_t=quittingTerminalSemanticDebtSum(pair_t).                    (N253)
```

**Proposition 91 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed
semantic path telescope).**  Every `pair_t` in `(N253)` belongs to the terminal-semantic
carrier and satisfies `pair_t.1=x_t`.  If `D_*>0` is the minimum total debt
from Section 70, then

```text
C_t D_t <= D_t-D_(t+1)                   for every t<m,          (N254)
D_* Collision(path) <= D_0-D_m <= D_0-D_*.                      (N255)
```

Consequently a positive-collision exact path starting on the minimum fiber
is impossible.  More quantitatively, under all local-face hypotheses of
Proposition 89, if the path contains a row of absorption at least `a>0` and

```text
||x_0-x_m||_infinity <= kappa*a/(4L),                           (N256)
```

then a semantic lift of `x_0` must satisfy

```text
D_0-D_* >= D_* kappa*a/(4BL).                                  (N257)
```

Thus the local-face payoff-near-return family cannot begin at semantic
sources tending to the positive minimum-debt fiber.  It must instead begin
at a fixed debt height, leave the singleton face, or fail to retain terminal-
semantic source provenance.

**Proof.**  Induct along `(N250)`.  If `pair_t` is in the carrier, then
`quittingTerminalSemanticPrefix_mem_carrier`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Root/TerminalSemanticPair.lean`) puts
`pair_(t+1)` in the carrier.  Its first coordinate is the root successor of
`pair_t.1`; `(N251)` therefore gives `pair_(t+1).1=x_(t+1)`.
Endpoint Nash is root Nash by
`isZeroQuittingRootEndpointNash_iff_isZeroQuittingRootNash`, so it transports
across `pair_t.1=x_t` without changing the root.

Fix a player `i` and write `d_(t,i)` for its debt in `pair_t`, and
`c_(t,-i)` for its opponent-absorption mass at `q_t`.  Every debt coordinate
is nonnegative because `pair_t` is in the carrier.  The collision event
`{at least two players Quit}` is contained in the event `{some opponent of i
Quits}`.  Equivalently, summing the exact product masses of all coalitions of
cardinality at least two gives the elementary row inequality

```text
0 <= C_t <= c_(t,-i)                    for every i.             (N258)
```

The checked defect-or-drift inequality
`sum_opponentAbsorptionMass_mul_debt_le_sumDebt_drift_add_totalNashDefect`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/TerminalSemanticPlateauDefectCharge.lean`)
has zero Nash-defect term here.  Hence

```text
C_t D_t
 = sum_i C_t d_(t,i)
 <= sum_i c_(t,-i) d_(t,i)
 <= D_t-D_(t+1),                                           (N259)
```

which is `(N254)`.  In particular the total debts are nonincreasing.  Since
every `pair_t` remains in the carrier, minimality gives `D_t>=D_*`.  Multiply
`C_t>=0` by this bound, sum `(N254)`, and telescope:

```text
D_* sum_t C_t <= sum_t C_t D_t
                <= D_0-D_m <= D_0-D_*.
```

This proves `(N255)`.  Under `(N256)`, Proposition 89 gives
`Collision(path)>=kappa*a/(4BL)`.  Substitution in `(N255)` proves `(N257)`.

**Boundary and falsification checks.**

- For one player, `C_t=0`, while opponent absorption is also zero; `(N258)`
  and the telescope are exact but vacuous.
- On a singleton-only path, every `C_t=0`; Proposition 91 does not replace
  the strict payoff separator in Proposition 89.  It controls precisely the
  simultaneous-quitter escape arm.
- At `D_*=0`, the aggregate lower bound disappears, just as in Proposition
  90.  No zero-debt theorem is being claimed.
- If `pair_0` is the minimum and `D_*>0`, `(N255)` forces every nonnegative
  `C_t` to vanish.  This recovers the one-row minimum-fiber conclusion at
  every prefix without selecting a separate lift.
- Merely requiring each payoff `x_t` to lie in the projection of the carrier
  is not enough unless the displayed lifts are the recursive prefixes in
  `(N253)`.  The theorem supplies that coherence from the single source lift;
  it does not permit independent carrier selections at later states.

**Exact source and novelty scope.**  Besides carrier invariance, the proof
uses the checked one-row defect-or-drift inequality named above and the
definition of the exact Bellman edge.  The elementary event inclusion
`(N258)` is already packaged directly as
`quittingRootCollisionMass_le_opponentAbsorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/AbsorptionPath/CollisionConcentration.lean`).
The coalition-level ingredient
`quittingRootCoalitionMass_le_opponentAbsorptionMass_of_other_mem` also appears
in the defect-charge file.

`sum_stageCollisionMass_mul_tailDebtSum_le_stoppedDefectExcess`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/TerminalSemanticPlateauDefectTelescope.lean`)
is a distinct actual-profile theorem.  It carries live-mass weights, shifted-
tail excess terms, and occupied local Nash defects.  Proposition 91 instead
concerns an arbitrary finite exact relation path: exact selected roots remove
the defect term, while a supplied source lift supplies the entire coherent
semantic chronology.  It does not construct that source lift, a path, or a
return.

The immediate producer-facing simplification is exact: conditioned packet
reprojection need not preserve an independently chosen semantic pair at
every row.  It suffices to attach the relation source to one actual terminal-
semantic pair and then keep the displayed roots exact against the recursively
prefixed payoffs.  The remaining obstruction is quantitative source
selection: the required local-face near-return must start at excess debt at
least `(N257)` and still return in payoff.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 49 independently checked the relation
orientation, carrier/payoff induction, endpoint-to-root Nash transport, the
named collision-to-opponent-absorption bound, zero total Nash defect, the
semantic-debt telescope, and the constants in the Proposition 89
composition.  It found Proposition 91 valid ordinary mathematics with no
objection.  The review also located the direct checked helper for `(N258)`
named above.  No producer, high-debt source, or payoff return is inferred.

Next concrete check: compare the paid first-disagreement source pair with
`(N257)`.  Determine whether its fixed positive debt separation is uniform at
the separator constants `kappa,a,B,L`, and whether the conditioned packet
construction preserves the exact recursive prefix chronology from that one
source.  Kill this route if its only high-debt pair is counterfactual to the
near-return roots or if seam exactification destroys the source payoff
equality in `(N251)`.

### 72. Favorable cap drops manufacture a second persistent label

The absolute-seam route is semantically rigid: bounded candidate annotations,
summable absolute seams, and vanishing literal clocks already reconstruct an
actual small-debt terminal profile.  The chronological certificate is weaker
because it charges only adverse direct-debt forcing.  Cedar Section 9 isolates
the exact surviving sign: if the donated endpoint cap is at least the next
block's source cap, the seam is favorable even when the cap drop is large.

This apparent freedom is not independent of the literal clocks.  Exact cap
recursion can replenish a bounded cap after repeated favorable drops only by
putting absorption mass on opponents of that cap's player.

Fix a player `i`.  Let the terminal rewards be bounded in absolute value by
`M`.  For block `k`, let

```text
q_(k,t),                 0 <= t < N_k,
b_(k,t,i),               0 <= t <= N_k,
```

be literal product rows and one candidate cap coordinate.  Assume exact
within-block cap recursion

```text
b_(k,t,i)=H_(k,t,i)(b_(k,t+1,i)),                      (N260)
```

where

```text
H_(k,t,i)(z)=max(Q_(k,t,i), C_(k,t,i)+O_(k,t,i) z),    (N261)
```

`O_(k,t,i)` is the probability that every opponent of `i` Continues, and
`C_(k,t,i)` is the unnormalized terminal contribution on the event that `i`
Continues and some opponent Quits.  Thus

```text
|C_(k,t,i)| <= M (1-O_(k,t,i)).                         (N262)
```

Assume one uniform candidate-cap bound

```text
|b_(k,t,i)| <= K.                                       (N263)
```

At seam `k`, write

```text
R_(k,i)=(b_(k,N_k,i)-b_(k+1,0,i))_+,   favorable drop,
V_(k,i)=(b_(k+1,0,i)-b_(k,N_k,i))_+,   reverse violation. (N264)
```

Finally let

```text
A_(k,i)=sum_(t<N_k) (1-O_(k,t,i))                       (N265)
```

be the block's rowwise opponent-absorption budget.  This is a union-bound
budget, not the probability of absorption somewhere in the whole block.

**Proposition 92 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed cap-replenishment
clock adapter).**  For every finite number `n` of seams,

```text
sum_(k<n) R_(k,i)
 <= sum_(k<n) V_(k,i)
    +(K+M) sum_(k<n) A_(k,i)+2K.                        (N266)
```

Consequently, if

```text
sum_k R_(k,i)=infinity,      sum_k V_(k,i)<infinity,    (N267)
```

then

```text
sum_(k,t) (1-O_(k,t,i))=infinity.                       (N268)
```

Because the player set is finite and

```text
1-O_(k,t,i) <= sum_(j!=i) p_(k,t,j),                    (N269)
```

there is one fixed `j!=i` whose marginal Quit-hazard series diverges.  If the
same chronology already has divergent marginal hazard for `i`, then `i,j`
are two distinct persistent labels.  The exact two-label clock criterion
therefore supplies joint and every-player-deleted survival from every suffix.

**Proof.**  From `(N261)`, the max is at least its Continue branch.  Therefore

```text
b_(k,t+1,i)-b_(k,t,i)
 <= (1-O_(k,t,i)) b_(k,t+1,i)-C_(k,t,i).
```

Taking the positive part and using `(N262)--(N263)` gives the one-row bound

```text
(b_(k,t+1,i)-b_(k,t,i))_+
 <= (K+M)(1-O_(k,t,i)).                                 (N270)
```

Summing inside one block yields

```text
b_(k,N_k,i)-b_(k,0,i) <= (K+M)A_(k,i).                 (N271)
```

The signed seam displacement is `R_(k,i)-V_(k,i)`.  Insert and subtract the
block source, then sum over `k<n`:

```text
sum_(k<n)(R_(k,i)-V_(k,i))
 =sum_(k<n)[b_(k,N_k,i)-b_(k,0,i)]
   +b_(0,0,i)-b_(n,0,i).                               (N272)
```

The first term is bounded by `(N271)` and the last two coordinates by `2K`.
Rearrangement proves `(N266)`.  Under `(N267)`, a finite sum in `(N268)`
would make the right side of `(N266)` uniformly bounded, a contradiction.
The event that some opponent Quits is the union of the individual opponent
Quit events, proving `(N269)`.  A divergent finite sum of nonnegative marginal
series has one divergent fixed summand.  This proves the last assertion.

**Boundary and falsification checks.**

- If `O_(k,t,i)=1`, then `C_(k,t,i)=0` and the Continue branch equals the
  successor cap.  Hence a prefix cannot lie below its successor; `(N270)` is
  exact at zero.
- A sure opponent exit has `O=0`, and `(N270)` permits the full bounded cap
  reset at cost at most `K+M`.  No small-probability claim is hidden.
- The reverse-violation summability in `(N267)` is essential.  Alternating
  arbitrary seam rises and drops can have divergent favorable variation with
  zero internal absorption.
- Uniform boundedness is essential.  An endpoint cap growing without bound
  can replenish seam drops through the terminal boundary rather than through
  opponent absorption.
- Divergent opponent absorption produces a fixed second label only because
  the player set is finite.  The selected opponent need not be the same in
  every block, but finite pigeonhole makes one label recur with divergent
  total hazard.
- Proposition 92 does not infer `(N267)` from one favorable seam or from atom
  access.  It says exactly what a repeated cap-drop construction would buy if
  the actual source selection supplies that orientation.

There is an important label-alignment refinement.  In the actual tangent
family the cap coordinate naturally belongs to an observer `i`, while the
already exposed persistent label may be the distinct mover `a`.  Proposition
92's pigeonhole could then return `a` again.  The following excess form states
exactly what is needed to force a genuinely new label.

**Corollary 93 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed excess-pump label
adapter).**  Fix `a!=i`.  For finite `n`, put

```text
P_a(n)=sum_(k<n) sum_(t<N_k) p_(k,t,a).
```

Then

```text
sum_(k<n)R_(k,i)-sum_(k<n)V_(k,i)-2K-(K+M)P_a(n)
 <= (K+M) sum_(k<n)sum_(t<N_k)sum_(j!=i,a)p_(k,t,j).   (N273)
```

Consequently, if the left side of `(N273)` is unbounded above, some fixed
`j` outside `{i,a}` has divergent marginal Quit hazard.  In particular `a,j`
are two distinct persistent labels whenever `a` was the pre-existing mover
stream.

**Proof.**  Refine `(N269)` by separating the known label:

```text
1-O_(k,t,i)
 <= p_(k,t,a)+sum_(j!=i,a)p_(k,t,j).
```

Substitute this in `(N266)` and rearrange to get `(N273)`.  If its left side
is unbounded, the finite sum of the remaining players' cumulative hazards is
unbounded, so one fixed summand diverges.

The excess condition cannot be dropped.  If every opponent absorption is
caused by `a`, the observer cap may be replenished indefinitely while no
third label ever Quits.  This is precisely the mover/observer alignment issue
in the current atom source: a positive observer-cap pump must outrun the
already-counted mover hazard, not merely coexist with it.

The following exact regression shows that this boundary is attained, while
all nonclock fields of the one-sided analytic construction can look ideal.

**Proposition 94 (proved ordinary mathematics; independently reviewed sharp monopoly
example).**  Take two players `{a,i}` and set every terminal reward coordinate
to zero.  Fix `0<h<1`.  At every date let `a` Quit with probability `1-h` and
let `i` Continue surely.  Regard each date as a one-row block.  Give it the
donated endpoint candidate pair

```text
u^-=(0,0),        b^-=(0,1),                            (N274)
```

and use at the block source, and as the next global candidate source,

```text
u^+=(0,0),        b^+=(0,h).                            (N275)
```

Then `(N275)` is the exact prefix of `(N274)` through the displayed root.
The global candidate chain is bounded, has nonnegative debt, and has initial
debt `h` only in coordinate `i`.  Every prescribed defect is zero.  Its only
direct-debt defect is

```text
E_i=h-h^2=h(1-h)>0,                                    (N276)
```

so every adverse direct-forcing sum is nonpositive.  The generated cap
secant is `h`.  At every seam,

```text
R_(k,i)=1-h=p_(k,a),       V_(k,i)=0.                  (N277)
```

Thus the favorable cap drops diverge and are replenished entirely by the one
persistent mover `a`; with the minimal bounds `K=1,M=0`, Corollary 93's
mover-subtracted quantity is the constant `-2`, hence bounded with zero linear
asymptotic excess.  Joint survival
and survival after deleting `i` vanish, but survival after deleting `a` is
identically one.  There is no second persistent label.

For every requested `eta>0`, choosing

```text
h=min(eta/2,1/2)
```

makes the initial candidate debt at most `eta`.  Hence boundedness,
nonnegative debt, small initial debt, prescribed discrepancy, generated
secants, and adverse direct forcing can all hold while precisely one deleted
clock field fails.

**Proof.**  With zero rewards, player `i`'s cap-prefix map is

```text
H_i(z)=h z,
```

because `a` is the only opponent and Continues with probability `h`.  Hence
`H_i(1)=h`, proving the donated-block recursion, while prefixing the actual
next candidate cap gives `H_i(h)=h^2`; their debt difference is `(N276)`.
The prescribed prefix is zero because both the terminal rewards and endpoint
prescribed values are zero.  The affine cap map has secant `h` between the
candidate successor cap `h` and the actual zero semantic cap.  Player `a`'s
candidate coordinates and all its defects are zero.

Equation `(N277)` is immediate from `(N274)--(N275)`.  The only nonzero
marginal series is `sum_k(1-h)` for `a`.  Deleting `a` leaves `i` Continuing
surely; deleting `i` leaves survival `h^n`; joint survival is also `h^n`.
All remaining certificate inequalities follow from zero prescribed defects
and the favorable sign `E_i>0`.

This all-zero game has a trivial uniform payoff and is not a counterexample to
the conjecture.  Its role is exact interface sharpness: observer-cap
replenishment alone cannot turn one persistent mover into two labels.  A
source theorem must either use the mover's own cap coordinate, produce the
strict excess in `(N273)`, or obtain the second label directly from a
non-singleton atom/finite observer response.

**Source and novelty scope.**  The one-row cap formula `(N261)` and the signed
seam orientation are the same elementary terminal-prefix mathematics used in
Cedar Proposition 4.  The two-label conclusion is the exact criterion already
isolated in `questions/PERSISTENT_TWO_LABEL_HAZARDS.md`.  The new content is
their quantitative composition through the telescoping block-cap account
`(N266)`.  No Lean theorem, atom-source adapter, or uniform-equilibrium
producer is claimed.

The producer-facing target is now concrete.  It is enough to orient one fixed
mover's atom/reset blocks so that its cap has divergent favorable seam drops,
only summable reverse violations, and divergent own hazard.  Proposition 92
then manufactures the second persistent label rather than requiring it as a
separate input.  Kill this subroute if actual mover replacement can make the
cap drops favorable only finitely often, or if every divergent favorable-drop
selection necessarily carries nonsummable reverse violations.

**Independent review.**  Gauss Round 50 independently checked the absorbing
contribution bound, max orientation across branch switches, signed block/seam
telescope including the `2K` boundary, divergence implication, and fixed-label
conclusion of Proposition 92.  It found the proposition valid ordinary
mathematics with no objection.  Gauss Round 51 separately checked Corollary
93's refined union-bound subtraction, empty-label boundary, and fixed third-
label conclusion, again with no objection.  Gauss Round 52 checked Proposition
94's donated/global cap distinction, direct defect, generated secant, seam
ledger, and every clock field.  It found the example valid and supplied the
constant `-2` precision recorded above.

### Actual first-row caps and the cost of unilateral earlier fragmentation

This is ordinary mathematics at the actual GLOBAL positive sum-debt
minimum, not a local positive-debt example or a raw UE producer. It uses
the strict margins already named below. The current first-atom source is
being independently checked separately; the assertions here may equally
be read with a supplied FIRST atom in an actual minimum. A supplied atom
is not being advertised as an arbitrary-table producer.

Fix a finite player set, independent private stopping laws and bounded
signed rewards |r_i(S)|≤M, with all-Never payoff zero. Put δ=D_*>0 and
γ=δ²/(8M). Assume either Fin4 with arbitrary own signs, or arbitrary finite
cardinality with all owns nonnegative, so that every genuine minimum has

    b_i−s_i≥δ+γ,       s_i=r_i({i}).                 (A1)

Let q be an ORIGINAL represented global minimum with first positive
atomic date a, no prescribed mass strictly before a, and first-row masses
p_i=q_i({a}). Earlier retained tests, if any, are empty and strictly
inactive by (A1). For each i, let Q_i be the response payoff from Quit at
a and C_i the supremum of ALL responses strictly after a, including
Never. These values depend only on the opponents, not on i's own law.
Every pure response is accounted for, and

    b_i=max(Q_i,C_i).                               (A2)

### No nonsure first row can have every cap strictly in Continue

Assume every p_i<1 and C_i>Q_i for EVERY player, including players whose
prescribed first-row mass is zero. Independently condition the original
laws strictly after a; all normalizers 1−p_i are positive. Denote their
actual tail values and complete caps by V_i,B_i. This tail pair has
actual finite approximants and its debt d=Σ_i(B_i−V_i) is at least δ.
No claim that it is minimal has yet been made.

Hold those tails fixed and vary the positive first-row coordinates. At
the original point these coordinates are interior and all full caps
strictly select Continue. Therefore the actual debt agrees locally with
the multiaffine polynomial P obtained by selecting each C_i branch.
All nearby profiles have actual finite approximants retaining the tied
date and every future test. Global minimality makes P constant on the
entire face of positive first-row coordinates, with value δ.

At the all-zero row vertex this polynomial has value

    P(0)=Σ_i(B_i−V_i)=d.

The ACTUAL tail, without inventing an empty pre-tail test, is an actual
carrier point and has debt d. Hence d=δ: the tail is truly minimal.
Applying (A1) to it gives B_i−s_i≥δ+γ for all i.

Choose any i with p_i>0. At the polynomial vertex with only coordinate
i equal to z, its selected Continue branches give exactly

    P(z e_i)=(1−z)δ+z(B_i−s_i).

Constancy instead forces B_i−s_i=δ. Contradiction. Consequently every
nonsure first atom in a genuine positive minimum has

    Q_j≥C_j for SOME player j.                     (A3)

The cap-attaining player may be inactive in the prescribed row. A tied
Quit/Continue cap is sufficient for (A3). Sure hazards are not included
in this argument: their root coordinates are not interior and their
conditional tail normalizers may vanish. There is no inference that the
first row is Nash, that some active player chooses a best response, or
that its tail is minimal when (A3) holds.

This is more specific than merely locating some finite cap maximizer:
it forces one at the FIRST tied row, with no Never lower bound. It is
an internal source restriction, not a claim of additional raw-table UE
coverage or a Lean theorem. The next cap-switch consumer must allow
inactive joining responders and exact ties.

### A legal relocation and its exact complete-cap account

Fix any i with p_i>0. Move an unconditional mass ρ from i's atom a to
one NEW date immediately before a, where no other player has prescribed
mass. Take 0<ρ≤p_i, first with ρ<1. Keep every other law and all old
response dates unchanged; on a natural calendar insert the date and
shift the later dates by one. No shared randomization is introduced.

This variation is concretely actualizable from the ORIGINAL sequence.
Its marked atom masses converge to p_i, so at sufficiently large
indices the chosen amount ρ can be moved from that literal date (use
ρ_k↑ρ if ρ=p_i). The vanishing original pre-mark masses remain in place.
Their payoffs and cap kernels converge to empty-head values. The added
date supplies its exact tester; all old tied/future/Never testers remain.
Thus the actual finite profiles converge in prescribed values and full
caps to the declared variation. Fixed conditional tails after the new
date have normalizer 1−ρ>0 and are actual carrier points too.

For j≠i, average over the other players' old first-row Bernoulli outcomes
S⊆I−{i,j}, and put

    Q_{j|i}=E[r_j(S∪{i,j})],
    C_{j|i}=E[r_j(S∪{i})],
    c_{j|i}=Q_{j|i}−C_{j|i}.

If i occupied its old atom, the event has already absorbed, so the second
conditional value is identical for EVERY future response of j, including
Never. Therefore the modified old tied and future response envelopes are

    Q_j(ρ)=Q_j+ρ[r_j({i})−Q_{j|i}],
    C_j(ρ)=C_j+ρ[r_j({i})−C_{j|i}].               (A4)

The newly inserted pure response of j pays
(1−ρ)s_j+ρ r_j({i,j}). The owner's new test pays s_i; its old cap b_i
is unchanged because its opponents are unchanged and b_i>s_i. All old
empty head tests remain inactive. For sufficiently small ρ, the added
tester of j is also inactive: it is at most s_j+2Mρ, whereas the old
envelopes remain at least b_j−2Mρ≥s_j+δ+γ−2Mρ.
Hence for small ρ the complete cap is exactly the maximum of the two
branches in (A4), even when the branches were originally tied.

The prescribed targets change by

    U_i(ρ)−U_i=ρ(s_i−Q_i),
    U_j(ρ)−U_j=ρ[r_j({i})−p_j Q_{j|i}
                                 −(1−p_j)C_{j|i}].

Until the first strict old-branch switch, the complete debt is EXACTLY

    D(ρ)=δ+ρ α_i,
    α_i=Q_i−s_i+Σ_{j≠i} E_{ji},                   (A5)

where

    E_{ji}=−(1−p_j)c_{j|i}       if Q_j>C_j;
    E_{ji}=p_j c_{j|i}          if C_j>Q_j;
    E_{ji}=max(−(1−p_j)c_{j|i},p_j c_{j|i})
                                           if Q_j=C_j.

This includes arbitrary signs, inactive or sure root players, simultaneous
old outcomes, every later stopping date and Never. The tie arm cannot be
replaced by an arbitrarily chosen cap selector.

### The actual minimum makes EVERY unilateral earlier relocation costly

The new first row is solo, of probability ρ. Its whole conditional tail
has actual debt d_ρ≥δ. The mover's opponents are unchanged, so its full
post-new-date cap is the ORIGINAL b_i. Apply the exact solo Bellman ledger
to this new profile, keeping every outsider Quit/Continue excess:

    D(ρ)=(1−ρ)d_ρ+ρ(b_i−s_i)
                 +Σ_{j≠i}[Q_j^new−C_j^new]⁺
          ≥δ+ργ.                                  (A6)

This holds for EVERY 0<ρ≤p_i with ρ<1, even beyond branch switches and
even when the new first tester of an outsider becomes binding. At ρ=1
the owner's prescribed target is s_i and its cap is b_i, so its own
debt alone is at least δ+γ; (A6) holds there too without forming a tail
on a null event. No modified source is assumed minimal in this argument.
The tail lower bound is precisely the global numerical infimum, and
the strict cap margin is used only at the ORIGINAL actual minimum.

In particular the explicit local account (A5) must satisfy

    α_i≥γ>0 for EVERY positive first-row owner.    (A7)

Thus this route is not just missing a favorable derivative: the true
positive minimum uniformly forbids a unilateral earlier fragmentation
at ALL amplitudes. Any successful first-row surgery must change opponents
simultaneously, retain a genuine collision, or alter the complete tail.
Independent one-owner phase preemptions cannot be an actual descent.
At nontied old cap branches, multi-owner infinitesimal early changes have
the sum of these strictly positive first derivatives; tied branches can
couple their slopes, so no analogous simultaneous claim is made here.

### Even an ordered small multi-owner prefix has a strict cost

The same actual-minimum account closes the loophole of coordinating several
small early private clocks. Move masses x_i≤p_i from the first atom into
ANY fixed finite ordered calendar strictly before it. Each moved part may
have its own independent finite head law; retain every new head tester and
every old tied/future/Never tester. Put H=Σ_i x_i>0. These are private mass
relocations, not correlated flags or a single shared head time.

The conditional law after the entire new head is

    q_i^+=(q_i−x_i δ_a)/(1−x_i).

For H≤1/2 its total-variation distance from q_i is at most 2x_i. All its
conditional normalizers are positive and its full tail pair is an actual
carrier point, with debt d≥δ. Its caps B_i over the original tied and
future tests obey

    B_i−s_i≥δ+γ−4MH.                              (A8)

This uses uniform opponent coupling and the ORIGINAL margin (A1), not a
claim that the new tail is minimal. Old empty pre-atom tests were inactive
at the original point, so the original cap is supplied by these retained
tests. The finite realizers insert the chosen head calendar immediately
before the unique marked atom date and remove exactly the indicated atom
masses. Vanishing old pre-mark masses have o(1) effect on every cap.
There is no assumption that a newly created head tester is free.

Let K be the set of players selecting their new head components. It is
an independent Bernoulli set. Its probabilities of no selector, exactly
one selector, and at least two selectors are R,W₁,W₂, respectively, with

    R=∏_i(1−x_i),
    W₁=Σ_i x_i∏_{j≠i}(1−x_j)≥H(1−H),
    W₂=1−R−W₁≤H²/2.

Price each cap only at responses AFTER the whole head; this is a LOWER
bound on the complete cap. Conditional on K=∅, the resulting sum-debt
polynomial contributes the true tail debt d. Conditional on K={i}, i
quits alone before every opponent and the contribution is B_i−s_i;
every other target equals its passive head response and cancels exactly.
Conditional on |K|≥2, each nonselector again cancels. A selector's
post-head response payoff and prescribed head payoff lie in [−M,M],
so the total contribution is at least −2Mn. This accounts for EVERY
relative order and every tie among the finite head laws. Consequently

    D_new≥R d+Σ_i x_i∏_{j≠i}(1−x_j)(B_i−s_i)−2Mn W₂
          ≥δ+W₁(γ−4MH)−(δ+2Mn)W₂.                (A9)

All additional head response opportunities can only INCREASE the left
side; (A9) has not dropped a profitable ghost tester from the actual cap.
Using δ≤2M, a single nonoptimized sufficient bound is

    0<H≤γ/[8M(n+1)]
       ⇒ D_new≥δ+γH/8>δ.                          (A10)

Indeed γ−4MH≥γ/2, W₁≥H/2, and
(δ+2Mn)W₂≤M(n+1)H²≤γH/8. The head law and its number/order of dates do
not enter this estimate. Thus even arbitrarily many fixed finite ordered
microdates cannot yield a sufficiently small early-only descent from a
GENUINE positive global minimum. Earlier solved-table examples of a
quadratic improvement after three tiny dates lacked (A1) and do not
falsify this actual-source bound.

This is a mechanism guardrail, not an extra UE class or a constants result.
An attack by early fragmentation alone must cross a finite total-mass
threshold, not merely cancel individual first derivatives at cap ties.
The present next direction is to change the CURRENT collision row or its
actual continuation, instead of adding a vanishing new head before it.

### A finite current-row reduction reaches a tied complete cap

There is a source-preserving finite-rank move even though earlier clocks
are costly. Suppose ALL first-row hazards satisfy p_i<1. Then there is
another actual global minimum, obtained by only DECREASING those hazards
and retaining their exact original conditional tails, for which SOME
player's first-row Quit and full Continue caps tie. The first row still
has at least two positive hazards. Every Never mass is at least its
original value. This does not say the ORIGINAL row already has a tie,
that the tied player is active, or that the resulting row is Nash.

Condition every original law strictly after the atom, with fixed positive
normalizers 1−p_i. The tail pair is in the actual semantic carrier. For
any new root vector p′∈[0,1]^I, prefix this same tail by one ordinary
joint Quit/Continue row. Its exact semantic pair is in the carrier by
`quittingTerminalSemanticPrefix_mem_carrier` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Root/TerminalSemanticPair.lean`.
The initially prefixed pair equals the original minimum pair after
deleting strictly inactive earlier empty tests. No conditional Nash
or minimum premise for the tail is needed.

If some Q_j=C_j already, stop. Otherwise choose a positive proper root
coordinate p_i. Hold all other root coordinates fixed. Each Q_j and C_j
is affine in this one coordinate, and each prescribed U_j is affine too.
The caps are their literal maxima. At the original coordinate all labels
are strict, so D is affine in a neighborhood. Actual global minimality
and p_i∈(0,1) make its slope zero. Thus D stays EXACTLY δ throughout the
connected strict-label interval containing that coordinate.

Decrease the coordinate to that interval's left endpoint. Either an
initial Quit/Continue tie occurs, or the coordinate reaches zero with
all labels still strict. The limiting pair is in the carrier and its
debt is δ by continuity. In the zero arm remove that root coordinate
and repeat. Every completed zero move reduces the number of positive
hazards by one. No sure hazard is created. A zero move leaving a solo
first row is impossible by the exact ledger and strict-margin exclusion;
the all-zero row cannot be reached through this procedure either.
Starting from m≥2 positive hazards, after at most m−1 coordinate segments
the procedure therefore stops at a tie. There is no infinite orbit,
new response opportunity, or selected auxiliary equilibrium in this move.

Since p_i′≤p_i and the conditional tail law is unchanged,

    q_i′(Never)=(1−p_i′)q_i(Never)/(1−p_i)
                  ≥q_i(Never).

Thus a positive-Never source stays positive-Never, and any original Never
lower bounds are retained. Terminal targets, individual debts and caps
may change; only the full GLOBAL minimum value and actual source membership
are preserved. After the tie is reached no claim of further purification
is made: the cusp can stop a coordinate move. At every intermediate
minimum the strict singleton margin guarantees that any old empty pre-row
tester would still be inactive, so omitting those dates from the root-prefix
calendar has not secretly changed the source's complete cap.

Together with the first-collision production, this reduces the consuming
question to a first row with a SURE hazard, or to a nonsure first collision
row with a genuine Quit/Continue cap tie. The tie may belong to an inactive
joining responder. The reduction is internal supporting mathematics, not
a newly solved reward class, and it gives no original-unmodified-stage
claim beyond the separate stage theorem. The next task is a real descent
or equilibrium consumer at that tied row, not another early-clock insertion.

### Sure hazards reduce to an exact finite punishment-floor objective

The sure branch has a direct reduction, but not an automatic Nash consumer.
If TWO or more players quit surely in the first row, every unilateral
deviator still faces some other sure quitter at that row. Hence ALL later
prescribed clocks are ghost for every target and every complete cap. Move
all their masses to Never and delete earlier empty dates: the same minimum
pair is realized by the LITERAL one-date/Never profile. This is an ACTUAL
finite-law global minimum, not merely a compact-limit pair. Repeating that
row stationarily is neither necessary nor being inferred safe. This
reduction alone gives no Nash condition for any sure owner or free player.

With just ONE sure owner z, free-player tails remain ghost for their
targets and their own complete caps, but not for z's quiet deviation.
Use the TRUE punishment value P_z, starting immediately at the next date
if everyone Continues; do not replace it by the nominal phase value or
by max(s_z,0). For independent free first-row rates p on I−{z}, define
the finite root quantities

    V_z(p)=E[r_z({z}∪S)],
    A_z(p)=Σ_{S≠∅}P_p(S) r_z(S),
    h(p)=P_p(S=∅),
    f_z(p)=A_z(p)+h(p)P_z−V_z(p).

For each free j let v_j(p) be its finite induced-game prescribed payoff
at this sure-owner row, and let a_j^Q(p),a_j^C(p) be its two root endpoint
payoffs. ALL future responses and Never coincide with the Continue
endpoint because z absorbs at that date. Put

    F_z(p)=[f_z(p)]⁺
              +Σ_{j≠z}[max(a_j^Q(p),a_j^C(p))−v_j(p)].   (A11)

For EVERY p, attach an ε-optimal independent opponent punishment profile
for z after the row, with owner z prescribed Sure Quit. The free players'
full debts are EXACTLY their displayed finite-game regrets. The owner's
full cap is max(V_z,A_z+h C_ε), where P_z≤C_ε≤P_z+ε.
Thus the resulting actual profiles have total debt in
[F_z(p), F_z(p)+ε]. Finite-censor the punishment laws if necessary, with
uniform full-cap error below any specified extra tolerance. Consequently

    F_z(p)≥δ for EVERY p;                          (A12)

its corresponding payoff/cap pair with exact P_z is in the actual carrier.
No punishment attainment is assumed. The finite root cube is compact,
so F_z has an attained numerical minimum, although its exact tail need
not have a single discrete-clock realization.

If an actual minimum has this sure owner, Section73's atomic-cut
canonicalization gives its owner cap max(V_z,A_z+hP_z). Any old empty
head test is inactive by the strict cap margin. Every other complete cap
already depends only on the sure-owner root. Therefore the ORIGINAL
root rates satisfy

    F_z(p)=δ=min F_z.                              (A13)

This is a finite-dimensional same-source reduction of the sure-owner
branch, with true punishment in the scalar coefficient. It is not a
claim that the root rates are finite induced Nash. If there is another
sure free player, h=0 and no tail coefficient matters, recovering the
literal finite-profile reduction above.

The exact existing consumer
`exists_uniformPayoff_or_singletonBase_pos_gap` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/Toggles/PersistentBaseConcreteGap.lean`
uses an induced PRODUCT Nash point and the owner-floor screen. With
free=I−{z} there are no outsiders, its floor is exactly f_z(p), via
`quittingSingletonBaseOwnerFloorExcess`. A Nash point with f_z≤0 is
already a UE producer. Under δ>0, the alternative forces a uniform
positive floor on EVERY induced Nash point. Minimizing F_z does not
establish that its free-game regret terms vanish, and substituting an
unrelated Nash point can increase the owner term. Thus this inspected
source does NOT directly consume (A13); it identifies the real remaining
tradeoff between free regrets and the TRUE owner-floor excess.

If a first-row tied cap belongs to the sure owner, its owner debt is
zero and δ sits in free-player regrets.
If it belongs to a free player, that player's own regret is zero at
the tie, even when it is inactive. Neither observation makes the other
regrets vanish or preserves the owner floor under their repair. Active
vs inactive ties therefore must remain distinct in a proposed descent.
The next sure-hazard consumer must exploit the actual minimization
(A13), not assume an induced-Nash root or appeal to stationary Never.

There is a finite-rank reduction inside (A13) as well. Each displayed
endpoint and target, and f_z, is affine in any one free coordinate.
If the owner's floor is not tied at zero and no free endpoint cap is
tied, F_z is locally affine in every proper free coordinate. At its
GLOBAL minimum the slope is zero. Decrease a proper coordinate until
an owner-floor tie, a free-cap tie, or its zero boundary is reached;
the value stays exactly δ. The exact-floor pair at every endpoint is
in the actual carrier by the punishment approximants, so the strict
margin remains available. Every zero move lowers the proper-coordinate
count. The process stops after finitely many segments, either at a
tie or at a pure free completion. The pure completion cannot have
all free players Continue: its sure owner's target would be s_z,
contradicting the strict prescribed-payoff margin at the returned actual
minimum. It therefore has another sure quitter, and its minimum pair
has the literal finite-profile realization described above.

If that finite profile still has proper free rates, apply the same
coordinate-flatness argument to its ordinary two-endpoint caps while
keeping two sure quitters fixed. It stops at a tied root cap or a PURE
first-row coalition S of cardinality at least two. In the latter arm
ALL laws may be literally Sure-at-date0 or Never, and the complete debt
is the exact finite expression

    δ=Σ_{i∈S}[r_i(S−{i})−r_i(S)]⁺
         +Σ_{j∉S}[r_j(S∪{j})−r_j(S)]⁺.           (A14)

For every player, the same true minimum has target r_i(S), so
r_i(S)−s_i≥γ. This gives a concrete raw table test for that pure residual,
not an equilibrium assertion: one or more terms of (A14) remain positive.
If every coalition of size at least two fails this ALL-player payoff
test, the pure arm is impossible and the sure-source reductions must
stop at a tied owner floor or tied free cap. No arbitrary finite Nash
point is substituted anywhere in this finite-rank reduction.
